# Changelog

secureconfig is published continuously and versions as `1.0.<pull request number>`, where the number is
that of the most recently merged pull request. The current value is in the `VERSION` file at the
repository root. There is no release artifact, so the version names a state of `main` rather than a
downloadable build, and each guide remains dated by its own "Sources (checked <month year>)" section.
Entries here are grouped by the date the change landed on `main`. Note that nothing in the gate suite
can check `VERSION` against GitHub: the suite is deliberately offline so that no outside service can
turn the build red, and a pull request number is only knowable from outside. Keeping `VERSION` in step
with the merged pull request is therefore an authoring obligation, not an enforced one.

## 2026-09-24

- Default-bind sources, batch 7 (#318). Three more default-listen claims now cite pinned source and name the release: n8n 2.40.6 `N8N_PORT` 5678 (`n8n.md`, beside the already-pinned `N8N_LISTEN_ADDRESS`), the Meilisearch v1.53.2 binary's `localhost:7700` default (`search-engines.md`, beside the already-pinned Docker image), and Directus v12.4.1 `HOST=0.0.0.0` on port 8055, which applies unless `UNIX_SOCKET_PATH` is non-empty (`headless-cms-instant-api.md`). No live bind was run.
- TGI's official image port (#316). `model-servers.md` said a bare TGI container answers on every interface after giving the launcher default `0.0.0.0:3000`; the image built from its main `Dockerfile` sets `PORT=80`, so the container listens on port 80, which the guide's `ss` listener list now includes. It does answer on every interface: Docker sets `HOSTNAME` to the container's hostname, which the router cannot parse as an IP address, so it falls back to `0.0.0.0`. Both are now stated and cited at v3.3.7, and the exposure index's 80, 443 row names the image and cites the guide.
- Default-bind sources, batch 4 (#314). Two more default-listen claims now cite pinned source and name the release. `streamlit.md`: at 1.64.0 an unset `server.address` falls back to `0.0.0.0` and is tried as `::` first when Python supports IPv6, and `server.port` starts at 8501 and moves up to 100 ports on when busy unless set explicitly; the recommended `config.toml` now sets `port = 8501` so a busy port stops Streamlit instead of moving it past the proxy and the checks. `ruby.md`: at v8.0.2 Puma binds `::` only when the host has a non-loopback IPv6 address, otherwise `0.0.0.0`, on port 9292; its configuration Sources line moves from an untagged commit to the tag, with a second line for the address constants. No live bind was run.
- Default-bind sources, batch 5 (#315). Three wildcard-bind defaults now cite pinned source and name the release: PostgREST v16.4 (`server-host` `!4` on port 3000, with the admin server's host falling back to it; `headless-cms-instant-api.md`), Text Generation Inference v3.3.7 (`--hostname` `0.0.0.0`, `--port` 3000; `model-servers.md`), and Typesense v30.2 (`api-address` `0.0.0.0`, `api-port` 8108, `peering-port` 8107; `search-engines.md`). No live bind was run.

The 9004, 9005, 9010 row's credential cell names ClickHouse, so a reader who finds Onyx's MinIO there does not read ClickHouse's empty-password default as MinIO's. `fronting-auth.md` says Authelia passes the host-and-port `:9091`, with its empty host, to `net.Listen`. DONE 1.129 says which of its two Sources lines is pinned.
- Exposure-index gate: backticked pairs and per-guide citations (#311), closing row 1.128.
  - `tools/check_exposure_index.py` now reads a code span (found by a one-line scan with CommonMark's backtick and escape rules: an escaped backtick is literal, a run closes on the next run of the same length, one padding space is stripped; links, raw HTML, autolinks, line breaks and table cells are not modelled) that holds only a published `[host:]H:C` pair, such as `8888:8080` or `127.0.0.1:8888:8080`, in prose or a table cell, and counts both sides. The bind host must be an IPv4 or bracketed IPv6 address, as in the published-mapping shape. A pair whose two sides both have two digits counts only when they are equal, so `80:80` is a pair and `10:30` or `70:30` is not.
  - A row now maps its ports only for the guides it cites. A mention of a port that the index lists singly or in a range of at most 101 ports fails as uncited, unless allowlisted, when no row listing that port cites the guide. A range wider than 101 ports still reports such a mention as unmapped.
  - Run over `main`, the new gate found 20 uncited pairs: 15 the earlier detector already saw, and 5 from backticked pairs. Three are now cited: `agent-builders.md` on row 5432 (Langflow's example Compose publishes `5432:5432`), `llm-observability.md` on row 9000 (the guide lists Helicone's host port `9000` among its Compose publications), and `tunnels.md` on row 3000 (the local backend in its `ssh -R` examples, as the row already cites `cloudflare.md` and `tailscale.md` for their tunnelled `localhost:3000` backends). The other 17 are allowlisted with a reason: outbound destinations, illustrative numbers, test-rig listeners, the README's certificate pointer, and container ports behind remapped publications.
  - Rows 6379 and 9001 also cite `llm-observability.md`, whose Helicone Sources line lists those host ports as bare numbers, which the gate does not read.
  - Row 3000 no longer states a guide count. Its "23 guides" figure was stale, and no gate keeps a count in step.
  - `tools/test_exposure_index.py` gains 19 cases (D42 to D47, N14 to N19, C1 to C7), and M2, M3 and M7 now mention ports from a guide their row cites.
- Rule 7 trap and IFS conditions (#310), row 1.133, carrying the maintainer's rulings of 2026-09-24 at 23:39Z and 23:56Z. CONTRIBUTING rule 7's guarded-read exception now has five conditions: the readonly-`IFS` refusal (already in every block since #301, #303 and #306) and `trap - DEBUG RETURN ERR` before `set +x +a`, ahead of the read, are added. It also states the remaining assumption, a calling shell not altered to intercept the block, with examples (an inherited DEBUG trap still runs once before the `trap -` line and under `extdebug` can skip it; functions, aliases on any word including reserved words, `hash -p` entries and disabled builtins), and says that no command typed into such a shell reliably escapes it, so such blocks belong in a freshly started shell whose startup files the reader trusts. `search-engines.md`'s assumption list is updated to match. Row 1.134 asks whether blocks that hold a secret only in positional parameters, with no read, should clear traps too. All 16 guarded-read blocks now clear the traps, with a one-line comment naming the clean-shell assumption and pointing to rule 7. With stub tools, before this change an inherited DEBUG trap under `set -T` read the typed secret in all 16 blocks, and an ERR trap under `set -E` in 13; after it, in none.
- `surrealdb.md` and `kubernetes.md` guard follow-ups (#309), rows 1.131 and 1.132.
  - `surrealdb.md`: the initial root-password block clears `SURREAL_USER` and `SURREAL_PASS` itself and refuses to start while any other `SURREAL_*` variable is set in the calling shell, naming each, so an inherited `SURREAL_UNAUTHENTICATED`, `SURREAL_BIND` or similar option cannot change the start; with stubs, the old block started with an exported `SURREAL_UNAUTHENTICATED` or `SURREAL_LOG` in the shell, and the new one refuses, naming the variable.
  - `kubernetes.md`: the htpasswd block asks for the password twice and refuses a mismatch or an empty entry, and creates the secret only if `htpasswd -vi` succeeds; its paste warning now describes the two prompts; with stubs, the old block wrote an empty password and ran `kubectl` after a failed verify, and the new one refuses both.
  - Row 1.133 records inherited DEBUG, ERR and RETURN traps (under `set -T` or `set -E`) as a corpus-wide gap in the guarded-read blocks.

- Environment-borne secrets under rule 7's prefix exception (#306), plan A4, closing row 1.126. The last sites in row 1.126's inventory that exported a secret, or read one without the guarded-read conditions, now follow rule 7.
  - `nats.md` (four blocks): natscli v0.4.0 has no stdin input for the password, so each `nats` command that needs it gets a one-command `NATS_PASSWORD="$pw"` prefix instead of an export. `NATS_USER` stays exported. The blocks turn off allexport as well as tracing, clear every variable they use (`pw`, `srv`, `opts`, `marker`, `v`, `f` and the `NATS_*` names) with `unset -n` then `unset -v` before assigning any, refuse a readonly `IFS` before their quoted ambient-credential loop, and clear each ambient `NATS_*` name the same way. With stub `nats` commands, the old blocks, under inherited shell state, sent an altered password (readonly `IFS=f`), exported `pw` to every `nats` process (`set -a`), sent no password while exporting it under another name (a `NATS_PASSWORD` nameref), and let an ambient `NATS_TOKEN` reach every call, including the no-credentials control (readonly `v`, or an inherited `IFS=_` splitting the unquoted loop). Review found further cases, now covered: a nameref `srv`, `opts`, `marker` or `NATS_USER` pointing at `pw` put the password in argv or replaced it, and a `RANDOM` nameref put it in the V3 publisher's marker, printed and published; that block no longer reads `$RANDOM`, since `date +%s%N` already makes the marker unique. The new blocks refuse or behave as the baseline in each case. The V2 `password mismatch` check is removed: with no exported variable it had nothing to compare, and the guard now covers the cases it caught. The probe order, the wrong-password control and all other messages are unchanged.
  - `nomad-consul.md`: the inline `read -rs NOMAD_TOKEN && export NOMAD_TOKEN` example becomes a guarded block that reads the token from the terminal and runs `NOMAD_TOKEN="$tok" nomad acl policy info anonymous`. The recorded `404` is now labelled as observed with the earlier export form.
  - `surrealdb.md`: the initial root-password block no longer exports `SURREAL_PASS`. It reads into `pw` and runs `SURREAL_USER=root SURREAL_PASS="$pw" surreal start ...`, guarded, and the guide says the password stays readable through `/proc/<pid>/environ` for the server's whole lifetime.
  - `kubernetes.md`: the htpasswd block runs in a subshell with `set +x +a`, both guards and `IFS= read -r -s` from `/dev/tty`; the password still reaches `htpasswd` on stdin. Its paste warning now says what pty tests showed: the block's own lines are read before the prompt; a line pasted after the closing `)` becomes the password without bracketed paste, and runs as a command after the block with it.
  - Sources: new pinned lines for nomad v2.0.7 token input and surreal v3.2.4 `--pass`. Rows 1.131 and 1.132 record two out-of-scope findings.

- Readonly-IFS guard for guarded reads (#303), plan A1b of row 1.126. A readonly `IFS` inherited from the calling shell survives the `IFS= read` prefix: bash reports the error and reads anyway, so the secret can be altered as it is read (under `readonly IFS=f`, a trailing `f` is dropped). #301 guarded `sqlite.md` against this; the same guard now precedes the read in the seven other guarded-read blocks: `code-server.md` (1), `gitops-controllers.md` (2), `haproxy.md` (2), `surrealdb.md` (1) and `search-engines.md`'s model block (1).
  - Each block runs `{ unset -n IFS; } 2>/dev/null` and exits 2 if it fails, as it does on a readonly `IFS`; it also removes an `IFS` nameref. The read's own `IFS=` assignment still supplies the empty `IFS`.
  - Nothing else in the blocks changed. Row 1.126 still tracks the rest: `surrealdb.md`'s initial root-password read, `nats.md`'s four prompts, `kubernetes.md`'s prompt and `nomad-consul.md`'s example.
  - Two backlog rows record what #300's review found out of scope: row 1.127 (Onyx development backing-service ports the index does not cite) and row 1.128 (the exposure-index detector does not see a backticked `host:container` pair in prose or a table cell, and row 3000's "23 guides" count is stale). Row 1.129 records #302's: `fronting-auth.md` quotes Authelia's default without the trailing `/` its Sources line shows, and cites nothing for how an empty host binds.
- frps proxy-listener citation (#304), row 1.125. `tunnels.md` said a registered proxy binds on `proxyBindAddr` but cited only how that value defaults. Three Sources lines now cite, at pinned tag v0.71.0, the TCP and UDP proxy listeners and the HTTP and HTTPS vhost listeners that bind it. The claim now carries "as of v0.71.0", the frps part of row 1.124.
- `CONTRIBUTING.md` (#299), carrying four further maintainer rulings of 2026-09-24.
  - Rule 7 now allows a second, narrow exception, for a tool that takes a secret through its environment and offers no stdin input for it, such as `nomad` (`NOMAD_TOKEN`, checked at v2.0.7) or `surreal start` (`SURREAL_PASS`, checked at v3.2.4). The tool may have other inputs outside argv, such as a file; the rule does not prefer them. Such a tool may receive the secret as a one-command prefix assignment, never through `export`; stdin stays required wherever the tool offers it. A further ruling makes a guard mandatory: before the prefix assignment, the block clears the name with `unset -n` then `unset -v` and refuses with exit 2 if either fails, because bash still runs a prefixed command whose name is readonly, and the tool then receives the reader's own exported value. The rule states that the value can remain readable through `/proc/<pid>/environ` by the same user and by root while the tool, or any process that inherited it, runs; unsetting the variable alone does not remove it there, though overwriting that memory or replacing the environment, as an `exec` does, can. It therefore counts as exposed for as long as the tool or any process that inherited it runs, which for a server such as `surreal start` is its whole lifetime.
  - Rule 5 now says "Adding a Verify run", the ruling's wording, where #296 had "Adding a demonstration run".
- Default-bind version notes, #293 set (#302), row 1.124. CONTRIBUTING rule 1 asks for a version note on a default that shifts, so the fourteen default-bind claims #293 checked now carry one in the body text, scoped to the part of the sentence the cited source shows.
  - Thirteen name the release of the guide's own pinned citation: oauth2-proxy v7.15.4 and Authelia v4.39.28 (`fronting-auth.md`), Grafana v13.2.2 and Prometheus v3.14.0 (`admin-uis.md`), Phoenix 20.16.0 (`llm-observability.md`), Python 3.14.4 (`python.md`), the Caddy admin API at v2.11.4 (`caddy.md`), Gitea v1.27.3 (`devops-uis.md`), Next.js v16.3.6 (`nextjs.md`), n8n 2.40.6 (`n8n.md`), SvelteKit adapter-node 5.5.7 and Vite 8.3.1 (`frontend-frameworks.md`), and LiteLLM v1.102.1 (`litellm.md`).
  - Docker's publishing default (`docker.md`) is cited from the vendor documentation rather than a tag, so it says "at the time of writing".
  - Only wording changed; no default, command or citation did. Row 1.124 stays open for the #294 set.
- Guarded secret reads, batch 2 (#301), plan A2: `sqlite.md`'s two secret inputs now meet rule 7's guarded-read exception.
  - Bundle scan: the block turns off allexport as well as tracing (`set +x +a`) and clears `tok` with `unset -n` then `unset -v` before its other guards, refusing with exit 2 if either fails, as it does for a readonly name. It also removes any `IFS` nameref with `unset -n` and refuses with exit 2 on a readonly `IFS`, which could otherwise strip or alter the token as it is read; the read's own `IFS=` assignment then gives it an empty `IFS`. The Turso check does the same. Its token-literal and jwt-shape lines and its not-scanning refusals are otherwise unchanged.
  - Turso check, row 1.123: the block no longer reads the token from `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN`. It prompts for it with input hidden, into one subshell-local variable under the same guards, before either request is sent, and refuses an empty, `REPLACE_WITH_` or control-character token, so the token cannot inject a second header. The anonymous and authorized requests are unchanged, and the check stays REASONED (row 2.37).
  - Row 1.126 stays open for the remaining sites.
- Exposure-index citations (#300), a one-off pass by the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-24. The gate's own detector found 62 guide/port pairs where a guide mentions a port that `exposure-index.md` maps but that port's row does not cite the guide. Each mention was read. The 46 that document a listener, a published port or an exposure on that port now cite their guide. One more pair came from outside the detector: `llm-observability.md` names Langfuse's bundled MinIO S3 API on host port `9090`, but a hard line wrap at `llm-observability.md:34-35` separates the number from its "port" word, a documented blind spot of the gate's `mentions()`; it was read and is cited on the same terms. That makes 47 guide/port pairs, carried by 41 new citations (six guides document both 80 and 443, which share one row), across 16 rows: 22, 53, 80 and 443, 3000, 3001, 5000, 5432, 7860, 8000, 8080, 8081, 8088, 8443, 8888, 9001 and 9090. By a further maintainer ruling of 2026-09-24, where a newly cited guide's product was missing from its row's "May be" cell, that product was appended to the cell and nothing else in it changed, on 13 rows: 80 and 443, 3000, 3001, 5000, 7860, 8000, 8080, 8081, 8088, 8443, 8888, 9001 and 9090. Rows 22, 53 and 5432 already named theirs (SSH, cluster DNS, PostgreSQL). The other 16 detector pairs stay uncited: outbound destinations, verification-rig and illustrative numbers, a tunnel's local target, a test-input list, the README's pointer, and one allowlisted grep caveat.
- Download-probe guard (#297), row 1.122. `sqlite.md`'s download probe named a literal `app.example.com`, so an unsubstituted paste probed that host. It now takes the HTTPS origin and a static path prefix on a `set -- PASTE_WHOLE_BLOCK` line inside a subshell, as the guide's listener and Turso blocks do, and drives its probes from positional parameters rather than named loop variables. It refuses to probe on a missing marker, a wrong value count, an empty or `REPLACE_WITH_` value, a non-HTTPS origin, an origin other than `https://` with a host and an optional numeric `:port` (one trailing `/` is dropped), or a prefix without a leading `/` or containing `//`, `?`, `#`, whitespace or a control character. The probe stays REASONED (row 2.37).
- Guarded secret reads, batch 1 (#298): six `read -s` prompts in four guides now meet rule 7's guarded-read exception.
  - Each block turns off tracing and allexport (`set +x +a`) before the read and clears the variable with `unset -n` then `unset -v`. If either fails, as it does for a readonly name, the block refuses with exit 2. The four reads that lacked `|| exit 2` now have it.
  - `code-server.md`: the session-cookie probe now rejects any control character in the cookie, not only a carriage return.
  - `gitops-controllers.md`: the Argo CD token probe now refuses an empty or control-character token. The Flux `generic-hmac` probe now runs in its own subshell and refuses an empty key; the subshell's exit discards the key and the signature, so the trailing `unset` is gone. The key still reaches `openssl` on its command line, as the block's comment says.
  - `haproxy.md`: the admin-password and stats-password probes now refuse an empty or control-character password. The admin probe escapes the password in place instead of copying it into a second variable, because the exception allows only one.
  - `surrealdb.md`: the system-user JWT probe, whose existing pattern check already refuses an empty or control-character token.
  - The six steps stay REASONED or UNVERIFIED, so no verification status changed. Row 1.126 stays open for the remaining sites.
- `CONTRIBUTING.md` (#296), carrying the maintainer's rulings of 2026-09-24.
  - Rule 5 now requires two things. A review brief for a demonstrated step states the exposed run, the fixed run and what each observed. Adding a demonstration run means rereading the guide's run-provenance sentence. Both lines were proposed by guardrails.
  - Rule 7 now allows one exception to its no-named-variable rule. `read` cannot target the positional parameters, so a key read with `read -r -s` may sit in a single subshell-local variable, if tracing and allexport are off (`set +x +a`), the variable is cleared first (`unset -n` then `unset -v`, failing closed), and it is never exported. A secret the reader already holds in an environment variable is not covered.
  - Row 1.126 tracks checking the 15 existing `read -s` invocations in nine guides against those guards.

  - Four of them had no source for the bind, and each is now cited: Authelia, Prometheus, Phoenix (including its separate gRPC server on `[::]:4317`) and Docker's publishing default.

- `sqlite.md` (#292): the bundle scan, the git inventory and the `stat` check were demonstrated in exposed and fixed states without opening a socket, and the guide records what was observed. It includes that `git check-ignore` stays silent on an already-tracked `app.db` while `ls-files` and `log` expose it. The download probe, which had been unmarked, is now marked REASONED at its step alongside the LiteFS/Litestream and Turso checks, each naming its missing capability. The download probe and the listener checks need a served application or a running LiteFS or Litestream, and those open listeners, which the authoring host forbids without an isolated network namespace. The outbound Turso check needs a Turso database and token. The `stat` step also ran with a live rollback journal. Rows 1.122 and 1.123 record two pre-existing issues found in review: the download probe has no placeholder guard, and the Turso token guard allows CR or LF. Row 2.37 is narrowed to the REASONED checks.
- Follow-ups (#291) from #289's and #290's final reviews: `streamlit.md`'s realm sentence is punctuated so it parses on one reading, and `ray.md`'s Verify comment marks its whole `10001` expectation (the node's address plus a loopback listener) as read from source, citing the pinned Ray Client server, proxier and launcher lines in Sources.
- `ray.md` (#290): the network-isolation step now says not to rely on Ray's bind addresses. In Ray 2.58.0's source the core gRPC servers listen on every interface unless the node address is exactly `127.0.0.1`, `::1` or `localhost`, and Serve replicas open a gRPC server on `[::]`. In four loopback runs with `--node-ip-address=127.0.0.1` the GCS still listened on `*:6379` (cause not established), and a watcher stopped each run because the authoring host forbids wildcard binds. The Verify `ss` comment no longer expects `6379` on the private interface only. Row 2.40 records that a live demonstration needs an isolated network namespace.
- Follow-ups (#289), closing backlog row 1.120: `search-engines.md`'s limit sentence says the `command_not_found_handle` that brought back the false pass printed a newline and the expected status. `streamlit.md` says the realm's fourth user had no `hd` user attribute, which is what was configured, rather than that no claim was emitted. DONE row 2.36 says the `hd` and email checks were removed together.
- `streamlit.md` (#288), closing backlog row 2.36: the Verify checks were demonstrated on loopback against Streamlit 1.64.0 with native `st.login` and behind Caddy v2.11.4 and oauth2-proxy v7.15.4, using Keycloak 26.7.4 as the OIDC provider, in the exposed and fixed states, and the guide records what was observed. The run found two things. First, section 2's install step needs Streamlit's `auth` extra: with Authlib alone, every browser login attempt got `Internal Server Error` for want of `httpx`. Second, a separately unprotected websocket location gave an anonymous client the app's output while the page returned `401`. It also shows that with static serving off, `/app/static/` returns the app's HTML with `200`, so a static probe is judged by its body. The external vantage and the wildcard bind remain REASONED (row 1.121). Row 1.120 records a non-blocking precision note from #287.

- Added a Default credential column to `exposure-index.md` (#277), closing backlog row 1.107. Each of the 150 rows now says what its cited guides state a fresh install ships with: a well-known account, an empty or placeholder password, no authentication, a secure default the guide states, "not stated" where the guides are silent, or "varies by service; see the guides" where one row covers services with different defaults. The column records the guides, not the vendors. `tools/check_exposure_index.py` now also holds the table to a strict grammar: the exact header and separator, then every line up to the blank line that ends the table in `| a | b | c | d |` form, every non-Port cell built only from a whitelist (printable ASCII text, single-backtick code, links with text and a simple target, and escaped pipes) and carrying a letter or digit, and a Port cell that is a list of ports or ranges from 1 to 65535 with no leading zeros. The page must open with a `# ` heading, and the header must appear once, with a blank line above it and no `<`, `$` or code fence anywhere above it, so the gate reads the table GitHub renders, and every link's text must show a letter or digit. A row that breaks the grammar fails the gate and maps nothing, and the old three-column header fails closed; the self-test grows to 123 cases.

## 2026-09-23

- Hardened the reasoned-row gate's parsing (#272). `tools/check_reasoned_rows.py` now splits guide and backlog text into lines on `\n` only, so a U+2028 or U+2029 inside a paragraph no longer fakes a heading, and its backlog filename match now also treats `+`, `~` and a non-ASCII letter, digit or other numeric character beside a basename as part of a longer filename, so `archive+redis.md`, `redis.md~` or a basename touching a non-ASCII letter no longer counts as tracking `redis.md`, while quotes of any kind, dashes, brackets and `*` around it still do. Underscore emphasis is still not credited, because without code-span parsing it cannot be told apart from underscores inside a filename. The shared `tools/_markdown.py` closes a fence only when spaces or tabs follow the closing run, so a non-breaking space no longer closes it (CommonMark). Regression cases 18 to 21 were added. The corpus result is unchanged (53 reasoned guides, 20 grandfathered, 0 new), and every gate that uses `tools/_markdown.py` gives identical output before and after. A fence opened inside a list item is still not closed at the item boundary, which remains a disclosed limit.
- Added the reasoned-row gate (#271): `tools/check_reasoned_rows.py` flags a guide whose Verify section marks a step "reasoned" but has no demonstration backlog row (a row naming the guide) in `TODO.md` or `DONE.md`, the tracking CONTRIBUTING rule 5 requires. Wired advisory into `tools/run_all_checks.sh` so it never fails the suite; the 20 pre-existing reasoned guides without a row are grandfathered in `tools/reasoned_row_baseline.txt`, and a `--strict` promotion to a blocking gate is left for Architect sign-off. Closes the recurrence path behind the #270 D1 finding.

## 2026-09-21

- Added `mcp-clients.md` (#270), a new client-side companion to `mcp-servers.md`, closing TODO 1.83 and covering the MCP 2026-07-28 client authorization obligations the server-operator guide defers, each verified at its primary source: authorization-response `iss` validation and mix-up defence (RFC 9207 section 2.4 requires simple string comparison and reject-on-mismatch, applied before the authorization code is redeemed per MCP Authorization, with the advertise/present handling table so the guide does not overclaim that every response lacking `iss` is rejected, plus RFC 9700 section 4.4 mix-up context); discovery-metadata issuer identity (RFC 8414 section 3.3, the metadata `issuer` must equal the issuer used to construct the well-known URL or the document must not be used, distinguished from RFC 9728 section 3.3, which validates `resource`); Client ID Metadata Documents (draft-ietf-oauth-client-id-metadata-document-00) preferred over deprecated Dynamic Client Registration, with MCP's requirement to specify an `application_type`; refresh-token confidentiality (OAuth 2.1 section 4.3) and `offline_access` request handling (OIDC Core section 11 prompt=consent condition); and OAuth-discovery SSRF (RFC 9728 section 7.7 SHOULD, raised to a MUST for server-deployed clients by MCP Security Best Practices). The Verify section runs the SSRF address-classification and cleartext-token checks and marks the client-behaviour checks reasoned, naming the absent client-and-authorization-server harness, per the CONTRIBUTING Verify rule. Registered the guide in `scripts/build-llms-full.sh` (which drives both `site/llms-full.txt` and the plugin bundle), `site/llms.txt`, `README.md`, `site/index.html`, and both `.aiqt/gensrc.json` source lists, and cross-linked it from `mcp-servers.md` and `oidc-integration.md`. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. VERSION 1.0.272.
- Added a subscription-stream proxying and response-caching section to `mcp-servers.md` (#269), completing TODO 1.84, source-verified against the MCP 2026-07-28 specification and the nginx proxy module: a `subscriptions/listen` POST opens a long-lived `text/event-stream` that a default nginx reverse proxy breaks by buffering the response and by the idle read timeout (Caddy flushes an SSE response immediately by default), so the new section 6 configures `proxy_buffering off` (or the server's `X-Accel-Buffering: no`, which nginx honours per response) and a `proxy_read_timeout` above the longest idle gap, and preserves the event-stream framing; and it warns that a shared response cache crosses authorization contexts, quoting the MCP caching rule that, for a private-scope response, caches MUST NOT be shared across authorization contexts and MUST NOT rely on `cacheScope` alone, with `proxy_cache off` for the POST-only MCP location and the note that nginx caches nothing by default. Updated the section 4 forward reference and added the MCP Subscriptions, nginx proxy-module, and Caddy reverse_proxy source citations. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`.

- Added a NativeAuthenticator password and signup-policy control to `jupyter.md` (#262), the follow-up deferred from #260, source-verified against the pinned `nativeauthenticator` 1.3.0 source: when a Hub uses NativeAuthenticator instead of `PAMAuthenticator` (`c.JupyterHub.authenticator_class = 'native'`) the host no longer supplies a password policy, so set `minimum_password_length` and `seconds_before_next_try` (both declared with a `.tag(default=...)` annotation that is traitlets metadata, not the trait default, so both effectively default to `0` -- no length floor and essentially no cooldown), `check_common_password` (default `False`, matched against the package's bundled 10,000-entry common-password list), and `allowed_failed_logins` (lockout disabled at the default `0`) explicitly, and keep `open_signup` at its default `False`. Documents that NativeAuthenticator's admin-approval step is separate from JupyterHub's `check_allowed` admission and that, under the section 5 `allow_all = False` plus populated `allowed_users` configuration, a not-yet-registered `allowed_users` or `admin_users` username is pre-authorized at signup, so a listed name can be claimed at the signup form by anyone who reaches it; passwords are stored as bcrypt hashes in the Hub database. A new REASONED Verify check (no Hub with NativeAuthenticator in the authoring environment) exercises the password, lockout, approval-hold, pre-authorization-exposure, and `enable_signup` 404 behaviours, with the JUPYTER-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations pin to `nativeauthenticator` 1.3.0 and the NativeAuthenticator quickstart.
- Added the pinned-citation gate (#254): `tools/check_pinned_citations.py` flags mutable GitHub source citations (`raw.githubusercontent.com/<owner>/<repo>/main|master/...` and `github.com/<owner>/<repo>/blob/main|master/...`), matching only the ref segment so a `main` or `master` inside a file path (for example a pinned tag's `.../src/main/java/...`) is not flagged. A citation against a mutable branch cannot be re-verified: the file can change while the URL still resolves, so both this offline gate suite and the advisory lychee sweep stay green as the evidence drifts. The gate lands ADVISORY: it reports the current mutable citations across the root Markdown files (baseline 91) and fails only if the count rises above that baseline, so the count can never rise above today's baseline while the corpus-wide pinning sweep proceeds as a tracked backlog. Includes a self-test and is wired into `tools/run_all_checks.sh`.
- Deepened `rabbitmq.md` (RabbitMQ 4.3) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#261), source-verified against RabbitMQ 4.3.6 and Erlang/OTP 27 (directives checked against the pinned `rabbitmq-server` v4.3.6 launch script and the OTP distribution-over-TLS documentation): encrypt inter-node and CLI distribution traffic with mutually authenticated TLS, configured through `rabbitmq-env.conf` (`SERVER_ADDITIONAL_ERL_ARGS` and `RABBITMQ_CTL_ERL_ARGS` selecting `-proto_dist inet_tls -ssl_dist_optfile`) and a separate Erlang-term `inter_node_tls.config` with `verify_peer` in both directions rather than a `rabbitmq.conf` setting, with the honest notes that distribution certificates are checked against the Erlang node name, that a plaintext node and a TLS node cannot cluster during a rolling switch, and that distribution TLS does not remove the Erlang cookie, epmd, or firewall requirements from the existing distribution-port section; and restrict routing keys on shared topic exchanges with `rabbitmqctl set_topic_permissions` (broker records managed by the CLI, not entries in `rabbitmq.conf` or `advanced.config`, available since RabbitMQ 3.7), noting that topic authorization is default-allow until a topic permission is set, that the write pattern gates publishing while the read pattern gates the routing keys a binding may use, that consumers still read from their queues under ordinary permissions so topic authorization does not filter messages already sitting in a queue, and that clearing a topic permission returns the exchange to the unrestricted default rather than denying it. New deployment Verify checks 8 and 9 are REASONED (no RabbitMQ, Erlang, or multi-node cluster in the authoring environment) with the RABBITMQ-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations pin to RabbitMQ 4.3.6, Erlang/OTP 27, and the official RabbitMQ documentation.

- Deepened `jupyter.md` (Jupyter Server 2.18+ and JupyterHub 5+) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#260), source-verified against pinned Jupyter Server 2.18.0, JupyterHub 5.5.2, and jupyter_client 8.6.3: preserve the deployment path prefix through the reverse proxy (`c.ServerApp.base_url` for a standalone Server, `c.JupyterHub.bind_url` setting the listener and base URL together, with the note that the configurable-http-proxy `--no-include-prefix` and `--no-prepend-path` flags are not an authentication boundary); trust forwarded headers only behind a sanitizing ingress (a standalone Server's `ServerApp.trust_xheaders` defaults to `False` while JupyterHub enables forwarded-header trust, so bind the backend privately and set `trusted_downstream_ips`, which selects the client address but does not stop a peer from supplying headers and whose empty list does not disable trust); and encrypt internal Hub communication (`c.JupyterHub.internal_ssl`, default `False`, generating an internal certificate authority and per-component certificates with client-certificate verification across the Hub, proxy, and single-user connections, with the honest boundary that internal TLS does not encrypt the single-user-server-to-kernel ZeroMQ transport, whose HMAC authenticates but does not encrypt). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no Jupyter runtime, Hub, or multi-user spawn in the authoring environment) with the JUPYTER-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended. The deferred password-policy refinement is surfaced as a scope decision rather than folded in, because the guide targets PAMAuthenticator and the concrete controls belong to the separate NativeAuthenticator. - Deepened `mongodb.md` (self-managed MongoDB Community and Enterprise) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#259), source-verified against MongoDB 8.0 documentation (edition and version boundaries raw-checked against the pinned 8.0 pages): enable MongoDB Enterprise auditing with a deliberate destination and event coverage (`auditLog.destination`/`format`/`path` on every `mongod` and `mongos`, an `auditLog.filter` that narrows the recorded events, and `auditAuthorizationSuccess`, which defaults to `false` so successful authorization checks go unrecorded until it is enabled at a performance cost), noting the facility is Enterprise-only and the Community diagnostic log is not a substitute; redact document values from the diagnostic log (`security.redactClientLogData`, a MongoDB Enterprise feature, set through the `--redactClientLogData` flag or the configuration setting, replacing message-accompanying values with `###` while metadata stays visible and the documentation scopes it to the diagnostic log); encrypt the storage engine with a managed key (MongoDB Enterprise 3.2 native WiredTiger encryption, `security.enableEncryption` defaulting to `false`, a local `encryptionKeyFile` distinct from the cluster-authentication `security.keyFile`, or the recommended `security.kmip` key-server block, with the honest notes that MongoDB cannot encrypt existing data and the local-key procedure does not support rotation); encrypt selected fields with client-side field level encryption (CSFLE, which has no `mongod` switch, with explicit encryption available in Community while automatic encryption requires Enterprise or Atlas and automatic decryption stays in Community, supplying the encryption schema locally so a compromised server cannot drop the requirement, and the randomized-versus-deterministic equality-matching trade-off); and query encrypted fields with Queryable Encryption (equality queries generally available from MongoDB 7.0 and range from 8.0, requiring a new `encryptedFields` collection, unsupported on standalones, and never combined with CSFLE on one collection, with the same automatic-versus-explicit edition split). Every added control is edition-honest: auditing, redaction, and native storage encryption are Enterprise-only, CSFLE and Queryable Encryption split explicit-Community from automatic-Enterprise, and none is presented as a Community capability it is not. New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no MongoDB Enterprise deployment, encryption keys, or KMIP endpoint in the authoring environment) with the MONGODB-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations pin to MongoDB 8.0 documentation.

- Deepened `elasticsearch.md` (self-managed Elasticsearch 8.x and OpenSearch 3.x) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#258), source-verified against Elasticsearch/Kibana 8.19 and OpenSearch/OpenSearch Dashboards 3.8.0: field-level and document-level security (an Elasticsearch Platinum/Enterprise feature, since the free Basic security tier excludes FLS/DLS, versus OpenSearch Security's free Apache-2.0 equivalent, with each product's query and aggregation limitations disclosed and separate indices recommended where stronger isolation is required, and the two products' rules never cross-attributed); storage encryption as an operating-system and dm-crypt control independent of transport TLS (no native switch; Elastic's "Encryption at rest support" is a subscription row, not a feature toggle); snapshot repository encryption distinguishing server-side, customer-managed-key, and client-side encryption per product (noting OpenSearch removed the S3 `server_side_encryption` setting in 3.1 in favour of `server_side_encryption_type`, defaulting to `AES256` from 3.8, and not substituting Elasticsearch and OpenSearch settings for one another); and the separate Kibana and OpenSearch Dashboards browser-facing listener on 5601, which defaults to plaintext and needs its own TLS distinct from its connection to the cluster. New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no live cluster or eligible Elasticsearch subscription in the authoring environment) with the ES-OS-VERIFY-1 demonstration-debt row extended. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations pin to Elasticsearch 8.19 documentation and version-tagged OpenSearch references.

## 2026-09-20

- Deepened `redis.md` (upstream Redis) with HA-set hardening controls (#257), source-verified against Redis Open Source 8.10.1 (config directives raw-checked against the pinned `redis/redis/8.10.1` `redis.conf` and `sentinel.conf`): authenticate replication through role changes (`requirepass` plus `masterauth`, or a dedicated `masteruser`/ACL replication account, noting `masterauth` is outbound only and a replication account still receives the full stream); secure Sentinel's separate 26379 listener and its incoming and peer authentication (protected mode is off by default in sentinel mode; `requirepass`, or ACLs from Redis 6.2); give Sentinel dedicated per-monitored-instance credentials (`sentinel auth-user`/`auth-pass` with a restricted `__sentinel__:hello` channel ACL); restrict the cluster bus (the data port plus 10000, typically 16379; `cluster-port` and `cluster-announce-*` publish endpoints but are not a firewall); enable TLS independently for replication (`tls-replication`) and the cluster bus (`tls-cluster`), noting the guide's deliberate `tls-auth-clients no` also removes incoming replica certificate requirements while the cluster bus always requires a peer certificate; configure TLS on Sentinel itself and its outgoing connections; and restrict trusted HA peer identities with `tls-expected-peer-name` (new in Redis 8.10, so version-gated and not backported to the 6.0+ baseline, not enforced on Sentinel's outgoing path, and warn-and-continue on builds compiled without peer-name verification). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no live Redis, Sentinel, or cluster in the authoring environment) with the REDIS-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended to the seven HA checks. The Valkey compatibility note is kept separate from these upstream-Redis claims. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations pin to `redis/redis` 8.10.1 and the official Redis documentation.
- Deepened `minio.md` (AIStor) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#256), source-verified against the current AIStor documentation: disable the embedded Console when administration uses `mc`/API clients (`MINIO_BROWSER=off`, distinct from the `MINIO_BROWSER_REDIRECT` redirect control); set a workload-sized cluster-wide API concurrency budget (`MINIO_API_REQUESTS_MAX`, divided among nodes, neither a requests-per-second nor a TCP-connection limit); map OIDC identities to scoped policies (`MINIO_IDENTITY_OPENID_ROLE_POLICY` bound to the existing `app-bucket` policy, or the mutually-exclusive `MINIO_IDENTITY_OPENID_CLAIM_NAME` claim mapping (configuring both is a startup error), with the honest note that authentication is not authorization and that an explicit `AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity` `DurationSeconds` up to a year takes precedence over a short `MINIO_STS_DURATION` default and the JWT `exp`); and shorten presigned-URL lifetime (`mc share download --expire`, default 7 days) backed by a server-enforced `s3:signatureAge` deny policy on the non-root application identity. New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no live AIStor, IdP, or load client in the authoring environment) with the MINIO-LIVE demonstration-debt row extended. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use the official AIStor documentation.
- Pinned CI's shellcheck to 0.11.0 (#255) so the local gate and CI lint with the same version. `ubuntu-latest` floats shellcheck, and in #231 the runner's older shellcheck raised an SC2015 on a correct `[ -f ] && [ -r ] || { ...; exit; }` guard that the gate's local 0.11.0 does not, so a green local run reddened only in CI after push. The `Checks` workflow now installs a SHA-256-verified shellcheck 0.11.0 before running the suite, the same determinism reason its Python pin carries; the gate itself still reads `shellcheck` from PATH and stays offline, and a release-asset outage failing the job is the tradeoff already accepted for the Python pin. Updated the `check_shell_blocks.py` docstring to match.
- Deepened `php.md` (PHP and Laravel) with production-hardening controls (#253), source-verified against PHP 8.x and Laravel 12.x: harden php.ini error handling and the banner (`display_errors=Off`, `display_startup_errors=Off`, `log_errors=On` to a protected `error_log`, `error_reporting=E_ALL`, and `expose_php=Off` to drop the `X-Powered-By: PHP` header, starting from the bundled `php.ini-production`); reduce the code-execution, inclusion, and upload-directory surface (`disable_functions` as an application-tested deny list, `open_basedir`, `allow_url_fopen` where unneeded, `allow_url_include` kept Off, FPM `security.limit_extensions`, and never executing PHP from writable upload directories); bound uploads and requests (`upload_max_filesize`, `post_max_size` above it, `max_file_uploads`, `memory_limit`, `max_input_vars`, `max_execution_time`, `max_input_time`, and FPM `request_terminate_timeout`); and harden session storage and lifetime beyond the cookie flags (`session.gc_maxlifetime`, `session.cookie_lifetime`, `session.use_only_cookies`, `session.sid_length`/`sid_bits_per_character`, and `session.save_path` permissions). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no PHP runtime in the authoring environment) with a PHP-VERIFY demonstration row (TODO 2.43); the local `php -i` directive inspection is likewise reasoned, since no PHP executable was available in the authoring environment. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use the official PHP manual and Laravel 12.x documentation.
- Deepened `dotnet.md` (ASP.NET Core / Kestrel) with server-hardening controls (#252), source-verified against .NET 10 LTS: keep the Developer Exception Page out of production (`UseExceptionHandler`, `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT` with Production as the unset default, the `DOTNET_` versus `ASPNETCORE_` precedence, and Development-only `AddDatabaseDeveloperPageExceptionFilter`/`UseMigrationsEndPoint`); bound request and connection budgets (`KestrelServerLimits.MaxRequestBodySize` 30 MB, `[RequestSizeLimit]`, `FormOptions.MultipartBodyLengthLimit` 128 MiB, the IIS `maxAllowedContentLength` distinction, plus `KeepAliveTimeout`, `RequestHeadersTimeout`, `MaxConcurrentConnections`, header count and size limits, minimum data rates, and the `Http2Limits` defaults, correcting stale window and field values); persist Data Protection keys for containers and replicas (`PersistKeysToFileSystem`, a shared `SetApplicationName`, `ProtectKeysWithCertificate`, and repository isolation, with the honest note that ephemeral keys break authentication and antiforgery rather than weakening the algorithm); and reduce auxiliary exposure (`AddServerHeader=false`, IIS `X-Powered-By`/server-header removal, Development-only OpenAPI/Swagger UI or `RequireAuthorization`, authorized health checks with the Host-header-spoofing caveat, and `AllowedHosts` host filtering). Also corrected the Kestrel `SslProtocols` default (None=OS, so the explicit TLS 1.2/1.3 is an intentional restriction), the lockout wording (`lockoutOnFailure: false` is the template's argument, not a parameter default), the forwarded-headers trust wording (loopback is trusted by default, and .NET 10 replaces `KnownNetworks` with `KnownIPNetworks`), the cookie `SameSite` claim (Strict is too categorical for the application cookie; correlation and nonce cookies default to None), the `SSL_CERT_FILE` (a file) versus `SSL_CERT_DIR` (a directory) conflation, and the Verify 401/403 expectation (pinned to a protected API and its scheme). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no .NET runtime in the authoring environment) with a DOTNET-VERIFY demonstration row (TODO 2.42). Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use official Microsoft Learn, .NET runtime and ASP.NET Core source, IIS, and OpenSSL documentation.
- Deepened `java.md` (Spring Boot) with server-hardening controls (#251), source-verified against Spring Boot 3.5.16 and 4.1.1 (Spring Security 6.5/7.1): lock down Actuator (expose only `health` by default, bind the management listener privately with `management.server.port`/`management.server.address` or disable it with `port=-1`, authorize every endpoint with `EndpointRequest.toAnyEndpoint()` on a dedicated filter chain, with the warning that any custom `SecurityFilterChain` makes Boot's default web security back off so an Actuator-only chain leaves the rest of the app unprotected; keep `shutdown` and `heapdump` access `none` and `env`/`configprops` `show-values` `never`); keep production error responses closed (`server.error.*` in Boot 3 and `spring.web.error.*` in Boot 4, include-message/stacktrace/binding-errors `never` and include-exception `false`, noting on-param is not a boundary and disabling the whitelabel page alone is not a fix), keep the H2 console off (`spring.h2.console.enabled=false`) and exclude devtools from production; bound uploads, forms, and headers (`spring.servlet.multipart.max-file-size` 1MB and `max-request-size` 10MB, `server.tomcat.max-http-form-post-size` 2MB, `server.max-http-request-header-size` 8KB, none of which is a universal JSON body limit); and add proxy-forwarded-header trust, stateless-API CSRF, and session-fixation qualifications. Also corrected the "every endpoint requires authentication" overstatement (Actuator health is permitted, and a custom chain backs off Boot defaults), the HSTS claim (the default writer emits only when the request `isSecure()`), and the generated-password Verify check (zero log lines does not prove the default user is gone); added the Boot 4 moved-package citations (`ServerProperties`, `EndpointRequest`, `RestClientSsl`/`WebClientSsl`). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no Spring Boot runtime in the authoring environment) with a JAVA-VERIFY demonstration row (TODO 2.41). Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use official Spring Boot, Spring Security, Spring Framework, and Tomcat documentation.
- Deepened `go.md` (Go `net/http`) with server-hardening controls (#250), current to Go 1.27.1: keep diagnostic endpoints private (importing `net/http/pprof` or `expvar` registers `/debug/pprof/` and `/debug/vars` on `http.DefaultServeMux`, which a nil-handler server exposes, so give the app its own `http.NewServeMux()` and serve any diagnostics on a separate loopback listener); bound request resources (`http.MaxBytesReader` per endpoint with an explicit 413, plus the separate `Server.MaxHeaderBytes` and Go 1.27 `Server.MaxHeaderValueCount` header limits); set the full `http.Server` deadline set (`ReadTimeout`, `WriteTimeout`, and `IdleTimeout` beyond the existing `ReadHeaderTimeout`, on the port-80 redirect server too, with `http.NewResponseController` for per-request budgets); and set security headers in middleware when Go terminates its own TLS (`Strict-Transport-Security` only when `r.TLS != nil`). Also corrected the internal-CA snippet to check both errors and reject a false `AppendCertsFromPEM` (with the Go 1.27 `SSL_CERT_FILE`/`GODEBUG=x509sslcertoverrideplatform` note), softened the `InsecureSkipVerify` "every connection" wording (the `VerifyConnection`/`VerifyPeerCertificate` exception), and qualified privileged-port binding as Linux's configurable default (`net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start`). Extended Verify with local and guarded off-host pprof-reachability checks. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use pkg.go.dev and the Go project documentation, current to Go 1.27.1.
- Deepened `ray.md` with residual Tier-1 controls (#249), pinned to Ray 2.58.0 and KubeRay v1.7.0: treat `runtime_env` as code execution (a submitted job installs its `pip`/`conda`/`working_dir`/`py_modules`/`worker_process_setup_hook` before the entrypoint runs, and Ray ships no admission control or allowlist for it, so submitting a job or reaching the Client port is permission to run arbitrary code, mitigated operationally by prebuilding reviewed dependencies into the image); keep Ray Serve application endpoints private (HTTP proxy on `8000`, defaulting to `127.0.0.1` from the Python `HTTPOptions` but `0.0.0.0` from a config file, `proxy_location` default `EveryNode`, no built-in application authentication so cluster token auth does not gate Serve requests, and an optional gRPC proxy on `9000` only when configured); clarify GCS versus Redis on the head `6379` (it is the GCS metadata server, Redis stopped launching by default in Ray 1.11, so isolate the network and enable token authentication rather than setting a database password, and external Redis is only an opt-in GCS fault-tolerance backend whose `--redis-password`/`--redis-username` and KubeRay `gcsFaultToleranceOptions.redisPassword` authenticate the separate Redis connection and not GCS clients, with `RAY_REDIS_PASSWORD` not a head substitute for `--redis-password`); and run Ray processes with restricted privileges (the official image runs as `ray` UID 1000 but with passwordless sudo, so set an explicit non-root `securityContext` with dropped capabilities and a `RuntimeDefault` seccomp profile on the head and worker containers). Also corrected the token-authentication version and scope (still disabled by default as of Ray 2.58.0, introduced in 2.52.0, authenticating Ray's control plane and not Serve application requests), named `6379` as the GCS server, and qualified that the head and Client ports are unauthenticated only when token authentication is disabled. The new Serve and runtime_env material is guidance; the service-dependent Verify steps (external reachability, the token-authentication controls, and the gRPC-TLS check) are marked REASONED with a new RAY-LIVE-1 demonstration row (TODO 2.40), since the authoring environment has no Ray cluster; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to Ray 2.58.0 and KubeRay v1.7.0.
- Deepened `ollama.md` with Tier-1 controls (#248), pinned to Ollama v0.34.2: make the private listener explicit (`OLLAMA_HOST` loopback default versus a `0.0.0.0` or published-container exposure, and the official image's wildcard listener); put authentication, TLS, and MFA at the fronting layer, since the local API has no native authentication and serves plaintext HTTP, so an authenticating reverse proxy or tunnel is the security boundary (with the honest notes that `OLLAMA_AUTH` is a client-side outbound-signing variable, not an inbound switch, and that an `https://` `OLLAMA_HOST` prefix does not enable server TLS), and keep the shared-static-credential and no-constant-time-comparison limits of the bearer block explicit; reserve the management surface for administrators with a default-deny proxy policy that permits only required inference paths (excluding model pull/push/create/delete/copy, blob uploads, and account, experimental, and diagnostic routes); limit additional browser origins (`OLLAMA_ORIGINS` adds to the built-in origins rather than replacing them); run under a dedicated unprivileged account and protect the model store (`User=ollama`, a restrictive `UMask`, owner-only store); bound inference pressure and distinguish limits from defaults (`OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL` default 1, `OLLAMA_MAX_QUEUE` 512, `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` five minutes, VRAM-dependent context defaults, with proxy request-size and rate limits since these scheduler settings are not RAM, VRAM, or connection caps); control model provenance and registry transfers; disable unused cloud execution; and keep diagnostics private and disable request-body logging. Also corrected the `OLLAMA_AUTH`, CORS, and `https://`-prefix misconceptions, the parallelism-defaults-to-1 (not 4) and VRAM-dependent context wording, and the create-request file-handling description (validated relative filenames and digest paths at v0.34.2, not arbitrary host-file access); and replaced the mutable `main` Dockerfile citation with the v0.34.2-pinned reference. New service checks are REASONED with an OLLAMA-LIVE-1 demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to Ollama v0.34.2.
- Deepened `nats.md` (NATS and JetStream) with Tier-1 controls (#247): make credential ownership concrete (individually scoped password or NKey users, plus a conditional operator-mode JWT recipe with a resolver and preloaded system account); scope subjects with queue-group subscriptions and bounded `allow_responses`; isolate accounts with explicit stream export/import restrictions and a reserved `system_account`; bound JetStream at server and account level (`max_memory_store`/`max_file_store`, and per-account `max_memory`/`max_file`/`max_streams`/`max_consumers`/`max_bytes_required`/`max_ack_pending`) and separate JetStream administration (`$JS.API.>`) from message access; conditionally encrypt JetStream storage at rest (`encryption_key`, `cipher`); give cluster, gateway, and leafnode links their own authorization and TLS with peer verification and no `insecure`; keep the monitoring port private with the honest note that it has no native authentication; keep MQTT, WebSockets, and auth callout conditional and scoped (`allowed_connection_types`, an explicit browser-origin policy, an isolated callout account with an `xkey`); bound connections and slow consumers (`max_connections`/`max_subscriptions`/`max_control_line`/`max_pending`/`write_deadline`, and authorization and TLS `timeout`); and give each process only its required files (a non-root runtime and owner-readable secrets). Also corrected the pick-one-mechanism framing, the NKey generation step, the two successive authorization examples (resolved into one coherent configuration), the `deny`-overrides-`allow` qualification once a tracked `allow_responses` reply is permitted, the `verify_and_map` note, the outbound-only leafnode listener, the monitoring `/connz` metadata description, and the CLI Verify defects (the consumer-password pipeline that set a token instead of a password, the second terminal block that could not inherit the earlier subshell's server and credential context, and the missing `--inbox-prefix`); and replaced the two mutable `main` GitHub citations with a natscli v0.4.0-pinned reference and stable hardening documentation. New service checks are REASONED with a NATS-LIVE-1 demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to NATS Server v2.14.7 and natscli v0.4.0.
- Deepened `object-storage.md` (S3, Cloudflare R2, Google Cloud Storage, Azure Blob, and Supabase Storage) with Tier-1 controls (#246): keep new buckets private and remove the secondary permission system (S3 Block Public Access on all four settings at the account level plus Object Ownership "Bucket owner enforced", GCS uniform bucket-level access with public access prevention, Azure `allowBlobPublicAccess=false`, R2 with the `r2.dev` Public Development URL disabled, Supabase private buckets); scope access per principal and per prefix (S3 IAM roles and conditioned bucket policies, GCS IAM with the uniform-access requirement and 90-day irreversibility caveat, R2 least-privilege API tokens, per-operation Supabase RLS policies on `storage.objects` scoped with `bucket_id`); reject broadly privileged account keys (Azure disallow Shared Key, and no root, account-key, or service-role credential in client code); share objects only through short-lived signed URLs (presigned and SAS expiry bounds, with R2 presigned URLs valid on the S3 API domain and not on custom domains); enforce HTTPS-only transport and network-scoped access where supported; give application credentials a finite lifetime and a real revocation path; require encryption at rest with customer-managed keys where independent key control is needed (noting no encryption scheme repairs public permissions); and add object-lock/WORM retention and access or audit logging for irreplaceable data. Also corrected the GCS seven-day CLI-signing qualification (it also works with an activated service account, not only `--private-key-file`), the Azure SAS expiration-policy behavior (`Log` records and permits out-of-policy use while `Block` denies it) and the Shared Key wording (account-key-authorized requests are rejected, rather than account-key SAS being impossible to generate), the Azure anonymous-denial status codes (the remediation page documents `401`/`409` while the configuration page documents `403`, so no single code is universal), the R2 custom-domain qualification (a custom domain can be protected by Cloudflare Access or WAF, so "no custom domain" is one valid private baseline and not the only secure one), and the Supabase ownership example (add `bucket_id` for a bucket-specific policy). New service checks are REASONED with an OBJECT-STORAGE-LIVE demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use official AWS, Cloudflare, Google Cloud, Microsoft Azure, and Supabase documentation.
- Deepened `firebase-supabase.md` (Firebase and Supabase) with Tier-1 controls (#245): Firebase - ownership rules for all three engines (Firestore, RTDB with the root-cascade warning, and Storage separately), authorize Callable and HTTP Functions and keep emulators unreachable, enforce App Check per product and restrict the public API key, and disable unused sign-in providers with email-enumeration protection. Supabase - close Data-API exposure (RLS plus grants and exposed schemas), the current publishable/secret key model versus JWT signing-key rotation, harden `SECURITY DEFINER` functions (`search_path`, `http`/`pg_net`, `vault.decrypted_secrets`), separate Storage and Realtime authorization, and constrain signups and anonymous auth. Also corrected the "only server-side gate is the rules layer" overstatement, the Firestore `rules_version`/recursive-wildcard semantics, the automatic-grants and "no policy means no access" qualifications, the view and `SECURITY DEFINER` overbroad claims, the Verify owner-write expectation, and the bundle-scan key guidance. New service checks are REASONED with a demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Citations use official Firebase, Supabase, and PostgreSQL documentation.
- Deepened `pocketbase.md` (PocketBase and Appwrite) with Tier-1 controls (#244): PocketBase - extend authorization beyond the five API rules to the auth `manageRule`, protected file fields with `viewRule` and file tokens, and realtime list/view subscription rules; operator protection (`superuserIPs`, proxy-restricted `/_/`) and explicit auth defaults (MFA/OTP for `_superusers`, `authRule`, the stateless-token caveat); settings encryption via `PB_ENCRYPTION_KEY` and `--encryptionEnv`; rate limits (`rateLimits.enabled`, default false) and working SMTP; and TLS/proxy-trust/`--origins`. Appwrite - collection/document permissions and `documentSecurity`, the server-key permission bypass and least-privilege key scopes; `_APP_CONSOLE_WHITELIST_*` registration and `_APP_OPTIONS_ROUTER_PROTECTION` hostname enforcement; `_APP_OPENSSL_KEY_V1`; `_APP_OPTIONS_ABUSE` and `_APP_OPTIONS_FORCE_HTTPS`; upload limits/antivirus and the function `execute`/scope boundary with a private executor/orchestrator. Also corrected the first-user bootstrap overstatement (the 30-minute installer token), the "no rule denies everything" overbroad claim (unprotected files; client-created grants), and replaced the two ineffective `curl -I` Verify checks with real authorization and registration probes. New service checks are REASONED with a demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to PocketBase v0.40.4 and Appwrite 2.2.0.
- Deepened `gradio.md` with four Tier-1 controls and a Gradio 6.28.0 currency update (#243): disable optional execution surfaces and distinguish API visibility from authorization (`GRADIO_VIBE_MODE` unset, `launch(mcp_server=False)`, and the 6.x `footer_links`/`api_visibility` replacements for the removed `show_api`/`api_name=False`); bound queued work and close the direct-API queue bypass (`queue(api_open=False, default_concurrency_limit, max_size)`, `launch(max_threads, max_file_size)`); restrict server-side URL fetching as an SSRF/outbound surface (no destination-allowlist argument, so deployment egress limits and `gr.load` target trust, with the 6.16.0/6.20.0 fix history); and disable unnecessary persistent collection (`flagging_mode='never'`, `run_history=False`, `analytics_enabled=False`). Also isolated the file cache (`GRADIO_TEMP_DIR`, allowed/blocked-path env inheritance), added external-auth/proxy/session guidance (`auth_dependency`, `server_name`, `root_path`, no server-side token expiry), and corrected the allowlist framing, the returned-path dotfile note, the upload-limit 6.20.0 baseline, and the port-search comment. New service checks are REASONED with a GRADIO-LIVE backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to the gradio@6.28.0 release tag.
- Deepened `open-webui.md` with six Tier-1 controls (#242): keep authentication on and persist a secret signing key (`WEBUI_AUTH`, an injected `WEBUI_SECRET_KEY`, `JWT_EXPIRES_IN`, Redis-backed revocation); close alternative registration and admin-impersonation paths (the `WEBUI_AUTH_TRUSTED_EMAIL_HEADER` proxy-strip requirement and the OAuth signup/domain/merge/role switches); restrict server-side Tools/Functions and code execution (`ENABLE_PLUGINS`, additive-across-groups workspace tool permissions, `ENABLE_CODE_EXECUTION`/`ENABLE_CODE_INTERPRETER`); constrain model access, backend connections, and admin data access (`BYPASS_MODEL_ACCESS_CONTROL`, private model access control, pinned connections, `ENABLE_DIRECT_CONNECTIONS`, `ENABLE_ADMIN_CHAT_ACCESS`/`ENABLE_ADMIN_EXPORT`); keep API keys off unless needed and endpoint-restricted (`ENABLE_API_KEYS`, `ENABLE_API_KEYS_ENDPOINT_RESTRICTIONS`); and bound retrieval, network, and resource abuse (`ENABLE_LOCAL_WEB_FETCH`, `AIOHTTP_CLIENT_ALLOW_REDIRECTS`, `RAG_FILE_MAX_SIZE`/`RAG_FILE_MAX_COUNT`, with the honest note that the upload and URL-ingestion handlers do not check the feature permission, so proxy request-body and route limits are also required). Also corrected the first-admin empty-database signup-bypass warning, the effective-signup-default lifecycle, and the `ENABLE_PASSWORD_AUTH`/trusted-header ordering. New service checks are REASONED with an OPENWEBUI-LIVE backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to commit 0a7c15832fb30b1903753e83f81dc7d27e5b0944.
- Deepened `mosquitto.md` with five Tier-1 controls (#241): topic authorization via `acl_file` (per-user `topic`/`pattern` read/write rules, `$SYS` isolation, and the subscribe-versus-message-access caveat) so authenticated devices cannot read or write each other's topics; explicit resource limits (`max_connections`, `max_packet_size`, `message_size_limit`, `max_inflight_messages`/`max_queued_messages`/`max_queued_bytes`, `max_keepalive`, with verified 2.0/2.1 defaults); per-listener scoping via `per_listener_settings` (and its 2.1 deprecation in favour of `listener_allow_anonymous`/`plugin_load`), including WebSocket listeners and the 2.0 explicit-listener default change; bridge security (`bridge_cafile`, `bridge_insecure false`, scoped remote credentials and topics, `try_private`); and host-side protection (privilege drop, persistence/log/secret file permissions, and the 2.0 early privilege drop). Also corrected the 2.0 anonymous-default note, the `mosquitto_passwd -D` reload/termination caveat, and the Verify CONNACK/exit-code guidance. - Deepened `litellm.md` with six Tier-1 controls (#240): per-key spend and throughput limits (`max_budget`/`budget_duration`, `rpm_limit`/`tpm_limit`, `max_parallel_requests`, with shared Redis required for correct limits and revocation across replicas, and `fail_closed_budget_enforcement` noted as not an absolute cap); explicit model-access allowlists on keys and teams (`models`, warning that `[]` means all models and that the master key bypasses model restrictions); finite key lifetime and real revocation (`duration`, `/key/delete`, `/key/block`, with in-place regeneration Enterprise-gated); separation of application credentials from admin authority plus removal of public surfaces (narrow `allowed_routes`, `admin_only_routes` exact-match and Enterprise-only, `NO_DOCS`/`NO_REDOC`/`NO_OPENAPI` with the URL-override caveat); constrained caller-controlled destinations and pass-through SSRF (`api_base` pinning is not an egress allowlist, `pass_through_endpoints` with `auth: true`); and keeping request content out of logs (`turn_off_message_logging`, `store_prompts_in_spend_logs: false`). PII masking guardrails and prompt-injection detection are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `memcached.md` with four Tier-1 access controls (#239): run under a dedicated unprivileged account (`-u`, no root, no shared login); bound authenticated and unauthenticated pressure with a connection cap and an item-memory cap (`-c`, `-m`) so connection file descriptors and total item memory stay bounded (bounded budgets, not a request-rate or eviction guarantee); disable cache-wide invalidation and the remote shutdown command (`-F` for `flush_all`, no `-A`); and disable key enumeration and live watch access (`-X` to disable the `stats cachedump`/`stats detail` and `lru_crawler metadump`/`mgdump` surfaces, `-W` to refuse `watch`). Also hardened the network posture (private `-l` binding, `-U 0` to turn UDP off so memcached cannot be used as a reflection amplifier) and corrected the SASL notes (binary protocol only, and the patched-baseline version). New service checks are REASONED with a MEMCACHED-LIVE backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. TLS (`--enable-tls` builds), Unix-domain-socket exposure, and the remaining optional command surfaces are recorded as a follow-up.

- Fixed a false positive in `check_guard_conventions.py --strict-guards` (#225): the opt-in C2-WARN-ONLY-GUARD rule flagged a probe already covered by an effective C2 sentinel guard (`mosquitto.md:69`, whose `nc` sits in the guarded `*)` arm of a case sentinel), because it picked the first probe after a warn-only sentinel `if` without checking the probe's own coverage. The strict rule now suppresses a probe only when every operand variable it interpolates is covered by a case guard whose subject is that same variable (the main C2 coverage plus a subject-operand match, computed regardless of `--no-c2`; an unknown subject or operand denies suppression). It no longer inherits C2's unrelated-value blind spot: the mosquitto false positive is gone, while a probe using one variable under a case guarding another is still flagged. Main C2 emission is byte-unchanged. Self-test grows from 157 to 213. This resolves TODO row 3.14: the general guard-grammar auto-gate stays UNIMPLEMENTED (a zero-false-positive lexical gate for the if/elif, `-z`, flag-variable, and renamed-sentinel shapes is not justified, because there are no corpus true-positives to gain and the corpus guard-then-early-exit idiom false-positives a lexical rule), and `check_guard_conventions.py --strict-guards` remains the opt-in manual audit tool; this change only removes that manual tool's one false alarm.

- Redefined and enforced the `check_guard_conventions.py` C2 rule (`C2-PROBE-OUTSIDE-GUARD`) (#220). A placeholder-guard `case` whose every `REPLACE_WITH_` sentinel arm unconditionally exits or returns now extends its coverage past `esac` to the close of its own subshell or function scope, so the house exit-guard idiom reads as safe; warn-then-fall-through, a conditional or backgrounded exit, an exit inside an inner subshell or command substitution, a redefined `exit`/`return`, `;&`/`;;&` fallthrough, a quoted non-matching pattern, and a probe inside the sentinel arm's own body all stay flagged. Sentinel recognition now inspects every `|` alternative of a case pattern rather than only the last, closing a pre-existing blind spot that left the placeholder-first idiom (about 72 of 97 corpus guard arms) unchecked. Correcting the recognizer surfaced three previously-hidden probe-quality defects, now fixed by guarding their placeholders (the `mcp-servers.md` Origin-control probes guard the auth-header file; the `model-servers.md` `/invocations` probe guards the served-model name); and 44 standalone probes that carry no reader-substituted value (a fixed loopback, an illustrative example host, or an interactively-supplied credential) across 18 guides now carry a one-line `# guard-conventions: allow` waiver stating why each is safe. With the corpus at zero C2 findings, `--no-c2` is removed from `tools/run_all_checks.sh`, so C2 is enforced on `main`. Six rounds of tri-family adversarial QA then hardened the rule: a case earns coverage only from a genuine, effective, unquoted sentinel alternative that is not pre-empted by an earlier overlapping arm (closing the arm-order, quoted-sentinel, bracket-expression, and pinned-prefix false negatives), and a quoted `()` argument no longer drops its command; the module's KNOWN REMAINING BYPASSES section now discloses the residual lexer and recognition limits inherent to a shell tripwire (composite punctuation, function-keyword and case-bodied guards, DEBUG-trap/eval/source/enable -n, negated-bracket and time-prefix recognition gaps, quoted heredoc, comment continuation, and placeholder dataflow). The self-test grows from 91 to 157 cases, pinning the termination-certificate, scope, alternative-recognition, and effective-sentinel rules including the false-negative canaries.

In `caddy.md` and `traefik.md`, the under-limit body-size positive control is tightened from "must NOT be 413" to requiring the app's own normal response (a 2xx, or its own 404 or redirect), never 413, and treats a 401 (credentials) or 000 (transport) as voiding the control rather than passing it (rule 5). Covers the argo-server exposure surface: `--port` 2746 with HTTPS and `--auth-mode=client` the default since v3.0 (client mode uses the caller's own Kubernetes bearer token and that identity's permissions); the dangerous pre-v3.0 `--auth-mode=server` default, where argo-server uses its OWN ServiceAccount for API requests so anyone who can reach it acts with that account's permissions without authenticating, and adding another mode does not remove the anonymous fallback while `server` stays enabled; `--auth-mode=sso` (v2.9+) with SSO RBAC (`sso.rbac.enabled`, v2.12+) mapping groups to narrowly scoped ServiceAccounts via `workflows.argoproj.io/rbac-rule` and `-rbac-rule-precedence`, without which authenticated users share the server identity; OAuth client credentials in Kubernetes Secrets; and keeping 2746 private behind an authenticating HTTPS proxy with MFA, NetworkPolicy, TLS left on (`--secure=false` disables it), and least-privilege server, SSO, and workflow-execution ServiceAccounts, since permission to submit workflows runs arbitrary containers. The Verify block gains a reasoned `/api/v1/workflows/{namespace}` discriminator (a server-mode anonymous 200 list is the finding; client/sso return 401 without a token or 403 for an authorization denial) with the bearer token fed to curl on stdin via `--header @-`, never argv. Fixed prose and config: the default bind is now stated as all interfaces on `8501`; the proxy example gives the `127.0.0.1:8501` upstream with the required WebSocket upgrade and Origin preservation; `server.enableCORS`/`enableXsrfProtection` stay enabled (with the XSRF-over-CORS nuance and a `cors.md` link) alongside `corsAllowedOrigins`/`allowedHosts`; configuration precedence and protecting the config surface are covered; the OIDC section names the 1.64.0 release (available since the 1.42.0 series) and the Authlib 1.3.2+ dependency; the auth gate covers multipage apps and privileged callbacks; secret handling adds never-render-or-log and `showErrorDetails`; the fallback section requires MFA rather than Basic auth or an emailed PIN alone; a new section covers upload limits, static serving outside the script's gate, the deployment half of code-execution risk (no eval of user input, least privilege), and an egress/SSRF link; and the client-id/secret placeholders use the house form. Reworked the Verify into a reasoned block (release blocker was the missing marker): a TLS probe with hygiene, a `/_stcore/health` no-auth discriminator that distinguishes the fronting-proxy pattern from native `st.login` (which serves an anonymous 200 by design), a guarded direct-origin `8501` reachability probe, and browser matched controls with an auth-disabled canary. Every curl `-q -g` with timeouts and no `-k`, no `A && B || C`. Ships reasoned; backlog row 2.36 opened.
