The domain is untrusted-code execution and credential theft, not an exposed listener. Fixed prose and threat model: the outbound-only framing now allows for autoscaler management and webhook-receiver listeners; a least-privilege runner account and enforced worker egress (with IMDSv2-is-not-enough) baseline; fork code in `pull_request_target`/`workflow_run` is unsafe even on an ephemeral runner; OIDC is another job credential on both platforms; the admin credential that provisions runners outranks the registration token and belongs off the worker; Kubernetes isolation (non-privileged, no privilege escalation, RBAC, token mounting) is specified; the GitLab privileged-mode quote is verbatim; the legacy registration token was disabled by default (17.0) with removal scheduled for 20.0, and the runner-creation access token is separate; the job-token allowlist controls inbound access on the target; a parent-project member can run a fork pipeline with parent resources; and the metrics and session-server listeners are described accurately. Reworked the Verify into REST eligibility audits (runner groups, group repositories, and repository-level runners), a workflow-permission check, a reasoned public-fork behavioral probe (a `runner-eligibility-probe` workflow plus the workflow-jobs assignment API), a Python `tomllib` config inspector that also catches a `tcp://` rootful daemon, GitLab job-token and runner API scope checks, a two-job contamination canary, and a guarded metrics-reachability probe; every curl `-q -g` with timeouts and no `-k`, no `A && B || C` construct, timeouts inconclusive. Ships reasoned; backlog row 2.29 broadened. Pinned Sources to the checked versions. Surfaced (out of scope) that the linked `egress-metadata.md` places AWS IMDS tokens in curl argv.
- Hardened `realtime-webhooks.md` against a two-family audit (#212; codex gpt-6-astra full-source FIX with 15 findings, several HIGH, cited against RFC 6455, the HTML and Fetch living standards, stripe-node v18.5.0 and stripe-python v12.5.0, Node crypto v22, and GitHub/Stripe/OWASP; claude-fable-5 the offline lens, convergent on the central defect that the Verify probes carried no positive controls and two negatives passed in exposed states). Ships reasoned; backlog row 2.35 tracks demonstrating them. Pinned Sources to the checked versions.- Fixed a guarded-probe defect in `clickhouse.md` surfaced by the workflow-orchestrators audit's offline lens (#209): the Verify subshell copied the validated positional into a named `host=$1` and then probed `"$host"`, the same guard-bypass class CONTRIBUTING rule 6 exists to prevent (a caller's `readonly host` makes the assignment fail while the probe reads the inherited value, and `declare -l`/`-u`/`-i` would transform it), so the probe could silently target the wrong host. Removed the named copy and used the positional `$1` directly in all three probes (plaintext `8123`, the HTTPS matched-triple `8443`, and the native `clickhouse-client`). No behavior change on a clean shell; ShellCheck does not catch this, only guard review does.
- Hardened `supabase-self-hosted.md` against a two-family audit (#208; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with fifteen findings, about seven HIGH, all cited against `self-hosted/v0.8.1`; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). The Verify was reworked: the functions probe takes an HTTPS base URL with `exit 2` guards and a matched authorized control, a default-API-key discriminator hits `/auth/v1/settings` with the shipped keys (not the admin-only `/rest/v1/` root), a forged HS256 `service_role` token signed with the public demo `JWT_SECRET` proves the secret itself was rotated, credentials move to stdin (`curl --config -`/`--header @-`), and a timeout is treated as inconclusive rather than the hardened state. - Hardened `surrealdb.md` against a two-family audit (#207; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with ten findings, about seven HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens, convergent on the missing positive control, the REPL-not-scriptable probe, the argv password, the absent capabilities surface, and the unnamed version). SurrealDB is a multi-model database over HTTP/WebSocket, so an open instance is read/write of every namespace and database. Confirmed sound and kept: the bootstrap `--user`/`--pass`/`SURREAL_*` flags and `--unauthenticated` granting root-`OWNER`-equivalent access, the `127.0.0.1:8000` modern default, record users with default-deny table permissions, `TYPE BEARER`, native TLS via `--web-crt`/`--web-key`, and the listener-inventory and `/health` framing. Fixed: the version boundary (since 2.0.0 authentication is enabled by default, `--auth` became `--unauthenticated`, and the CLI bind default moved from `0.0.0.0` to `127.0.0.1`; omitting `--user`/`--pass` does not disable authentication or rotate an existing root password); the root password is now supplied through the environment or a prompt rather than argv; a new Capabilities section (`--allow-all`/`--deny-all` default off, network denied, functions allowed, scripting and guests off, more-specific-wins with deny-over-allow; `http::*` and JWKS retrieval make outbound requests, so restrict destinations and apply [egress-metadata.md](egress-metadata.md) because authentication does not prevent SSRF); system users at every level are governed by RBAC and bypass table and field `PERMISSIONS`, so a namespace or database system user is not record-level isolation; `TYPE JWT` grants system-level sessions whereas `TYPE RECORD WITH JWT` is the record-level path, and JWT trust needs issuer and audience enforcement (native `AUDIENCE` is a 3.3 feature, so on 3.2.x enforce it in `AUTHENTICATE`); `/signup` and `/signin` are public entry points, so omit `SIGNUP` when self-registration is unwanted (the legacy `DEFINE SCOPE` is removed in 3.0); and MFA belongs at the identity provider or an access gateway. The Verify was reworked into a paired `INFO FOR DB;` probe (no credentials versus a valid system-user JWT supplied through `curl --header @-` on stdin, against the same namespace/database and endpoint) plus a guarded origin-reachability probe, replacing the non-discriminating REPL check and the unrunnable `curl -vI https://`. The Verify ships reasoned because the authoring environment has no SurrealDB runtime; backlog row 1.82 tracks the live demonstration. Added capabilities, permissions, DEFINE ACCESS, 2.0.0-release, and HTTP-protocol references to Sources.
- Hardened `nats.md` against a two-family audit (#206; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with twenty findings, about eleven HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens, convergent on the credential-inheritance and cert-presenting negative controls, the unguarded placeholders, the argv password, and the missing route surfaces, and adding a plaintext no-credential probe). NATS and JetStream; an open server is publish/subscribe to every subject plus JetStream, KV, and object-store access. Confirmed sound and kept: the bare-server-unauthenticated-on-4222 default, `authorization` users/password/nkey/token, the `no_auth_user` warning, the allow-map syntax with deny-over-allow, the `verify`/`verify_and_map` TLS fields (both default false for client TLS), and the monitoring framing (8222 conventional, `/varz`/`/connz`/`/routez`/`/jsz`, no native login). Fixed: the cluster (6222), leafnode (7422), and gateway (7222) route listeners are separate surfaces with their own authentication and TLS (each defaulting its host to 0.0.0.0) that the client `authorization` and top-level `tls` do not cover, now a dedicated section; accounts and multitenancy (the shared `$G` account is not tenant isolation, so use distinct `accounts` with reviewed exports/imports) and the system account (`system_account`, default `$SYS`, whose credentials are administrative); the JWT signing hierarchy (the operator signs account JWTs and each account signs its user JWTs, not both by the operator); that `publish.allow` and `subscribe.allow` are independent; `default_permissions` and account scope for a user with no `permissions` block; a service-specific `_INBOX` prefix instead of `_INBOX.>`; private monitoring binding (`http: "127.0.0.1:8222"`, and `https_port` does not authenticate); and `max_connections`/`max_payload` (defaults 65536 and 1 MiB) plus a non-root process identity. The Verify was reworked: matched authenticated publish and subscribe positive controls with the password via the `NATS_PASSWORD` environment variable rather than argv, negative controls in a CLEAN environment (`env -i` plus `--no-context`, because an empty `--context` name does not clear the `NATS_*` variables) including a plaintext no-TLS no-credential probe whose success is itself the exposure, a forbidden-subscribe test that requires a permissions error rather than a quiet timeout, and a guarded monitoring probe; the unverified verbatim `:8222` documentation quote was paraphrased. - Hardened `haproxy.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#205; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with sixteen findings, six HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens, convergent on the body-size false-pass, the unmatched 401, and the missing management plane). HAProxy 3.0 TLS terminator and reverse proxy; its frontend, stats interface, runtime API, and directly reachable backends are separate exposure surfaces. The Verify was reworked into a guarded subshell: an auth triple (no, wrong, and valid credentials against one resource, the password supplied through `curl --config -` stdin rather than argv), the body-size cases run with valid credentials so a 401 cannot masquerade as the result plus the HAProxy-attribution fixture, payloads in a `mktemp -d` with a cleanup trap, `-q -g`/`-sS`/`--noproxy`/timeouts, and reasoned stats, runtime-socket, and mTLS checks. The Verify ships reasoned because the authoring environment has no HAProxy runtime; backlog row 1.80 tracks the live demonstration. - Hardened `connection-poolers.md` against a two-family audit (#203; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with thirteen findings, six HIGH; claude-opus-4-8, the offline lens, returned SHIP and diverged: it is network-blocked and could not source-verify, so its judgment was set aside for the source-verifying arbiter, whose Verify defects were reproduced and confirmed against the live guide). Covers PgBouncer 1.25.2 and pgpool-II 4.7.2. Confirmed sound and kept: the port and default inventory (PgBouncer 6432, client TLS `disable`, server TLS `prefer`, `auth_type=md5`, empty admin/stats lists; pgpool-II 9999/9898, `enable_pool_hba` false), the four-CVE 1.25.2 warning, the `auth_type=any` console analysis, the forced-`user=` and pg_hba-moves-to-the-pooler reasoning, and the `ss`-is-inventory framing. Fixed: the PgBouncer SCRAM pass-through prerequisites (client SCRAM, no forced user, identical secrets, and that local `peer` cannot supply the SCRAM exchange); `auth_query` activation and safety (`auth_user`, a trusted `search_path`, execution revoked from `PUBLIC`, and the CVE-2025-2291 `VALID UNTIL` check a custom query must keep); the pgpool-II backend-TLS fallback pinned to 4.7.2's `pool_ssl_negotiate_clientserver` (plaintext fallback is a limitation, not mandatory TLS, and a backend HBA rejection does not prove the pooler refused the downgrade); the `.pgpoolkey` mode-`600` requirement with `pg_enc` prompt-not-argument provisioning; and PCP credential provisioning (`pcp.conf` `username:MD5` via `pg_md5 -p`). The Verify was reworked: every probe is self-contained with TLS written literally in each connection string (the shared `$TLS` could inherit empty into a separately pasted block and silently drop to libpq `prefer`); the `RELOAD` runs under `ON_ERROR_STOP` in a guarded subshell (a following `grep` had masked a failed reload), with a caveat that a successful reload does not prove every HBA line loaded; a direct-database-bypass test requires the database to reject a client that must go through the pooler (`hostaddr` for the address, `host` for the certificate name); the PCP admin channel is tested with a correct and a wrong password on loopback-only 9898; and pgpool-II gets its own `PGPOOL SHOW enable_pool_hba`/`ssl` and `pool_hba.conf` inspection. - Hardened `code-server.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#202; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with ten findings, four HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens, convergent). code-server is browser-hosted VS Code, so an exposed instance is remote code execution and host filesystem access as the running user. Confirmed sound and kept: the SSH-forwarding-first preference, the `127.0.0.1:8080` bind and `password` auth default with a randomized generated password, the login rate limit (two per minute plus twelve per hour), and the single-factor login routed to a fronting MFA layer. Fixed: the Verify block did not discriminate authentication (a `-I`/HEAD request cannot tell a login page from the editor, and a rejection with no authenticated positive control proves nothing because a fronting proxy can answer 401 while the origin stays open), so it is reworked into a guarded subshell that keeps the response body, probes the same editor URL unauthenticated and then with a valid session (the cookie via a stdin header, never argv), probes every public origin address directly (any HTTP response there is itself a finding), and is confirmed in a browser; credential precedence is now explicit (`PASSWORD` and `HASHED_PASSWORD` override the file, the hashed form wins over plaintext, there is no `--password` command-line flag, and any flag overrides the file, with the path from `--config`/`CODE_SERVER_CONFIG`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`); the config example no longer drops the generated password when copied over the generated file and no longer ships an active certificate path (`cert: false` is the default and a bare `--cert` self-signs); the built-in proxy exposure surface is documented (`/proxy/<port>/`, `/absproxy/<port>/`, and `--proxy-domain` subdomains use code-server's own authentication that `auth: none` disables, plus `--skip-auth-preflight` and `--disable-proxy`) with an egress boundary linking [egress-metadata.md](egress-metadata.md); and the SSH forward now binds its local listener to loopback. - Hardened `clickhouse.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#201; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with fifteen findings, four HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). ClickHouse is a columnar OLAP database, so an open instance is read/write of every table plus host and network pivoting through table functions. Confirmed sound and kept: the upstream port and default inventory (a `default` user with an empty password, `networks` `::/0`, and `access_management`; `http_port` 8123, `tcp_port` 9000, `mysql_port` 9004, `postgresql_port` 9005, `interserver_http_port` 9009 enabled, with 8443/9440/9010 shown commented), `password_sha256_hex`/bcrypt (72-character limit)/`GRANT`/`ssl_certificate CN`, and native TLS via `openSSL`. Fixed: the external-source and SSRF surface was missing (the `url()` table function and HTTP dictionaries fetch URLs, `file()` reads local files vs `remote()` reaching other servers, the `SOURCES` grants split into READ and WRITE forms since 25.7, and `remote_url_allow_hosts` is matched before DNS resolution and on every redirect while omitting the section allows all hosts) so the guide now covers it and links [egress-metadata.md](egress-metadata.md); replication has its own `interserver_http_credentials` that a SQL-user password and TLS do not provide (keep `allow_empty` false); the listed client listeners follow `listen_host` but replica traffic follows `interserver_listen_host`; the upstream base defaults are not universal (the official Docker image restricts the `default` user's network access when no `CLICKHOUSE_*` variable is set); `config.d`/`users.d` merge semantics matter (commenting an element out does not remove an inherited listener, so use `remove="remove"` and `networks replace="replace"`); and ClickHouse 26.2 introduced native TOTP for XML-defined users (`time_based_one_time_password`), which is not available for SQL-driven users. The Verify block was reworked into a guarded subshell that probes the SAME origin with `--noproxy '*'`, timeouts, and CA trust (never `-k`): the disabled plaintext port 8123 must fail (any HTTP status is a failure), an empty/wrong/correct credential triple runs as `app` with the key passed through stdin headers (`X-ClickHouse-User`/`X-ClickHouse-Key`, not argv), and native 9440 is tested as a separate authenticator; `ss` is reframed as a listener inventory. The example `listen_host` used a TEST-NET-3 documentation address and is now a private-IP placeholder. Repointed the server-settings Source off its index and added `remote_url_allow_hosts`, `GRANT`/`SOURCES`, table-function, native-TOTP, 26.2-release, configuration-files, Docker default-user, and client references. The reworked Verify ships reasoned because the authoring environment has no ClickHouse runtime; backlog row 1.77 tracks the live demonstration.
- Hardened `llm-observability.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#200; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with seventeen findings F01 through F17, six HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). LLM tracing and observability tools (Langfuse, Arize Phoenix, Helicone, OpenTelemetry Collector) that store full prompts, completions, and provider API keys. The Langfuse forged-cookie analysis (`NEXTAUTH_SECRET`/`SALT`/`ENCRYPTION_KEY`), the Phoenix admin-password first-startup inertia, and the guarded Phoenix probe were confirmed sound; the findings were per-tool auth/exposure gaps and Verify discrimination. Phoenix: `PHOENIX_OAUTH2_<IDP>_ALLOW_SIGN_UP` defaults `True` so an OAuth provider self-provisions accounts, and local password login bypasses the IdP's MFA, so set it `False` and `PHOENIX_DISABLE_BASIC_AUTH=True` (F01); Phoenix also binds HTTP `0.0.0.0:6006` and a separate gRPC `[::]:4317`, and its Compose publishes PostgreSQL (F12). Helicone: it self-hosts with Better Auth whose `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` ships a placeholder default (`change-me-in-production` in the all-in-one image, `your-secret-key` in the repo Compose) that must be replaced, and its Compose publishes the backing stores (PostgreSQL, ClickHouse, MinIO/S3, Redis, MailHog) on host ports independent of the dashboard login, so the section now names Better Auth, replaces the secret, restricts signup, and removes or loopback-scopes every backing publication and the Jawn/S3 services (F02, F03). Langfuse: the session default is 14 days (20160 minutes), not 30, and the floor is an integer above five (F10), and the first account must be created before signup is disabled (or provisioned with `LANGFUSE_INIT_*`) or you lock yourself out (F11). Collector: the localhost default arrived in v0.104.0 and the feature gate was removed in v0.112.0 (not v0.110.0) (F09), and an authenticator must be listed under `service.extensions` and attached to each receiver, with the internal-metrics and zPages/pprof/health-check listeners inventoried (F14). Langfuse's container deployment does not terminate TLS, but Phoenix 8.29+ can terminate TLS natively for HTTP and gRPC via `PHOENIX_TLS_ENABLED` (default off) with `PHOENIX_TLS_CERT_FILE`/`PHOENIX_TLS_KEY_FILE`, so its default is TLS off, not TLS unsupported (F13). The Verify was reworked: a rejection is no longer read as authentication without a matched authenticated positive control (a fronting proxy can 401 while the origin is open), so the Langfuse public API and the Collector OTLP/HTTP endpoint are each probed without and with a valid credential (F04); `ss` is reframed as a namespace listener inventory needing an external probe (F05); the Langfuse dashboard check is a browser step because a discarded-body GET of `/` cannot tell a login page from a project view (F07); the probes gained `--noproxy '*'`, timeouts, and `-g` (F08); the Phoenix OTLP/HTTP path is now probed with an empty-`ExportTraceServiceRequest` protobuf body (an anonymous 401/403 versus an authenticated 2xx) and the separate gRPC `4317` receiver is called out as its own `ApiKeyInterceptor` (F06); the incorrect "415 before authentication" ordering claim was dropped (F15); and the guarded subshell now returns a nonzero not-probed status (F16). Backlog row 1.45 was broadened to the full reworked Verify with the reasoned-not-demonstrated checks named per tool (F17). Added the Helicone Docker, Langfuse headless-initialization, Phoenix OAuth/basic-auth, and Collector version-history references to Sources. Confirmed sound and kept: the Langfuse email/password and open-signup default with `AUTH_DISABLE_SIGNUP`/`AUTH_DISABLE_USERNAME_PASSWORD`, the Phoenix auth-off default with `PHOENIX_ENABLE_AUTH`/`PHOENIX_SECRET`, the `admin@localhost`/`admin` account and first-account-only password initialization, the Collector no-auth default with the `basicauth`/`bearertokenauth` extensions, and the per-tool port inventory.
- Hardened `kafka.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#199; codex gpt-6-astra full-source against Apache Kafka 4.3 returned FIX with eight findings KAF-01 through KAF-08, three HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). Apache Kafka (KRaft), where an open broker lets anyone read and write every topic and reconfigure the cluster. The TLS/SCRAM/ACL choices, the default-exposure warning, the bind-versus-advertise distinction, and the SASL_SSL-listener-prefix subtlety were confirmed sound, so the findings were controller coverage, a configuration conflict, and Verify discrimination. (KAF-01, HIGH) mapping the controller listener to `SASL_SSL` does not authenticate it (its SASL mechanism defaults to GSSAPI) and on a combined broker-and-controller node the controller listener must be in `listeners`, so the configuration now includes the controller listener with its own SASL mechanism, JAAS credential, and `super.users`, pointing to the listener-configuration reference for the version-sensitive specifics. (KAF-02) `security.inter.broker.protocol=SASL_SSL` was replaced with `inter.broker.listener.name=SASL_SSL`, because the shipped `server.properties` sets `inter.broker.listener.name=PLAINTEXT` and setting both is a startup error. (KAF-03) the `kafka-storage.sh format` example now generates the cluster ID once and reuses it across nodes (a fresh `random-uuid` per node cannot form a cluster) and passes a required quorum option (`--standalone` / `--initial-controllers` / `--no-initial-controllers`). (KAF-04, HIGH; Verify) the Verify never attempted an unauthenticated or PLAINTEXT connection, so it now adds an external PLAINTEXT / no-SASL probe that must be refused. (KAF-05, HIGH) the whole sequence could pass with `allow.everyone.if.no.acl.found=true`, so it now runs a valid non-superuser against a topic with no ACL and requires an authorization denial, and lists ACLs with `--resource-pattern-type match` so wildcard and prefix rules are included. (KAF-06) the positive control was made deterministic: it produces a unique record and consumes it from a fresh group with a bounded `--timeout-ms` (a reused group's committed offset makes `--from-beginning` read nothing). (KAF-07) `--consumer.config` (deprecated since 4.2) was replaced with `--command-config`. (KAF-08) the Redpanda Console note now states its built-in login requires an enterprise licence. Sources were moved to versioned 4.3 documentation and a release-tagged source file, replacing `trunk` and the 4.0 reference, and the KRaft-provisioning and Redpanda-Console-authentication references were added. Confirmed sound and kept: the `listeners=PLAINTEXT://:9092` / empty-authorizer defaults, that `advertised.listeners` is not access control, the 9092/9093 port conventions and the broker-9093-versus-controller-9093 conflict note, the JKS/PEM TLS syntax with hostname verification on by default, SCRAM-SHA-512 with TLS, the `StandardAuthorizer` deny-by-default, the KRaft/ZooKeeper-removed-in-4.0 boundary, and the `openssl s_client` TLS probe.
- Hardened `neo4j.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#198; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with nine findings N01 through N09, three HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). Neo4j graph database, where an exposed instance with the default `neo4j`/`neo4j` credential is full data access (and, via procedures, code/SSRF). The initialization, connector, TLS, and user/role settings were confirmed sound; the findings were omitted procedure/SSRF controls, Verify discrimination, and Docker/framing accuracy. (N01, HIGH) a new section restricts procedures and outbound requests: keep `dbms.security.procedures.unrestricted` empty and narrow `dbms.security.procedures.allowlist` (default `*`), keep `apoc.import.file.enabled=false` (APOC settings live in `apoc.conf` in Neo4j 5, with `apoc.import.file.use_neo4j_config=true`), and because `LOAD CSV`/`apoc.load.*` make outbound requests (an SSRF vector Neo4j documents), set `dbms.security.allow_csv_import_from_file_urls=false` when unneeded (it defaults to `true` in the source despite a vendor page saying otherwise), restrict egress from the metadata subnet ([egress-metadata.md](egress-metadata.md), newly cross-linked), and set `internal.dbms.cypher_ip_blocklist` where supported. (N02, HIGH; Verify) the HTTP-off probe hid errors with `-s` and had no proxy bypass or timeout, so a silent DNS failure (curl exit 6) read as connection-refused; it now uses `--noproxy '*'`, timeouts, `-g`, and reads the exit code (7 refused is the pass, 6/28 inconclusive). (N03, HIGH) the plaintext-Bolt test used a deliberately wrong password, so on an `OPTIONAL` listener it would connect and then fail auth, masquerading as a TLS refusal; it now probes the transport over `bolt://` and treats an authentication error as "plaintext accepted", not a pass. (N04) the Verify gained an authenticated `RETURN 1 AS ok` positive control (with `cypher-shell` prompting for the password rather than taking `-p`), an HTTPS-7473 authentication check, and clearer negative-result classification; `ss` was reframed as a namespace listener inventory. (N05) the intro now notes the official Docker image sets `server.default_listen_address=0.0.0.0`, and documents `NEO4J_AUTH=neo4j/<password>` (never `none`) plus that it does not rotate an existing database's credential. (N06) a "password change required" response to a `neo4j`/`neo4j` login is called out as meaning the factory credential is still accepted, not a rejection. (N07) plain LDAP `simple`-bind password authentication is distinguished from MFA (enforce MFA at an OIDC provider, or a verified LDAP-MFA integration). (N08) the initial-password and verification commands keep the real password out of shell history (prompting for `cypher-shell`). (N09) the bind example is a `REPLACE_WITH_PRIVATE_IP` placeholder rather than the unusable `203.0.113.10` documentation address. Added the Docker, securing-extensions, APOC-configuration, and SSRF references to Sources. Confirmed sound and kept: `neo4j-admin dbms set-initial-password`, the `auth_enabled`/min-length/lockout defaults, the connector and TLS settings (`dbms.ssl.policy.*`, `bolt.tls_level=REQUIRED`, HTTP off), the `openssl s_client` Bolt-TLS probe, and the `CREATE USER`/Enterprise-`GRANT ROLE` model with the Community-implied-admin caveat.
- Hardened `n8n.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#197; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with nine findings N8N-01 through N8N-09, five HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). n8n workflow automation, where an open instance is a stored-credential vault plus a code-execution and webhook surface. The binding/TLS names, the login/first-owner model, the removed-in-1.0 basic-auth/JWT, the public-API `X-N8N-API-KEY`, and the webhook default-`none` auth were confirmed sound, so the findings were Verify discrimination and omitted controls. (N8N-01/N8N-02, HIGH; Verify) HTTPS plus a public-API 401 can coexist with an unclaimed instance, and a webhook 401/403/200 can come from a proxy or a workflow rather than node auth, so the Verify now requires completed owner setup before exposure, tests the public API as a matched pair (no key gives 401 from n8n, a valid key returns the workflow JSON, with the key on stdin via `-H @-`), notes the editor's separate `/rest/login` session, and tests each production webhook three ways (absent, wrong, and valid credential) with a valid payload and execution evidence, in guarded subshells with `--noproxy '*'`, timeouts, `-g`, and status/exit (N8N-03). (N8N-05, HIGH) the secrets-vault note now covers `N8N_ENCRYPTION_KEY`: unset does not mean plaintext (n8n auto-generates and saves a key under `~/.n8n`), but losing it while keeping the database strands every stored credential and committing it leaks the decryption secret, so set it explicitly, keep it identical across workers, and back it up. (N8N-06, HIGH) a new section adds code-execution isolation: the Code and Execute Command nodes run code (Execute Command is off by default from 2.0), so use hardened external task runners rather than the insecure-by-design internal mode, keep `N8N_BLOCK_ENV_ACCESS_IN_NODE=true`, and allow modules narrowly via `NODE_FUNCTION_ALLOW_BUILTIN`/`NODE_FUNCTION_ALLOW_EXTERNAL`. (N8N-07, HIGH) the same section adds outbound containment: a webhook or the HTTP Request node can be steered at internal services or the cloud metadata endpoint, so enable `N8N_SSRF_PROTECTION_ENABLED=true` (from 2.12.0) and restrict workload egress ([egress-metadata.md](egress-metadata.md), newly cross-linked). (N8N-04) the intro distinguishes host loopback from a bridged container's loopback (use a private Compose network or `127.0.0.1:5678:5678`, and keep Postgres and runner ports unpublished). (N8N-08) the public URL is set fully (`N8N_PROTOCOL`, `N8N_WEBHOOK_URL` replacing the deprecated `WEBHOOK_URL`, `N8N_SECURE_COOKIE=true`, the trusted proxy-hop count). (N8N-09) the environment-managed MFA policy is qualified as n8n 2.18.0+. Added the encryption-key, task-runners, SSRF-protection, and reverse-proxy references to Sources, and dropped three failing `.md` suffixes from existing Source URLs. Confirmed sound and kept: `N8N_LISTEN_ADDRESS=::` / port 5678 / `N8N_SSL_*`, the first-owner model with no default password, that basic-auth and JWT were removed in 1.0 (the guide does not use them), the `X-N8N-API-KEY` / `/api/v1` public API, the webhook default-`none` auth with per-node Basic/Header/JWT, `N8N_SECURE_COOKIE` default true, and SQLite-default with Postgres optional.
- Hardened `jupyter.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#196; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with six findings JUP-01 through JUP-06, one HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). Jupyter Server (also JupyterLab and Notebook 7), where an exposed server is remote code execution. Every configuration command (`jupyter server --generate-config`, `jupyter server password`, `certfile`/`keyfile`, the `jupyter lab` aliases, `ServerApp.ip`) was confirmed current and sound, so the findings were Verify discrimination and framing. (JUP-01, HIGH) the Verify did only an HTTPS `GET /` (which proves TLS, not authentication) and a browser login-prompt observation (only UI behaviour, and a proxy can guard the UI while `/api` stays open), so it now probes a PROTECTED API route (`/api/kernels`) both ways through the external URL: without a token native Jupyter returns 403 (not 401) and a valid token returns the kernel JSON (the positive control), with the token fed to curl on stdin (`-H @-`). (JUP-02) the probe gained `--noproxy '*'`, timeouts, `-g`, and exit/error reporting. (JUP-03) the `--cacert` guidance was made portable: a public-CA certificate uses the normal trust store, a private CA its own bundle, a self-signed certificate the cert from step 2 (hostname verification stays on, never `-k`). (JUP-04) the intro no longer says to keep token authentication specifically, because setting a password replaces the auto-generated token by default; the requirement is that some native credential stays in force (a token and a password can coexist as alternatives, not a second factor), and the common-mistakes line now clarifies that disabling the token when a password is set is fine while disabling both is not. (JUP-05) the `ss` annotation was reframed as a namespace listener inventory (loopback can be IPv4 `127.0.0.1` and/or IPv6 `::1`, the port starts at 8888 but can differ, and it is not a firewall or forwarding check, so probe externally too). (JUP-06) the Jupyter Server security, configuration-reference, Notebook-migration, and curl TLS-verification references were added to Sources (the sole prior source still demonstrates the deprecated `ServerApp.password` and SHA-1 hashes). Confirmed sound and kept: the configuration commands and Argon2 password hashing, the random-token default and that emptying the token disables token auth only when no password remains, the Notebook 7 = Jupyter Server backend, the TLS setup, and that the original probe put `-q` first and rejected `-k`.
- Hardened `litellm.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#195; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with eight findings F1 through F8, one HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). LiteLLM proxy (master key + virtual keys) fronting paid model APIs. The master-key names and format, the virtual-key setup, and the #92 explicit-loopback bind were confirmed sound, so the findings were Verify discrimination and coverage. (F1, HIGH) the Verify pair could pass while LiteLLM itself was keyless behind another auth layer (a fronting gateway can return 401 without a header, then forward a header-bearing request to a keyless proxy that returns the model list), so the check now probes the BACKEND directly on loopback three ways - with no key and with a wrong key it must return 401, and a valid virtual key returns the model list - inside a guarded subshell, with the valid key passed via `curl -H @file` so it stays out of shell history and `/proc`, plus `--noproxy '*'`, timeouts, `-g`, and printed status/exit; `ss -tlnp` is reframed as a namespace listener inventory (not a firewall/NAT/publication check) with an external-reachability step. (F4) credentials were kept out of shell history and process arguments (the master-key `export` and the `-H "Authorization: Bearer $KEY"` request). (F5) the admin UI at `/ui` was documented as having its own login (`UI_USERNAME` defaults to `admin`; without `UI_PASSWORD` it accepts the master key), with `DISABLE_ADMIN_UI` and `PROXY_BASE_URL` for `Secure` cookies. (F6) stored-secret handling was added: `general_settings.store_model_in_db` persists provider credentials encrypted with `LITELLM_SALT_KEY` (which falls back to the master key), so a permanent salt must be set before adding credentials, and virtual keys are stored hashed. (F7) route coverage was corrected: `/health/liveliness` is public, `/health` authenticated, the docs routes public, and `/metrics` authenticated by default since v1.85.0 (so a "public /metrics" claim would be stale). (F8) configurable outbound destinations (`api_base`, pass-through `target`) were named an SSRF and egress trust boundary, with a new `egress-metadata.md` cross-link. Confirmed sound and kept: `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY`/`general_settings.master_key`/the `sk-` prefix with config-over-env precedence, that an unset master key permits no-auth inference (logged, not silent, and granting an internal-user rather than proxy-admin role), the PostgreSQL/`DATABASE_URL`/`POST /key/generate` virtual-key flow, and the `--host 127.0.0.1` loopback fix. Added the LiteLLM UI, security-best-practices, config-settings, Prometheus, and pass-through references to Sources.
- Hardened `ai-infra-services.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#194; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with seven findings F1 through F7, one HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). Six self-hosted AI-infra services (SearxNG, LocalAI, Text Embeddings Inference, LangServe, Mem0, Onyx). The authentication defaults, the backing-store publication controls, and the guarded probes were confirmed sound, so the findings were accuracy and Verify-discrimination refinements. (F1, HIGH) the checks could certify an unprotected ingress: a keyed backend behind a TLS-only reverse proxy passes checks 5-7 while anonymous ingress `/metrics` and docs stay open (Text Embeddings Inference applies its key middleware to the inference routes only), so check 5 now tests the ingress and the native layer as separate controls, probing a route outside the native key (TEI's `/metrics`/`/docs`) anonymously THROUGH the ingress and, where ingress and app credentials differ, sending the app credential without the ingress one to confirm the ingress still rejects. (F3) the claimed second TEI metrics listener on `--prometheus-port` 9000 was corrected: the documented HTTP build serves `/metrics` on the main application port (its exporter future is discarded by `.build()`), while the standalone `--prometheus-port` exporter belongs to the gRPC build. (F4) `retain its loopback bind` was corrected because LocalAI defaults to `:8080` and TEI to `0.0.0.0:3000`, so a direct-host run must bind them explicitly (`LOCALAI_ADDRESS=127.0.0.1:8080`, TEI `--hostname 127.0.0.1`). (F5) SearxNG's `server.secret_key`/`SEARXNG_SECRET` (a cryptographic secret whose shipped `ultrasecretkey` placeholder is rejected at production startup) and its server-side URL fetching / image-proxy SSRF surface were added, with a new `egress-metadata.md` cross-link. (F6) Onyx's stored document-source connector credentials (a retained `credential_json` per connector) were named as protected stored data needing least privilege, backup protection, and rotation. (F2) concrete request fixtures were added (TEI `POST /embed` with `{"inputs":"probe"}`, a 401 without the key and a 2xx with it) and check 6 now pairs anonymous, wrong-key, and valid-key against the same private backend. (F7) the check-1 and check-3 placeholder guards now return a nonzero not-tested status, and the check-1 `grep` and check-4 `nc` capture their exit under `set -e` instead of aborting the block. Confirmed sound and kept: each service's authentication default and the silent-no-auth cases (LocalAI/TEI unset keys), the Mem0 `AUTH_DISABLED`/`JWT_SECRET`/first-admin model, Onyx's first-user-admin and inert `AUTH_TYPE`, the backing-store removal and `!reset` publication guidance, and the guarded curl/nc/ss probes' transport hygiene.
- Hardened `gpu-clouds.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#193; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with thirteen findings GPU-01 through GPU-13, four HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline discrimination lens). Rented-GPU platforms (RunPod, Vast.ai, Lambda, Modal), where a bound listener becomes internet-reachable with only its own auth once exposed or mapped. The recurring Verify defect: a probe of the platform proxy hostname cannot attribute a 401/200 to the listener's own auth versus the platform proxy, and the section tested only a loopback Jupyter port plus one hostname. The Verify was reworked to LEAD with a direct-loopback matched pair testing the listener's OWN auth with no platform proxy in the path (Jupyter's `Authorization: token` scheme, 403 without and 200 with), then confirm each EXPOSED port from outside through a guarded per-port probe (paste/count/placeholder guard, `--noproxy '*'`, timeouts, `-g`, printed exit codes), reading an external 401 only alongside the direct pair; `ss -tlnp` is reframed as TCP listening sockets in this namespace (not UDP, not a firewall, not the platform's NAT/proxy publication). Accuracy corrections at source: the Vast.ai portal token is `OPEN_BUTTON_TOKEN` (with `WEB_PASSWORD` and an `ENABLE_AUTH`/`AUTH_EXCLUDE` pair), not `PORTAL_CONFIG` (which only configures routing), and a token-bearing portal link IS the credential; Modal's constructs were separated (a web endpoint, current decorator `modal.fastapi_endpoint`, is public until `requires_proxy_auth=True`; a Dedicated Endpoint uses `--unauthenticated`; a Server sets `unauthenticated=True` on `@app.server`; a Shared Endpoint always needs a Proxy Token); the "require the server's own API key" instruction was corrected to native-auth-where-supported, else an authenticating gateway, because Ollama's API has no native authentication; the intro no longer claims a bound port is public "the moment it listens" (RunPod exposes, Vast maps, Lambda's inbound firewall is deny-by-default, Modal depends on the construct); and the "a firewall rule deleted" exposure example was corrected because deleting a Lambda allow rule closes the port, so the risk is a widened rule. Added per-platform SSH key-handling guidance (Lambda requires a key at launch, Vast.ai disables password auth, RunPod recommends keys). Confirmed sound and kept: the RunPod HTTP/TCP distinction and the symmetrical `>70000` mapping, the bind-`0.0.0.0`-for-the-RunPod-proxy versus loopback-behind-Vast's-Caddy distinction, Lambda's deny-by-default inbound with the ICMP/port-22 exception, Modal's proxy-token headers, the noVNC/VNC remote-desktop caveat, and the account-MFA-versus-workload-auth separation.
- Hardened `agent-builders.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#192; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with twelve findings AB-01 through AB-12, four HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 was fully network-blocked and returned FIX on offline Verify-discrimination alone, its factual claims discarded where unverified, including a mistaken claim that egress-metadata.md was already cross-linked which the live file refuted). Self-hosted agent/workflow builders (Dify, Flowise, Langflow, LibreChat), each an editor UI plus callable APIs over a stored-secret vault. The two families converged on the Verify section: a bare 401 does not prove the app's own auth (a fronting proxy can return it) and a HEAD or GET of `/` cannot tell an open editor from a login page (all four are SPAs serving the same shell), so the probes were reworked to disable client proxies (`--noproxy '*'`), bound and `-g`-guard each request, read the effective URL and status after following redirects, and test each app's own auth on its loopback address with the fronting reverse proxy out of the path (Dify's 8080 is its own bundled nginx forwarding to the api service, so strict attribution to that service uses a Compose-network probe), as a matched pair per app that shows the response body (without the key the app returns its own rejection, a Dify 401 requiring a Bearer token as distinct from a 400 app_unavailable, and a Flowise chatflow with no key assigned is public by design; with a valid key a 2xx returning real output is the positive control), while the edge curl of `/` is a transport-reachability note because curl cannot run a SPA's client-side login routing, so the editor and Dify `/install` setup state (an open create-admin form means the first visitor owns the instance) are confirmed in a browser; the external 5003 `nc` probe also gained a positive reachability control (a filtered path also times out). The biggest structural gap was that the guide covered authentication but not what a flow can EXECUTE or REACH: all four run custom-code and HTTP-request/tool nodes, so a new section frames the editor as code-execution and SSRF authority and adds runtime isolation, default-deny egress with the metadata address blocked (a new cross-link to `egress-metadata.md`), and keeping each tool's own guards on (Flowise `HTTP_SECURITY_CHECK`/`CUSTOM_MCP_SECURITY_CHECK`, LibreChat Actions' domain allowlist, Dify's SSRF proxy). Dify's pre-launch checklist was corrected because changing `SECRET_KEY` alone leaves working default service credentials in `docker/.env.example` (source-verified verbatim): `DB_PASSWORD`/`REDIS_PASSWORD` (`difyai123456`, with `CELERY_BROKER_URL` embedding the Redis password), the sandbox pair `CODE_EXECUTION_API_KEY`/`SANDBOX_API_KEY` (`dify-sandbox`), `PLUGIN_DAEMON_KEY` and `PLUGIN_DIFY_INNER_API_KEY`, and the agent keys `DIFY_AGENT_API_TOKEN` and `DIFY_AGENT_SERVER_SECRET_KEY` (whose known default permits agent-token forgery), plus the Weaviate keys and anonymous-access flag when that vector store is used; `ALLOW_REGISTER=false` (Dify) is noted as the default to keep. Registration and cookie corrections: Langflow's `LANGFLOW_NEW_USER_IS_ACTIVE=False` does not close signup (`ENABLE_SIGNUP` defaults `True`; set `LANGFLOW_ENABLE_SIGNUP=false`), and behind HTTPS `LANGFLOW_ACCESS_SECURE`/`LANGFLOW_REFRESH_SECURE` must be `true` (both default `false`, so session cookies lack `Secure`); `LANGFLOW_HOST=localhost` is wrong inside a bridged container (bind `0.0.0.0` and publish to host loopback) and the `LANGFLOW_*` settings must be injected into the container via Compose, not merely written to a `.env`; LibreChat's `.env.example` secrets are blank (a missing value bootstraps a random key, persisted only if its credentials file is written and kept, and a retired published `JWT_SECRET`/`JWT_REFRESH_SECRET` default is rejected at startup), so set your own permanent values; the `!override`/`!reset` Compose tags need Compose 2.24.4+; and the MFA note now distinguishes Flowise's Enterprise-only SSO and Langflow's external JWT/JWKS integration from the fronting-layer path. Confirmed sound by both families and kept: the loopback-bind and `!reset`/`!override` merge model, Dify `/install` setup (not a default admin pair) and the `INIT_PASSWORD` gate, Flowise's v3.0.1 account transition and per-chatflow key model, Langflow's default username and legacy-password rejection, LibreChat's first-account-admin and crypto-material lengths, and all existing cross-links and Sources. Added the Langflow security-model and LibreChat Actions references to Sources.
- Hardened `egress-metadata.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#191; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with eleven findings F01 through F11 and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX-minor while partly network-blocked, the two families converging on the IPv6-endpoint gap, the NetworkPolicy DNS breakage, and the missing probe timeouts, with codex's fuller pass adding the proxy-bypass, Flow-Logs, and authenticated-probe items). This guide is the deployment-side backstop against an agent/RAG/webhook SSRF reaching the cloud metadata service and exfiltrating credentials; the config facts (AWS IMDSv2 flags and hop-limit, the security-group metadata exemption, Azure `Metadata: true` and `X-Forwarded-For` rejection, the GCP header, Docker `--internal`) and the Verify's `time_connect`/exit-code discrimination were confirmed sound. The biggest gap was the IPv6 metadata endpoint: AWS also exposes `[fd00:ec2::254]` on Nitro instances in an IPv6-enabled subnet (IMDSv2-compatible) and GCP `[fd20:ce::254]` on IPv6-only instances, so an IPv4-only address block or probe passes while a credential endpoint stays reachable; the guide now blocks and probes both. The address block is reframed as a workload/guest control (host firewall, CNI, or egress proxy) because GCP VPC and hierarchical firewall rules, like AWS security groups, do not filter metadata traffic; a host-process `iptables` `OUTPUT` owner-match (with an nftables/forward-proxy note) was added because IMDSv2 plus hop-limit-1 still lets a process on the host obtain its own token. The AWS security-group guidance was corrected from "deny the rest" (security groups are allow-only) to removing the default allow-all egress and using a network ACL for deny rules, accounting for every attached group. The Kubernetes NetworkPolicy example was fixed to allow DNS (it otherwise breaks all hostname egress once it selects the pod) and to mark the `203.0.113.0/24` CIDR a placeholder, and the prose now notes policies are additive (a second policy can re-permit metadata) and that `hostNetwork` behaviour is undefined. The hop-limit "default 1" was qualified (launch parameters and the IMDSv2-only `ImdsSupport: v2.0` setting can make it 2). The Verify was hardened: the three header probes gained `--noproxy '*'` (Azure documents that IMDS queries must bypass proxies) and timeouts; the authenticated network-block probe gained the real AWS IMDSv2 two-step (PUT a token, then GET with it, since a tokenless 401 proves the header requirement not a network block) and an Azure `Metadata: true` variant, run before the block too as a positive control; the metadata-denial corroboration was corrected because AWS VPC Flow Logs exclude `169.254.169.254` traffic, so a CNI/iptables drop counter or packet capture is required for that flow (Flow Logs remain fine for the other egress destinations); and the `describe-instances` check now requires `State: applied`, not just the desired values. Added the AWS instance-metadata-options, AWS Flow Logs limitations, and GCP querying-metadata references to Sources. A further QA round scoped the NetworkPolicy DNS exception to the kube-system cluster DNS pods (an empty namespace selector had allowed a `k8s-app: kube-dns` pod in any namespace to be an allowed 53 destination), added a runnable IPv6 metadata probe for `[fd00:ec2::254]`/`[fd20:ce::254]` and made the `describe-instances` check assert `HttpProtocolIpv6: disabled` so the IPv6 path is verified rather than only described, hardened the authenticated AWS probe to validate token acquisition (recording the PUT exit and using `curl -f` so an HTTP error body is not captured as a token, with an empty token treated as inconclusive rather than a pass), relabelled a 200 from Azure's `/metadata/instance` as metadata reachability rather than credential issuance (Azure's managed-identity token is a separate `/metadata/identity/oauth2/token` request; GCP's probed path returns an access token, while AWS's `iam/security-credentials/` lists role names and the token PUT issues an IMDS session token, so the AWS checks establish metadata reachability, not credential issuance), and added `--noproxy '*'` to the STS positive control and the example.com negative control so they exercise direct egress rather than a forward proxy.

## 2026-09-17
- Hardened `gradio.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#190; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX against Gradio 6.27.0 and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX with partial network access, the two families converging: every `launch()` argument and default the guide uses is real and correct, and the guide rightly treats `auth` as a login form rather than HTTP Basic, so the findings fell in the Verify section and a few exposure-completeness notes). Gradio web-UI framework. The two families agreed the highest-severity gap was that the Verify section never tested `share=True`, the exposure the guide's own section 3 names as the primary risk: a share tunnel keeps the local server on `127.0.0.1:7860` and opens an OUTBOUND connection to Gradio's relay that publishes a `*.gradio.live` URL, so `ss -tlnp` (listening sockets only) shows "loopback" while the app is public. The Verify now confirms share is off and treats it as UNKNOWN unless all three hold: the previous app process or notebook kernel and its tunnel are terminated first (a rerun with `share=False` in a persistent kernel clears the URL but can leave the earlier tunnel connected), `share=False` is set in the `launch()` call, and THIS fresh run's startup output shows no `Running on public URL: https://<name>.gradio.live` line, plus confirming any known former public URL is now unreachable (a fragile log-grep or `ss` process match was tried and dropped, since the tunnel is an `frpc` child and an absent or stale log cannot establish share-off); terminate any tunnel after a demo. The auth check was also non-discriminating: a served login page does not prove the API is gated, so it now probes a protected API route (`/gradio_api/info`) from outside WITHOUT a Gradio session cookie but keeping any fronting-proxy authorization, so a 401 from Gradio's own `login_check` (native auth returns 401, not a redirect) is attributable to Gradio and not the proxy, never a 200 with the app config with an authenticated positive control (post credentials to `/login`, keep the cookie, re-request, expect 200) so a denial is Gradio's own auth firing and not a fronting proxy, noting that static assets are public so "some route returns 200" is not a failure. The probe gained `--noproxy '*'` and a note that a self-signed cert (section 2) needs `--cacert`, never `-k`. The file-access marker test now requires both markers to return their contents before one is blocked, distinguishing a `blocked_paths` refusal from a missing file, wrong path, login prompt, or proxy denial (the `/gradio_api/file=` route returns the same refusal for several). Exposure and auth completeness: the section-1 launch example now sets `server_name="127.0.0.1"` and `share=False` explicitly, because an omitted `server_name` can inherit `GRADIO_SERVER_NAME` and an omitted `share` can inherit `GRADIO_SHARE=True` or auto-enable in Colab and hosted notebooks; native `auth` is noted to have no rate limiting, throttling, or lockout and the pyotp workaround to need enrollment, secret storage, and replay rejection (the fronting route supplies these); and `max_file_size` was added, since it defaults to `None` (unlimited uploads) and is per-file, not a storage quota. The vendor security advisories for the historical `/file=` traversal/SSRF (fixed 4.11.0) and the case-handling blocklist bypass were added to Sources to substantiate the guide's bypass caveats. Both families confirmed sound: the default `127.0.0.1` bind, the login-form `auth` semantics and single-factor honesty, `ssl_verify=False` describing the launcher's server-side startup self-check (the repo's own `tools/check_verify_safety.py` corroborates this intended use), the `share=True` model, the `/gradio_api/file=` current route prefix and the default-set / returned-path / shared-cache / `blocked_paths`-precedence file-access model with its case and NTFS-ADS bypass caveats, and the cross-links.
- Hardened `headers.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#189; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX with partial network access, the two families converging: every header value, the caching guidance, and the six-token `Clear-Site-Data` logout example are correct and confirmed verbatim at MDN, so the substantive findings all fall in the single `## Verify` grep command plus a few low-severity accuracy notes). Security response-headers guide. The one-line `curl -q -sI ... | grep -iE 'strict-transport|content-security|...'` check could report success while every intended header was absent or neutralized: the substring alternatives matched a header name anywhere in a line (so `X-Debug: x-frame` or a disabled `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=0` passed), and `content-security` also matched `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`, which enforces nothing and is exactly the rollout state the guide itself recommends first. It was reworked to fetch the final page and read its headers by eye rather than parse them in shell (a shell parser repeatedly mishandled HTTP 1xx Early Hints, trailers, and redirect chains across QA rounds, and cannot certify enforcement anyway): the probe follows redirects (`-L --proto-redir '=https'`, refusing an https-to-http downgrade so you inspect the page a browser lands on, not a 302), disables client proxies (`--noproxy '*'`, since `-I`/HEAD can differ from the GET a browser and the securityheaders.com scan make), dumps every response's headers with `-D -` and prints the effective URL and status with `-w`, and the surrounding prose tells the reader to read the block under the last `HTTP/..` status line and check the VALUES there (a large non-zero HSTS max-age, an enforcing `Content-Security-Policy` and not only `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY` or CSP `frame-ancestors 'none'`, and so on), treating a header missing from the final response or present only on an earlier hop as a finding and a failed fetch as inconclusive. The securityheaders/console-errors step was corrected: no CSP console violations is necessary but not sufficient (a permissive policy also produces none), so the reader now confirms a deliberately disallowed resource is actually blocked while an allowed one loads. Low-severity accuracy fixes: HSTS preload is described as a slow-to-reverse (weeks, browser-dependent) long-lived commitment rather than "effectively irreversible"; the `frame-ancestors` note now gives the concrete `frame-ancestors 'none'` directive as the modern equivalent of `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and notes `default-src` does not cover framing (dropping the "costs nothing" absolutism); the `Clear-Site-Data` logout example is preceded by its scope (`"cookies"` clears cookies across the whole registered domain and HTTP-auth credentials, `"storage"` removes origin storage, IndexedDB, and service workers); and the Sources "MDN HTTP headers reference" link was repointed from the HTTP overview to the actual headers reference. Both families confirmed sound: the HSTS/CSP/X-Content-Type-Options/Referrer-Policy/Permissions-Policy/X-Frame-Options values and the absence of any deprecated header (no `X-XSS-Protection`, `Feature-Policy`, `Expect-CT`, HPKP, or `ALLOW-FROM`), the `Cache-Control: private` vs `no-store` quotes, the `no-cache`/bfcache/`Clear-Site-Data` logout reasoning, and that the original probe had `-q` first, used HTTPS, and carried no `-k`/`--insecure`.
- Hardened `mosquitto.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#188; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX while network-blocked, the two families converging: every configuration option, default, and the 1883/8883 conventions are correct and confirmed at the vendor, so all findings fall in the `## Verify` section plus a version qualification). MQTT broker deployment-exposure guide. The Verify checks could pass while the broker was still open, so they were reworked. The negative "no credentials" control tested only that a subscribe was denied, which an anonymous CONNECT followed by an `acl_file` SUBSCRIBE denial also produces while the broker still accepts anonymous clients; it now requires a connection-level refusal, MQTT 3.1.1 `CONNACK rc 5` ("Connection Refused: not authorised") or MQTT 5 reason 135, printed by `mosquitto_sub -d`, and treats an anonymous CONNECT that only fails the SUBSCRIBE as a finding. The positive control blocked forever on an idle topic (no observable success), so it gained `-W 2` for a bounded, discriminable exit and `-d` to show `CONNACK rc 0`, and the password placeholder replaced the bare `'...'`. The section never tested the plaintext `1883` listener that the guide itself names as the main exposure, and `ss` only reports a local bind, so `ss` is now framed as a listener inventory (noting it needs `sudo` for the process column and is not a firewall or reachability check) paired with an off-host `nc -vz` probe that `1883` is refused. The mutual-TLS interaction is noted: with `require_certificate true`, TLS rejects before the password check, so the password positive control also needs `--cert`/`--key` and the no-client-certificate rejection is a separate test. A note clarifies that `-x` is the MQTT session-expiry option (not a TLS bypass), `--insecure` disables hostname verification, and mosquitto reads a SOCKS proxy from its client config (there is no curl-style `--noproxy`), and that a default client config can silently supply credentials or a proxy so the checks want a clean environment. The intro now qualifies the sane defaults as Mosquitto 2.0 and later, and a note was added that the `conf.d` snippets take effect only if the running broker loads `include_dir` and that a `listener` change is not applied on a reload signal, so the broker must be restarted and its startup log checked. The `mosquitto_sub` and `mosquitto_passwd` manuals were added to Sources. Both families confirmed sound: `per_listener_settings false` applying the global `allow_anonymous false`/`password_file` to the TLS listener, the `mosquitto_passwd -c` create/overwrite semantics, the `cafile`/`certfile`/`keyfile` and `require_certificate` framing, and the absence of any TLS-verification bypass in the original probes.
- Hardened `mlflow.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#187; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with eight blocking findings F1 through F8, and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX while network-blocked, contributing the Verify-discrimination analysis and a `/signup` self-registration question; a third codex worker then arbitrated the seven contested vendor facts at source). The single most consequential correction: MLflow no longer ships a default admin password. The guide said the first start creates `admin`/`password1234`; current MLflow has no default admin password, requires a bootstrap password of at least 12 characters supplied as `MLFLOW_AUTH_ADMIN_PASSWORD` or `admin_password`, and explicitly rejects the legacy `password1234`, so the guide now says that and startup fails without a strong password (an already-bootstrapped server does not need it re-supplied). The password-rotation `curl` and the Verify checks no longer use `password1234`: the rotation example takes the current admin password, the positive control uses the real admin password (the earlier `-u admin` with no colon would have prompted or sent an empty password and never returned 200), and the former "default password gone" check is reframed as a plain wrong-password negative control since there is no default to remove. The claude lens suspected `/signup` allowed unauthenticated self-registration, but source arbitration REFUTED that for current MLflow: user creation (UI `/signup` and the REST route) is admin-gated and fail-closed, so the guide's existing claim was correct and is unchanged. Accuracy corrections from the arbitration: the opening line no longer names `mlflow.server:app` as the default application (the default Uvicorn backend serves `mlflow.server.fastapi_app:app`; the Flask identifier is for the Gunicorn/Waitress backends); "no TLS option of its own" and "no certificate flags" are softened because `mlflow server --uvicorn-opts` can forward `--ssl-keyfile`/`--ssl-certfile` to the default Uvicorn server (a reverse proxy stays the recommended pattern); and the `--allowed-hosts`/`--cors-allowed-origins`/`--disable-security-middleware` flags are qualified as MLflow 3.5.0+ and Uvicorn-only (rejected under `--gunicorn-opts`/`--waitress-opts`), with the note that `--allowed-hosts` validates the request's Host header against a localhost/`0.0.0.0`/private-range pattern list, which is a Host-header check and not a client-source firewall. The Verify section was reworked: every probe disables client proxies (`--noproxy '*'`); because a 401 at the public hostname can come from the fronting proxy rather than MLflow, a backend no-credential check on the tracking server's own address (`http://127.0.0.1:5000`) must also return 401; and because `experiments/search` only returns results the caller may see (testing authentication, not authorization), an authorization check was added that a low-permission user GETting a specific experiment they cannot access must receive 403. Two coverage corrections: rate-limiting must cover the authentication-bearing API routes, not only the login path (basic auth is checked on every protected request); and `--serve-artifacts` proxies only experiments recorded under the proxied store, so experiments created before proxying or with an explicit direct artifact location keep that location and stay reachable independently of the tracking server's permissions, meaning storage IAM must be applied directly. `MLFLOW_TRACKING_TOKEN` gained a caveat that MLflow's own basic-auth credentials take precedence over the bearer and that some proxies (Cloudflare Access service tokens) expect their own client-ID/secret headers. Confirmed correct by the arbitration and left unchanged: the `--host` help quote "This is NOT a security setting" is verbatim, `MLFLOW_FLASK_SERVER_SECRET_KEY` is genuinely required, update-password is `PATCH`, the Docker 28.0 loopback caveat, and the `NO_PERMISSIONS`/`READ` default-permission and SQLite `database_uri` defaults. The reworked backend and authorization Verify checks ship marked reasoned, not demonstrated, with the demonstration debt tracked as backlog row 1.76 (CONTRIBUTING rule 5), because the authoring environment has no live MLflow basic-auth server.
- Hardened `model-servers.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#186; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with nine blocking findings MS-01 through MS-09 and one non-blocking MS-10, and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX while network-blocked, the two families converging independently on every high-severity item). This guide covers self-hosted LLM inference servers (llama.cpp, vLLM, TGI, SGLang, Triton, LM Studio, text-generation-webui). Both families found the same three high-severity defects. The Verify section certified isolation from `ss` plus proxy-edge probes, but `ss` reports only a namespace-local bind (not a host firewall, a cloud security group, or Docker `-p` NAT publication) and the curl probes only ever reached the proxy, so an exposed raw backend port went untested; the block now says so and points to the off-host external probe (the guarded subshell, confirmed sound by both families) for each backend port, and the `ss` listener list gained the text-generation-webui `5000` and `7860` ports it had omitted. The `/invocations` probe sent `-d '{}'`, which curl labels form content and which fails vLLM's JSON schema, so a genuinely exposed unauthenticated route would not return the `200` the guide flagged; it now sends `Content-Type: application/json` with a minimally valid body and treats any vLLM-originated reply (200, 400, 422, or a JSON error) as a reachable no-key route, distinguished from the proxy's own 404/403 denial. The TGI `--api-key` claim that "every inference request" is covered was corrected: the router applies the key to the standard inference `base_routes` only, so builds that enable the KServe (`/v2/...`) endpoints register them outside the key middleware, `/v1/models` sits outside it too, and the Vertex feature route configured through `AIP_PREDICT_ROUTE` is registered after the auth layer as well (the built-in `/vertex` route is inside authenticated `base_routes`), leaving those paths unauthenticated unless the proxy allowlists routes and enforces the bearer check; the first Verify curl is relabelled a proxy-policy test because a `200` on `/v1/models` is not proof the backend enforces the key. Both probes now print `http`/`exit` and disable client proxies (`--noproxy '*'`). The llama.cpp and SGLang launch commands now wrap each key in an inline `${VAR:?set a non-empty ...}` that aborts the launch on an unset or empty value (inline, so there is no temporary variable an inherited `declare -i`/readonly attribute could subvert), and the prose warns that llama.cpp still discards a comma-only or quoted-empty (`""`) CSV key and starts unauthenticated while SGLang serves ordinary requests whenever its api key is empty even with an admin key set, so a real generated key must be used and confirmed with the Verify no-key probe rather than the startup log (the key-loaded message is trace-level and hidden at default verbosity). Triton's "no authentication at all" was softened to "enables no authentication by default", since its native gRPC mutual TLS and shared-secret restricted APIs exist but do not replace the gateway, and a note was added that builds with Vertex AI (`AIP_MODE=PREDICTION`, port `AIP_HTTP_PORT` else 8080) or SageMaker support add conditional listeners beyond the three to disable or inventory. vLLM's native TLS (`--ssl-keyfile`/`--ssl-certfile`/`--ssl-ca-certs`, uvicorn passthrough) is now acknowledged rather than omitted, with the TLS proxy kept as the recommended pattern. A note was added that starting text-generation-webui's API logs the configured `--api-key` (and any distinct `--admin-key`) verbatim, so that output must be protected and the key rotated after disclosure. Both families and the orchestrator confirmed sound the llama.cpp/vLLM/TGI/SGLang/Triton/LM Studio/text-generation-webui defaults, flags, and ports, the vLLM protected-prefix set (`/v1`, `/v2`, `/inference`) and unprotected `/invocations`, the TGI `--prometheus-port` default 9000 and 2026-03-21 archival, and the guarded subshell probe's placeholder guard, curl 7.75.0 write-out variables, and TLS-verification integrity.
- Hardened `nextjs.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#185; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with ten blocking findings N01 through N10 and one non-blocking N11, and claude-opus-4-8 returned SHIP with only low non-blocking items while partly network-blocked, reaching the Next.js v16.3.5 and Vercel documentation but not authjs.dev, better-auth.com, or the Vercel changelog; the two families converged on the Verify probe gaps and the framework authorization defaults, and the orchestrator reconciled the rest at source). Four framework-accuracy fixes: `next.config.js`'s `env` key inlines a variable into the client bundle regardless of prefix, so "every other variable is server-only" is now "server-only BY DEFAULT" with an explicit never-put-a-secret-in-`env` warning; the Better Auth server check `await auth.api.getSession(...)` retrieves and discards a session without rejecting anyone, so it is now assign-and-reject (`const session = await auth.api.getSession(...)` then `if (!session) return new Response(null, { status: 401 })` then authorize); the Auth.js `export { auth as proxy }` with an empty `providers` and no `callbacks.authorized` authorizes every request by default, so the guide now says to add an `authorized` callback or a redirecting wrapper if it is used to gate; and `twoFactor()` makes TOTP, OTP, and backup codes AVAILABLE rather than enrolled or enforced (emailed or SMS OTP additionally needs `otpOptions.sendOTP`, and requiring 2FA for an operation is a separate check). The `trustedOrigins` comment was corrected from "cross-origin requests from unlisted origins are rejected" to its real scope (origin/CSRF and redirect validation that skips GET, HEAD, and OPTIONS and does not gate arbitrary app routes). The session example now signs `role` (`createSession(userId, role)` and `SignJWT({ userId, role, expiresAt })`) so the admin checks that read `session.role` are internally consistent rather than always denying, and a note marks the auth snippets as fragments whose `session.role` must come from a trusted user store. The Verify section was reworked: the protected-route probes disable client proxies (`--noproxy '*'`), print the status and the redirect target instead of piping to `head` (a failed pipeline exits 0 and hides the body), stop pinning a single redirect status (a `redirect()` yields 307 and a Server-Action submit 303), and add a positive control that the same dashboard route WITH a valid session cookie returns 200 so a denial is the auth check firing and not a route broken for everyone; the client-bundle secret scan now searches the FULL secret with a literal `grep -rIF`, guards on the secret being set and the build directory existing, and reads grep's exit status (a missing directory makes grep exit 2 with empty output, which is not a pass); and the Server Action test now does an authorized mutation on a disposable record as a positive control, then replays the same request with no cookie and as an unauthorized user, checking the store directly rather than trusting a blocked form. A codex QA-of-fix of the applied change then found the first-round Verify rework still under-discriminated, and a second round corrected it: the unauthenticated rejection differs by surface (a Route Handler returns a `Response` while a Server Component or Server Action must `redirect()` or throw, since a `Response` is not a valid render or action result), the route probes now inspect the response body rather than the status alone and add an authorized positive control on the admin route itself (authorization, not only authentication), the client-bundle secret scan is `set -e`/`set -u` safe and reads grep's exit status through a `case` rather than an inline `echo` that a clean grep's exit 1 would skip, and the Server Action test recreates a fresh disposable record before each attempt (an authorized mutation changes its own precondition) and requires an authorization refusal rather than a not-found; the Better Auth `trustedOrigins` note was also refined to say the general origin check skips GET, HEAD, and OPTIONS while individual endpoints still validate `callbackURL` on GET. The imprecise "USD 150 per month" figure attributed to Hobby was dropped to "a paid Advanced Deployment Protection add-on" because the current Vercel pricing ties that figure to an existing Pro-team legacy package. Both families confirmed sound the `proxy.ts` rename in Next.js 16, the `next start -H`/`-p` and `0.0.0.0:3000` defaults and the `PORT`-not-in-`.env` rule, the Server Actions direct-POST reachability and the "page-level check does not extend to the Server Actions defined within it" quote, the DAL and `verifySession()` model, the jose `SignJWT`/`jwtVerify` HS256 example and hardened cookie, the `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` build-time inlining and the `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` base64 length and Origin/Host CSRF behavior, and the Vercel Deployment Protection scopes and the free-on-every-plan change. The Auth.js and Better Auth library specifics and the exact Vercel changelog date were confirmed by codex at source where claude's session was network-blocked.
- Hardened `ray.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#184; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX while network-blocked, the two families independently converging on the two most important findings). The Verify section only probed the dashboard `8265` from outside, while the guide's own threat model calls the head port `6379` and the Ray Client port `10001` unauthenticated code-execution endpoints; the external `time_connect` probe now loops over `8265`, `6379`, and `10001` (a non-zero `time_connect` is the finding even when the gRPC port then speaks no HTTP). The token-authentication check was a bare negative (`ray job submit ... must fail: Unauthorized`) whose failure mode was indistinguishable from a down tunnel; it now runs a positive control first (with the token the submit succeeds, proving the endpoint is live) and requires the negative, from a client with no token available, to fail with `401 Unauthorized` specifically, treating a connection error as inconclusive. A gRPC-TLS check was added (run `ray health-check` with the correct `RAY_TLS_CA_CERT` and then a mismatched or absent CA) because the socket checks do not prove the gRPC layer is encrypted. The SSH dashboard forward now binds the local end to loopback explicitly (`ssh -o ExitOnForwardFailure=yes -L 127.0.0.1:8265:127.0.0.1:8265`, since a bare `-L 8265:` follows the client's GatewayPorts). The KubeRay token-retrieval command is quoted (`kubectl get secrets 'REPLACE_WITH_SECRET_NAME' --template='{{.data.auth_token}}'`) so the shell does not read the `<name>` placeholder as input redirection or glob the template braces. Both families and the orchestrator confirmed sound the Ray 2.52.0 token-auth model (`RAY_AUTH_MODE`, `ray get-auth-token`, the `RAY_AUTH_TOKEN` / `RAY_AUTH_TOKEN_PATH` / `~/.ray/auth_token` precedence), the KubeRay v1.6.0 `authOptions`, the `--dashboard-host`/`--dashboard-port 8265`/`--port 6379`/Client `10001` defaults, the Docker loopback-publishing 28.0 caveat, the `RAY_USE_TLS` variables, and the security-page quotes on arbitrary code execution and network isolation.
- Hardened `vault.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#183; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with five Verify and CLI items, and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX while network-blocked, judging the external probe's `time_connect` discriminator and placeholder guard sound and flagging one quotation for confirmation; the orchestrator confirmed at the vendor that the audit-refusal quote "Vault refuses to service the corresponding API request" is verbatim and that `sys/health` and `sys/seal-status` are on the audit-exemption list, so both claude items resolved to no change). The Verify section was hardened: the on-host `ss` note now says it shows the bind, not the firewall, and that "8201 only between nodes" is proven by the external probe rather than the bind; a `VAULT_ADDR`/`VAULT_CACERT` export is set before the CLI checks because the CLI defaults to `https://127.0.0.1:8200` and an inherited `VAULT_ADDR` could target another Vault (with `VAULT_SKIP_VERIFY` kept unset); the audit-correlation `vault token lookup` gains `-format=json` because table output omits the request ID needed to match `request_id` to the log entry's `request.id`; and the external unreachability probe adds that its `time_connect=0` fixed state proves only the tested address is unreachable, not that Vault is, so a mistyped or misrouted address must be ruled out by confirming it is the real external address of the on-host listener. The second audit device note clarifies that `vault audit enable syslog` is independent only when it ships to a separate host or failure domain, not a local syslog agent writing the same disk. The Login MFA reference was added to Sources. Both families confirmed sound the defaults and semantics: the loopback bind and `tls_disable=false` and UI-off defaults, the 8200/8201 ports and `api_addr`/`cluster_addr` advertise-only role, dev-server behaviour, Shamir versus auto-unseal and recovery keys and initialize-once, the fail-closed audit device and HMAC-SHA256 default, the root-token retirement order, and the unauthenticated bootstrap paths.
- Hardened `web-exposure.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#182; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with five blocking Verify and coverage items, and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX while network-blocked, both converging on the deny-rule probes; claude source-verified the config claims). The Verify section now plants a real file at a denied dotfile path so a 403/404 proves the deny RULE fired rather than the file being absent (Caddy's `respond ... 404` returns 404 by design, identical to not-found), disables client proxies with `--noproxy '*'` on every probe, shows the body so leaked content is visible, and notes that a bare 404 on an unplanted path is inconclusive; the uploads-directory autoindex probe says to plant a throwaway file first so a listing would actually reveal something; the ACME positive control is clarified as a webroot-exemption check with a note that ACME HTTP-01 validation itself runs over HTTP on port 80; and the well-known-backup negative test notes that a bare 404 is inconclusive without a planted file. The per-server coverage gaps are stated: nginx's `location ~ /\.` denies dot-prefixed paths only (a non-dotfile export like `dump.sql` needs keeping out of the root or an explicit deny), and the Caddy `respond` list is not exhaustive. The Apache `.well-known` exemption is corrected to describe the whole directory (acme-challenge and other legitimate files such as `security.txt`), not only acme-challenge. The source-map note distinguishes a separate `.map` file, an inline `data:` URI, and a `.map` a `//# sourceMappingURL` comment points to, and notes that removing the comment does not unpublish an already-deployed map.
