Both families confirmed sound: nginx `^~` longest-prefix skipping regex locations, Apache `<FilesMatch>` matching the basename and `<DirectoryMatch>` the unanchored filesystem path, `mod_autoindex` hiding 403 entries unless `ShowForbidden`, the `NEXT_PUBLIC_`/`VITE_`/`REACT_APP_` build-time inlining, and the exit-status-aware secret scan over the built bundle.
- Hardened `container-hardening.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#181; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with six blocking findings, and claude-opus-4-8 returned FIX while network-blocked, agreeing on the read-only write probe and flagging a Pod Security Standards currency question the orchestrator resolved from the raw documentation source: the Restricted profile still does not require `readOnlyRootFilesystem`, so the guide's honest note is correct). The Kubernetes `securityContext` now pins `runAsGroup: 10001` (an unset primary group runs as GID 0), and the guide notes Pod Security Admission rejects violating Pods at admission only for Pods created after the namespace label is set (relabeling a namespace that already runs violators warns rather than evicts them, so recreate those workloads). The NetworkPolicy guarantee was narrowed from "every other path is refused" to the paths the namespace's own policies govern, because NetworkPolicy does not cover host-network or node-local traffic and the DNS rule allows port 53 to every pod in `kube-system` rather than only CoreDNS. The Verify section was hardened: the read-only write probe now demonstrates both states (temporarily remove `read_only`, confirm the same `touch` writes, then restore it and confirm it is blocked), because a `touch` failure on a missing or non-writable path prints the same "blocked" on a writable filesystem; the declared `securityContext` print is paired with a runtime inspection of `id`, `NoNewPrivs`, and `CapEff`; the forbidden NetworkPolicy probe is reframed to state that a `role: other` pod is stopped at its own default-deny egress (which tests the egress side), with a note on isolating the database's ingress filter by granting a non-app label egress to the database IP; the `--overrides` mechanism is corrected from "strategic merge" to the JSON merge patch (`--override-type=merge`) that replaces the containers array; and the Docker checks use `docker compose exec` because `app` is a Compose service name rather than a container name. The `no-new-privileges` equivalence is reattributed to Docker's engine-level `--security-opt`, and the Docker run security-options reference was added to Sources. Both families confirmed sound: the `USER user:group` root-group behaviour, `read_only`/`tmpfs`/`cap_drop`/`no-new-privileges`, never mounting the Docker socket, the securityContext field validity, the additive-NetworkPolicy and enforcing-CNI caveat, and the well-built `probe_override` JSON-quoting and by-IP positive-control-first probe design.
- Hardened `elasticsearch.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#180; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with five blocking Verify items, and claude-opus-4-8 returned SHIP with non-blocking clarity items while network-blocked, noting that codex's plaintext-probe finding is a false-alarm risk rather than a false-secure pass since the exposed case is still caught; both confirmed the guide's central `http.host: 0.0.0.0` auto-configuration claim). The Elasticsearch 8.x auto-security description was qualified: auto-configuration is skipped when startup output is redirected, the config directory is not writable, or security or discovery settings already exist, and Debian and RPM packages do not print the generated password (reset it with `elasticsearch-reset-password -u elastic`), so the guide now says to confirm security actually came up. The Verify section was hardened: the plaintext probe shows the response body (no `-o /dev/null`) and disables client proxies (`--noproxy '*'`), because with HTTP TLS enabled a node closes a plaintext connection without sending an HTTP response (so `http=000` from the reset or empty reply is the pass, the preceding https probe having proved the port is up), and any HTTP status is a finding, including a `401` on an authentication-enabled node still serving plaintext (authentication without transport encryption is still an exposure); the two authenticated probes disable client proxies, show the status, and carry timeouts, and note that OpenSearch uses its own `admin` user and demo CA (`admin` and `root-ca.pem`) rather than Elasticsearch's `elastic` account and `http_ca.crt`; and the `ss` listener check notes that `http.port` defaults to the range `9200-9300` (binding the first free port, source-verified against the networking-settings reference) so the reader must use the node's actual HTTP port and also check the transport listener (default `9300-9400`). The TLS enable setting was corrected from the namespace `xpack.security.http.ssl` to the key `xpack.security.http.ssl.enabled`, and the security-settings reference was added to Sources. Both families and the orchestrator confirmed correct: the OpenSearch `OPENSEARCH_INITIAL_ADMIN_PASSWORD` setting only the `admin` account, the seven demo `internal_users.yml` accounts, `securityadmin.sh` applying changes to the `.opendistro_security` index, and the `-k` warning and the `ss` `sport` filter.
- Hardened `rabbitmq.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#179; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX at audit and across two QA-of-fix rounds, claude-opus-4-8 was network-blocked, and the orchestrator source-verified the definitions-export sensitivity). The prior-hardened mutual-TLS `openssl s_client` pair was confirmed sound by both families and left intact. The Verify section treated `ss` as proof of isolation; it is now a listener inventory (a private-address or `0.0.0.0` bind says nothing about the firewall), and the guide adds external-unreachability probes for epmd `4369`, the inter-node distribution port `25672`, and the management UI `15672`/`15671` from a non-peer host (these are the remote-node-compromise surface, since the Erlang cookie plus reach to `25672` is `rabbitmqctl`), a peer-side positive control against the private distribution interface (no public bind required), and an Erlang-cookie file mode and owner check; the probe block uses the corpus positional-parameter guard so a reader's typed or readonly shell variable cannot bypass it. The guest check now matches the guide's own configuration, which deletes `guest`, by requiring `rabbitmqctl list_users` to show it absent rather than testing the keep-and-restrict localhost message. A note was added that a non-zero exit on the mutual-TLS negative run is only the missing-client-certificate rejection if it is not a connection or server-certificate failure. The prose now distinguishes the server certificate the cross-links cover from the `clientAuth` client certificate mutual TLS also needs, chaining to a CA trusted via `ssl_options.cacertfile`, and warns that exported definitions contain password hashes and hashing metadata (RabbitMQ classifies user records as sensitive) so they stay restricted and out of images and version control; the definitions documentation was added to Sources. Across two QA-of-fix rounds codex found that reworking the probe introduced a split-DNS hostname ambiguity, an overstated firewall attribution, and a dropped management-UI check (all fixed), then that a shell-variable guard could be bypassed by a reader's `declare -i` integer attribute (fixed by adopting the positional-parameter guard, the fix locally reproduced clean).
- Hardened `tunnels.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#178; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX and source-verified every frp, WireGuard, and OpenSSH claim, while claude-opus-4-8 was network-blocked and contributed a reasoning lens; the fix converged over three codex QA rounds, the last returning SHIP). Three content corrections, all source-verified against frp: a strong `auth.token` or OIDC authenticates `frpc` to `frps` but not the callers of a published service (a registered proxy binds on `proxyBindAddr`, default `bindAddr` `0.0.0.0`), so published services need their own authentication or an authenticated TLS proxy; frp's OIDC audience validation is skipped when `auth.oidc.audience` is empty (it sets the verifier's `SkipClientIDCheck`), so a non-empty audience and issuer must be set; and an absent `auth` block is not fail-closed, since frp defaults to the token method with an empty token that a matching empty-token client satisfies. The WireGuard nftables forward-drop rule now states it needs a hooked `forward` base chain (an unreferenced regular chain receives no traffic), `net.ipv4.ip_forward`, and covers only routed IPv4 traffic, not the host itself or IPv6. The Verify section was reworked over the QA rounds: the frp controls are reproducible with a `login to server success` positive control (`frpc` runs in the foreground) and version-agnostic auth-failure wording; the WireGuard listener is confirmed with `sudo wg show wg0` and `ip link show wg0` rather than an over-stated `ss` claim (the in-kernel socket has no owning process, and the interface shows operational `state UNKNOWN`); the firewall-counter check is an explicit, honestly-scoped two-host sequence with a verdict-pinned rule match; and the `ufw` check requires an active default-deny or default-reject policy across both address families. The frp TLS page and the nftables manual were added to Sources.
- Hardened `kubernetes.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#177; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned DO NOT SHIP with one MEDIUM and source-verified every version pin and control-plane quote, while claude-opus-4-8 at high effort with partial network access returned SHIP on the reachable portion, independently confirming the same probe gap and catching a misquote; the two families' source access was complementary, and codex cleared every claim claude could not reach). This 341-line guide is otherwise exceptionally thorough and accurate: both families and the orchestrator confirmed the ingress-nginx March 2026 retirement (the 29 January 2026 statement announces "In March 2026, Kubernetes will retire Ingress NGINX"), Envoy Gateway v1.9.1 (current, latest as of 28 August), cert-manager 1.15+ for `config.gatewayAPI.enabled=true`, the GKE 1.32 read-only-port default, the Gateway API v1 resource shapes, the EKS and GKE API-server public-by-default and DNS-endpoint distinctions, the self-managed control-plane and worker ports, the etcd plaintext-at-rest and root-equivalent-access quotes, the kubelet flag-versus-file default split (`--anonymous-auth` true by flag but disabled by KubeletConfiguration, `--read-only-port` 10255 by flag but 0 by file), the EKS AMI KubeletConfiguration values, the kubeconfig ExecConfig `env` quote, and the Envoy Gateway SHA-only basic-auth, OIDC, and extAuth-Authelia claims. Two fixes: the app-facing Verify probes (`curl -q -sI http://app.example.com/` and the 401 check) honored the reader's proxy environment (the section's later `unset` only affects the commands after it) and had no positive control, so they now disable client proxies with `--noproxy '*'` (matching the section's own API-server probe, which likewise uses no `-g`), show the redirect `Location`, and add a positive control that a valid basic-auth credential read from a mode-0600 netrc file returns 2xx plus a check that the `SecurityPolicy` is Accepted for the route (an application's own login 401 is not that control); and the intro's quotation of Kubernetes documenting Gateway API as "the successor to the Ingress API" was corrected because that exact phrase does not appear on the cited Gateway concept page (the orchestrator verified this against the live page), so it now says Kubernetes recommends Gateway API over Ingress, whose API is frozen, and links a migration guide. Added the curl manual to Sources for the new `--noproxy`/`--netrc-file` flags. The extensive control-plane API-server, `nc`, and `nmap` Verify probe (python3 endpoint parsing, curl exit-code discrimination, control-plane-first scanning, and the IPv6 and NodePort-range caveats) was scrutinized by both families and confirmed sound.
- Hardened `bi-dashboards.md` against a source-verified audit (#176; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned DO NOT SHIP with two HIGH and three MEDIUM and source-verified every tool fact; the second-family claude-opus-4-8 audit was OOM-killed twice on the shared host, so this shipped on codex's full-source audit plus independent orchestrator source-verification of the subtler findings and the codex QA-of-fix re-checking the applied diff, the documented memory-pressure fallback). The Verify HTTP check (`curl -q -sI https://bi.example.com/ # 401/403 or a login redirect, never a dashboard`) was the same unguarded probe the corpus ruled DO NOT SHIP on in admin-uis.md #169: a HEAD reads no body, so a 200 login page and a 200 dashboard are indistinguishable, a redirect can be canonicalization, and client proxies were not disabled; it is now a subshell-guarded block that disables client proxies (`--noproxy '*'`), reads the body of the tool's front page and a known protected route (a positive control run first with valid authorization), and accepts a denial from whatever protects the route (the tool's or the proxy's 401/403, or a Cloudflare Access challenge or redirect into that login) without over-attributing to the tool's own denial, treating a 200 shell, an arbitrary redirect, or a transport error as inconclusive. The `ss -tlnp` note now covers Docker's NAT (a published container port has no matching host listener, so absence from `ss` is not proof of isolation) and directs probing each direct origin and backend port from another host. Three source-verified accuracy corrections: the Superset `AUTH_ROLE_PUBLIC` guidance no longer implies that leaving the variable unset disables anonymous access (Flask-AppBuilder still assigns a Public role; what protects the data is that the Public role grants no data access by default, per Superset's "Data access is still required by default"), and it corrects that `PUBLIC_ROLE_LIKE` copies another role's permissions at `superset init` rather than scoping datasets (a broad role such as `Gamma` grants more, not less); the Redash quotation, previously truncated at "you should enforce HTTPS", now carries the vendor's full requirement "and make sure you set the cookie secret", with the `REDASH_COOKIE_SECRET`/`REDASH_SECRET_KEY` signing secrets (the latter encrypts stored data-source credentials); and the read-only-database advice now says to verify the database principal's effective grants and inherited roles at the database and test that writes are denied, since a role named readonly can still hold write, ownership, or admin privileges, rather than only reading the connection string. Added Docker port-publishing, Redash secret-keys, and Metabase database-privileges Sources. codex confirmed correct and unchanged: the Metabase first-account-admin, public-links/embeds-by-default and embed-URL-recoverable and Pro/Enterprise-only row/column security, the Superset `SECRET_KEY` quote and admin/admin Compose default and not-for-production warning, the Redash first-run-admin quote, and the Metabase 3000 / Superset 8088 / Redash 5000 default ports.
- Hardened `open-webui.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#175; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned DO NOT SHIP with a HIGH plus two MEDIUM and a LOW and source-verified every env-var claim, while claude-opus-4-8 was network-blocked and raised two HIGH suspicions from recall that codex's live checks and an independent orchestrator WebFetch both refuted). codex's HIGH: the `docker run` command omitted the `-v open-webui:/app/backend/data` volume the vendor requires, so replacing the container (an upgrade starts a fresh one) resets the database and, by the guide's own first-account-becomes-admin rule, hands the administrator seat to the next registrant if the public proxy keeps routing; the command now mounts the volume, and the guide says to reuse it and to keep the public route disabled until the admin account exists and signup is confirmed off. The `ss -tlnp` note now accounts for Docker's NAT (an absent host listener does not prove the container port is unreachable) and requires Docker Engine 28.0 or newer for loopback publishing (older engines let a same-L2 host reach a localhost-published port), the confirmed `127.0.0.1:3000->8080/tcp` mapping, and an external-host probe. Tailscale was moved out of the "login in front" grouping: Tailscale Funnel is public and adds no login, so the guide now points to Tailscale Serve (restricted by tailnet policy) or a Cloudflare Tunnel with Access, and says to keep Open WebUI's own authentication on behind Funnel (matching the sibling `tailscale.md`). The Verify HTTPS probe gains `--noproxy '*'`, `-g`, `-sS`, and a shown status so a client proxy cannot answer for the origin, a programmatic signup-state check against `/api/config` is added (confirm the signup feature flag is false), and the manual browser check now requires an actual registration refusal after the admin exists, not merely an absent sign-up button. Both families (codex live, orchestrator-reconfirmed by WebFetch) verified the accuracy core correct and unchanged: `ENABLE_SIGNUP` default `true` and its `ConfigVar` persistence, `DEFAULT_USER_ROLE` default `pending`, `ENABLE_PERSISTENT_CONFIG` default `true`, the `WEBUI_ADMIN_EMAIL`/`WEBUI_ADMIN_PASSWORD` first-launch admin bootstrap, `ENABLE_PASSWORD_AUTH=false` disabling password login for SSO-only operation (claude's recalled `ENABLE_LOGIN_FORM` is a different, UI-only variable), the first-account-becomes-admin rule, the Docker image and `3000:8080` loopback mapping, and all internal links. The claude family's two recall-based HIGH suspicions (that `ENABLE_PASSWORD_AUTH` and the `WEBUI_ADMIN_*` pair were invented) were deliberately not acted on: live source verification refuted both, and acting on recall over source would have broken correct fail-open controls.
- Hardened `mcp-servers.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#174; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned DO NOT SHIP with four Verify defects, claude-opus-4-8 at high effort with network access returned DO NOT SHIP on the stale spec version plus two Verify-rigor gaps, and an independent orchestrator check confirmed the guide pins to a superseded MCP spec revision). The guide targets MCP `2025-11-25` and dates its Sources "checked September 2026", but the current revision is `2026-07-28` (verified at the MCP versioning page): every security control the guide teaches (Origin validation, loopback binding, the OAuth 2.1 / RFC 9728 / PKCE / RFC 8707 / audience-validation / no-token-passthrough rules) carries over unchanged, but 2026-07-28 removes protocol-level sessions and the `MCP-Session-Id` header, removes the standalone GET stream, and replaces the `initialize`-handshake version negotiation with a per-request `_meta` field, an `MCP-Protocol-Version` header, and a mandatory `server/discover` RPC, so the guide now carries an explicit spec-revision note pinning it to 2025-11-25, flagging 2026-07-28 as current with those transport changes, and pointing readers to the current specification (a full re-pin to 2026-07-28 is tracked as a follow-up). The Verify section was hardened: the unauthenticated-initialize, metadata-discovery, and wrong-Origin probes gain `--noproxy '*'` and `-g` so a client `HTTPS_PROXY` cannot answer with its own 401/407/403 in place of the target (the wrong-Origin false-pass was the real risk); the wrong-Origin check now isolates the Origin control with a positive control (an allowed Origin plus a valid initialize body must return a 2xx result) before changing only the Origin to a disallowed value and requiring 403, instead of posting an invalid `{}` body whose 403 could come from insufficient scope; the metadata probe now follows the `resource_metadata` URL the 401 advertises (with the endpoint-specific then root fallback) rather than assuming the root well-known path; the bearer credential moves off the command line into a mode-0600 `-H @` header file per `secrets.md` rule 5 (the same file convention machine-auth.md and python.md use), and the wrong-audience check is marked reasoned per CONTRIBUTING rule 5 with its command shape, prerequisite, and expected outcomes. Both families confirmed the rest correct and unchanged in 2026-07-28: every MCP-spec MUST/SHOULD/OPTIONAL quote is verbatim (the two transports and deprecated HTTP+SSE, the Origin-403 requirement, the OAuth 2.1 resource-server role, RFC 9728 metadata and `authorization_servers`, both discovery mechanisms, PKCE S256 and the `code_challenge_methods_supported` refusal, the RFC 8707 `resource`, audience validation and 401/403, no-token-passthrough and the separate upstream token, and the non-authenticating-sessions rule), the Azure `WEBSITE_AUTH_PRM_DEFAULT_WITH_SCOPES` preview setting, the nginx `if`/`map` Origin blocks (only `return` inside `if`, and the empty-key match for a missing Origin), the guarded loopback LAN probe, and all fourteen internal cross-links. The one divergence (codex flagged the argv bearer token, claude called it house style) was resolved at source in codex's favor: secrets.md rule 5 forbids secrets on the command line, and machine-auth.md:58, paas.md:38, and python.md:99 already use the `-H @file` convention.
- Hardened `vector-databases.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#173; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned DO NOT SHIP on a single LOW Verify item and source-verified every version and default claim, while claude-opus-4-8 was network-blocked this session, ran a clean offline internal-consistency and Verify-reasoning review with no defect, and honestly marked the external claims NOT-LIVE-VERIFIED rather than dressing recall as verification; the two families agreed on the one actionable finding, and codex's live checks resolved every claim claude could not reach, including claude's highest-risk "possibly invented `alt_api_key`", which codex confirmed exists as of Qdrant v1.17.0). The Weaviate and Chroma quick-checks (`curl -q -si https://... | head -1`) did not disable client proxies, so with an HTTPS forward proxy configured the `head -1` line could show the proxy's `200 Connection established` CONNECT response instead of the service's status, and they lacked the status write-out and positive control the guide's own guarded Qdrant blocks already use; they now disable client proxies with `--noproxy '*'`, print `http=%{http_code}`/`exit=%{exitcode}` instead of piping to `head -1`, add `-g`, and add a valid-key positive control read from a file, so the checks cannot pass merely because nothing answered (the denial is accepted from Weaviate or the fronting proxy, not over-attributed to one layer). Both families confirmed the rest correct: the curl exit-code discrimination (7 connect-fail, 28 timeout, 6 DNS, 3 malformed, 52 empty reply, 56 receive failure) and the `http=000` ambiguity paired with the exit code, the plaintext-probe-to-a-TLS-only-port reasoning (the TCP handshake completes regardless of TLS), that the client-API key check cannot catch an exposed internal 6335 (6333 answers 401 then 200 identically either way), the single-node-with-cluster-enabled 6335 caveat, and the pgvector row-level-security positive control (superuser/`BYPASSRLS`/owner-unless-`FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, with the top-k caveat). codex additionally source-verified the version-gated claims that are the guide's accuracy core: Qdrant `enforce_internal_auth` (added v1.18.0, off by default, per the v1.18.0 release notes) and `alt_api_key` (available v1.17.0, per the security page), Weaviate anonymous-access default `true` and RBAC generally available from v1.29, the Milvus standalone compose publishing the bundled MinIO `9000`/`9001` on every interface (tagged compose source) and the default `root`/`Milvus`, the Chroma v1.0.0 removal of built-in authentication, pgvector on PostgreSQL 13 and later, and that every Sources URL resolves.
- Hardened `nodejs.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#172; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned DO NOT SHIP with nine findings, and claude-opus-4-8 at high effort returned DO NOT SHIP with a subset of four, the two families agreeing on every substantive item). The guide told readers to "bind the Node app to `127.0.0.1`" and its `ss` Verify note expected loopback only, with no managed-platform case, so a reader deploying to Render, Railway, Fly, or Heroku produced an app the platform ingress cannot reach and might then bolt on self-TLS that `paas.md` forbids; mirroring the sibling `python.md` #171 fix, the intro and section 1 now carve out platform-terminated ingress (bind `0.0.0.0` on `$PORT` for Render, Railway, and Heroku Cedar; `::` on `$PORT` for Heroku Fir; `0.0.0.0` matching `internal_port` for Fly), note that the section-1 all-interface `listen(443)`/`listen(80)` is intended only for the Node-terminates-TLS layout, add the `paas.md` link, and the `ss` note references the platform address and port, with application authentication still required. The Verify authentication probe (`curl -q -s https://example.com/api # expect 401/403`) printed the body not the status, used a placeholder path that on most apps 404s (or passes while the real data route is open), disabled no client proxy, and had no positive control; it now disables client proxies with `--noproxy '*'`, shows the status, targets a real protected path (single-quoted), accepts a denial from whatever layer protects the route rather than over-claiming the app's own, and adds a valid-session positive control read from a cookie file (not argv) so the check cannot pass while auth is simply broken for everyone. All four Verify `curl` probes gain `--noproxy '*'`, since `-q` alone suppresses only `.curlrc` and not the `http_proxy`/`HTTPS_PROXY` environment proxies that would otherwise answer for the target and corrupt attribution; the `/whoami` forwarded-header probe also notes an overwriting proxy can mask a too-broad `trust proxy`, so a clean result there does not by itself prove the trust boundary. The port-80 redirect reflected the client-controlled `req.headers.host` into its `Location`, an open-redirect and cache-poisoning vector where a cache sits in front of `:80`; it now redirects to a fixed canonical host from the environment. The rate-limit recipe recommended express-rate-limit v7, which allowed an IPv6 client to bypass a per-IP login limit by rotating addresses within its ISP-assigned subnet; the orchestrator source-verified against the v8.0.0 changelog (claude could not reach that host) that v8 masks each IPv6 client to a `/56` subnet by default and closes that bypass, so the guide now recommends v8 or newer and notes an IP limit alone does not stop credential-stuffing from many addresses (pair it with per-account controls). The password-hashing lead framed bcrypt as primary with argon2 an "equivalent alternative", contradicting `authentication.md` rule 4 ("prefer argon2id; ... bcrypt only for legacy systems"); it now prefers `argon2id` for new applications and keeps the bcrypt example for existing systems. The client-TLS rule said both `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0` and `rejectUnauthorized: false` "disable certificate validation for every connection"; corrected to distinguish the process-wide environment variable from the per-connection option (which also affects any connection sharing an agent configured with it). As an entailed cross-guide fix, `nginx.md`'s two `location /` blocks set `Host`, `X-Forwarded-For`, and `X-Forwarded-Proto` but not `X-Forwarded-Host`, which nginx forwards from the client by default (`proxy_pass_request_headers on`, source-verified) and which Express derives `req.hostname` from under `trust proxy` (the behind-proxies doc, claude LIVE-VERIFIED, requires the last trusted proxy to overwrite `X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Forwarded-Host`, and `X-Forwarded-Proto`); both blocks now overwrite it with `$host`. Both families confirmed correct and unchanged: the `trust proxy` semantics and the "never `true`" and hop-count cautions (claude LIVE-VERIFIED against expressjs.com behind-proxies), every `express-session` option and the MemoryStore/no-default-maxAge notes (claude LIVE-VERIFIED against the session middleware doc), the `helmet()` HSTS default (codex verified `max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains`), `https.createServer(...).listen(443)`, the `bcrypt.hash`/`bcrypt.compare`, `crypto.timingSafeEqual`, otplib, and qrcode APIs, the `limit` rate-limit option name (current, `max` still supported), and all internal cross-links resolve. (Two codex-only low items were assessed and dropped as non-defects: the `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` setuid/file-capability caveat could not be live-verified this session and the current text is not wrong, and the `CAP_NET_BIND_SERVICE` privileged-port statement holds at the default `ip_unprivileged_port_start` threshold.)
- Hardened `python.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#171; codex gpt-6-astra full-source and claude-opus-4-8 with partial network access both returned DO NOT SHIP). The `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER` caveat only said the proxy must "set" the header, but Django requires the proxy to STRIP any client-supplied `X-Forwarded-Proto` from every request and set its own from the real connection scheme; otherwise a client can spoof it and make Django treat plain HTTP as HTTPS. The `0.0.0.0`-bind rule ("only when the process itself terminates TLS") wrongly branded the mandated managed-platform configuration as misconfigured and contradicted the sibling `paas.md`: on Render, Fly, Railway, and Heroku the app must bind the platform-required address on the injected port (0.0.0.0, or :: on Heroku Fir) while the platform terminates TLS at its ingress (a finding pre-flagged during the paas.md #166 audit and confirmed by both families against the platform docs), so the rule now carves out platform-terminated ingress, the intro and the `ss` Verify note reference it, and the `$PORT` premise is refined (Fly matches its `internal_port`). The Django HSTS example set `SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS = True` with a one-year `max-age`, committing every subdomain to HTTPS-only before they are confirmed HTTPS-ready and irreversibly for the duration; it now starts with a small `max-age` and `includeSubDomains` off, to be raised after HTTPS is validated (the same footgun fixed in apache.md #168). The Verify authentication probe (`curl -s https://example.com/api # expect 401/403`) printed the body not the status, did not disable client proxies, used a generic path, and had no positive control; it now disables client proxies with `--noproxy '*'`, shows the status, targets a real protected path, requires a rejection without credentials and acceptance with a valid one (noting that a reverse proxy in front can enforce it too, so the layer is confirmed by probing the app directly or in its logs), and adds a valid-credential positive control read from a file. The claim that the `fastapi.security` classes "validate nothing" is corrected: they check the credential's presence and scheme or format but do not authenticate it (the `OpenIdConnect`-stub warning stays). Both families confirmed correct: the Flask/Werkzeug `adhoc` dev-only note, the Gunicorn and Uvicorn TLS flags, the Django setting names and `check --deploy`, the argon2 API, `secrets.token_urlsafe`, the `REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE`/`SSL_CERT_FILE` client-CA variables (including httpx honoring `SSL_CERT_FILE`), and the `http.server` all-interfaces/symlink/not-for-production warning; codex verified every external link resolves.
- Hardened `object-storage.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#170; codex gpt-6-astra had full access to all five providers' docs and returned DO NOT SHIP, while claude-opus-4-8 could reach only the AWS and Azure hosts and returned SHIP, so codex is the authority for the R2, GCS, and Supabase claims). The IAM Access Analyzer note over-promised: it lists public buckets only in a Region that has an account-level external-access analyzer, its findings update asynchronously, and archived findings drop from the active view, so an empty list is not proof; the guide now says to create an analyzer per bucket Region, review archived findings, and confirm Block Public Access directly. The Verify anonymous probe now runs the authorized signed GET first as a positive control (S3 returns 403 for an object you cannot list, so an isolated anonymous 403 could just mean the object is missing), disables client proxies with `--noproxy '*'` on every probe, notes that one object does not establish bucket-wide privacy, and treats a DNS/TLS/proxy error as inconclusive. The `gcloud storage sign-url` note is corrected: signing needs a service account (via `--impersonate-service-account` or an activated key), not ordinary `gcloud auth login` user credentials, with the 12-hour limit for system-managed signing and 7 days for a `--private-key-file`. The GCS public-access-prevention Verify now accepts `inherited` when a `storage.publicAccessPrevention` org policy enforces it (it previously demanded `enforced`, contradicting the guide's own line about org/folder/project enforcement). The R2 token note now distinguishes `Admin Read & Write` (can create and delete buckets) from `Admin Read only` (cannot), and the Azure anonymous-denial code is clarified as `401` for modern clients and `409` for legacy clients predating the bearer challenge. Both families confirmed correct: the S3 Object Ownership default and Block Public Access settings, the presign default/maximum, the R2 defaults and SigV4 limits, the GCS uniform-access 90-day irreversibility and PAP `412`, the gcloud update boolean flags, the Azure account defaults and SAS guidance, and the Supabase private-by-default RLS and the `owner_id` policy column.
- Hardened `admin-uis.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#169; codex gpt-6-astra full-source found a HIGH plus three MEDIUM, and claude-opus-4-8, network-blocked, independently confirmed the HIGH against the corpus's own precedent). The Verify HTTP check (`curl -sI ... # 401/403 or a login redirect, never a dashboard`) could not establish that a dashboard is protected: a HEAD reads no body, so a 200 login page and a 200 dashboard are indistinguishable, a generic redirect could be canonicalization rather than authentication, and client proxies were not disabled (a proxy's own redirect reads as the panel's login gate). This is the same class the corpus ruled DO NOT SHIP on in paas.md #166. The check now disables client proxies (`--noproxy '*'`), makes an unauthenticated request that must draw a denial from whatever protects the panel (its own 401/403, the reverse proxy's, or a Cloudflare Access challenge) or a redirect into that layer's login, reads the body (a login page and a dashboard can both be 200, and an arbitrary redirect is inconclusive), and requests a known protected route (positive-controlled, a 404 or error being inconclusive) to catch SPA panels that return 200 for both the login page and the dashboard. The concrete Grafana and Prometheus recipes bound only TLS and passwords, leaving both on their default all-interface listeners despite the guide's own loopback rule; Grafana now sets `http_addr = 127.0.0.1` and Prometheus starts with `--web.listen-address=127.0.0.1:9090`. The mongo-express enable-variable claim was corrected: it is not a clean "1.0.x reads `ME_CONFIG_BASICAUTH`, 1.1.0 reads `ME_CONFIG_BASICAUTH_ENABLED`" boundary (codex source-verified that v1.0.0 keys the login off a non-empty username and that `ME_CONFIG_BASICAUTH_ENABLED` already exists in v1.1.0-rc-4), so the guide now says to set both flags, use non-empty credentials, and verify the login actually appears rather than assert the boundary. The `ss` note now accounts for Docker-published and NAT-only ports and points to the external probe as authoritative. Both families confirmed correct: the mongo-express variable names and `admin`/`pass` defaults, the Grafana first-login and HTTPS keys and anonymous-off default, the Prometheus `basic_auth_users`/`tls_server_config`/`promtool` config, the bcrypt-cost comment (unchanged from #163; `$2y$` hashes validate against a `$2b$` placeholder), and the Adminer and phpMyAdmin/pgAdmin advice. (Out of scope, recorded for follow-up: `bi-dashboards.md:49` carries the same unguarded `-sI ... never a dashboard` probe, and a `run_all_checks.sh` gate could flag external panel/app Verify curls that lack `--noproxy '*'` or an "any other 3xx is not a pass" qualifier.)
- Hardened `apache.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#168; codex gpt-6-astra full-source found six MEDIUM issues, and claude-opus-4-8, after several OOM retries on the shared host, completed and concurred on the ship-blocking mutual-TLS finding). The mutual-TLS block carried no placement guidance, so a reader adding `SSLVerifyClient require` to the human-facing vhost would reject every browser user during the TLS handshake before basic auth applied; the guide now says to put mTLS in a separate machine-client vhost and keeps `SSLCACertificateFile` at server/vhost level. The `SSLProtocol` floor is set inside the vhost, but a per-vhost `SSLProtocol` takes effect independently only on Apache 2.4.42+ (with OpenSSL 1.1.1+ and SNI); on older builds the base or first-listed vhost decides, so the guide now says to set the same floor in the base config. The `certbot --apache` note no longer claims it performs steps 1 to 3 (it installs the certificate and usually the redirect, but `--hsts` is off by default, which also reconciles the guide's own HSTS warning). The HSTS example drops `includeSubDomains` from the default and says to add it only once every subdomain serves valid HTTPS, because it forces them all onto HTTPS and blocks any HTTP-only one. The htpasswd path is annotated as Debian/Ubuntu with the RHEL/Fedora `/etc/httpd/conf/.htpasswd` equivalent (the RHEL note on the `AuthUserFile` line is placed on its own comment line, since Apache config has no inline comments). The three Verify HTTP probes gain `--noproxy '*'` and `-sS` so a client proxy cannot answer for the server and transport errors stay visible. codex confirmed correct: the TLSv1.3 version gate, the `SSLCertificateChainFile`-deprecated-since-2.4.8 claim, the bcrypt cost, the directive names, the package/module commands, the still-current "Authelia does not support Apache" claim, and the mod_auth_openidc OIDC path.
- Hardened `deployment-lifecycle.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#167; both codex gpt-6-astra and claude-opus-4-8 returned DO NOT SHIP, agreeing on the stale Vercel hedge). Section 3 carried a hedge that "the configuration reference page itself still listed All Deployments as Pro and Enterprise only", which the live Vercel Deployment Protection reference now contradicts ("Vercel Authentication for All Deployments... do not require a paid add-on") and which disagreed with the sibling `paas.md`; a Hobby-plan reader could have read it as needing a paid upgrade to protect the production domain and left it open, so the hedge is removed and the guide now matches the live docs, with the 9 September 2026 changelog added to Sources. The teardown step now keeps the released resource reserved until the record's old TTL drains and removal is confirmed at the authoritative nameservers, because deleting a record does not flush answers already cached by resolvers (a released IP is still reachable by cached clients during the TTL window). The retired-preview Verify was tightened: a `curl` connection/DNS/TLS failure is not proof the record is gone (a dangling record to a released target fails the same way), so the `dig` check now queries A, AAAA, and CNAME rather than only the default A (and, because a plain `dig` only reflects the configured resolver, the guide says to confirm removal against the authoritative nameservers) (an AAAA-only dangling record was invisible to the old check, contradicting section 1's IPv6 requirement), treating SERVFAIL/timeout as inconclusive. The external `nmap -p-` sweeps are annotated as TCP only, with a pointer to probe UDP services separately (`nmap -sU`). Both families confirmed the rest sound: the safe-initialization order, the setup-wizard re-check, the DNS-before-app teardown order, the offboarding layers, the guarded IPv4/IPv6 nmap probes (including that the IPv6 placeholder is caught), the `nc`/`dig`/`nmap` flags, and the identity-verifying external certificate-expiry `openssl s_client` pipeline.
- Hardened `paas.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#166; both codex gpt-6-astra and claude-opus-4-8 returned DO NOT SHIP on the same HIGH Verify gap, with all dated vendor claims confirmed current). The Verify check `curl … /api/… # 401/403 without credentials` did not distinguish the application's own authentication from the platform's deployment-protection gate: Vercel Authentication (and similar gates) returns 401 before the app is reached, so the check passed even when the endpoint had no application auth, and once the reader disables deployment protection to serve the public the endpoint is open, exactly the failure the guide's section 1 warns about. The Verify section now disables client proxies (`--noproxy '*'`) and prints the status like the rest of the corpus, tests the app's own rejection with deployment protection disabled (or a Protection Bypass token) plus a positive control that an authorized identity succeeds, frames the HTTP->HTTPS redirect as a spot-check that does not prove app-wide HTTPS or proxy-awareness, and scans the deployed browser bundle (not just the repository) for every credential type. Section 4 now says managed databases must also be network-restricted (many, including Render's Postgres, are reachable from any IP by default, so TLS alone is not enough) and that certificate verification support varies by endpoint (Render's internal connections do not support `sslmode=verify-full`, so set `sslmode=require` to force encryption there and do not treat it as identity-verified). The Password Protection note now gives the per-plan split (unavailable on Hobby, paid on Pro, included on Enterprise), and the Hugging Face "public by default" line is qualified because the default depends on the creation path and organization policy. Both families confirmed the current vendor facts: Vercel Authentication for All Deployments is free on every plan including Hobby (the 9 September 2026 changelog is live), the HF visibility tiers, the Static-Space `window.huggingface.variables` secret exposure, Dev Mode's SSH/VS Code endpoint, the `NEXT_PUBLIC_` client inlining, the Express/Django proxy settings, and the links. (Out of scope, recorded for python.md's own audit: python.md:31 allows binding `0.0.0.0` only when the process terminates TLS, which needs a platform-ingress exception since PaaS platforms terminate TLS at their edge.)
- Hardened `mongodb.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#165; codex gpt-6-astra returned DO NOT SHIP on the Verify section, claude-opus-4-8 returned SHIP with the same items as LOW). The Verify section was entirely negative, so a completely unreachable or misconfigured deployment passed every check and read as "hardened"; it now adds an authenticated positive control (an `admin` connection over TLS must succeed and list databases), reframes the plaintext probe (the signal that matters is a plaintext connection that succeeds, since a bare refusal also occurs when the host is simply unreachable), makes the checks non-interactive with `--eval`, and requires an attributable MongoDB authorization error rather than any failure. The admin-bootstrap sequence now authenticates the shell (`db.getSiblingDB("admin").auth(...)`) after the localhost exception ends and before creating application users, which the exception does not do for you (verified at the MongoDB SCRAM setup procedure). The concatenated server PEM step now sets ownership to the account `mongod` runs as (`mongodb` on Debian/Ubuntu packages, `mongod` on RPM) alongside `chmod 600`, so a reader whose `mongod` cannot read a root-owned key fixes ownership rather than loosening the key to world-readable. The driver note no longer implies every driver accepts `tlsCAFile` in the connection string (the option "is not supported by all drivers"). Both families confirmed the rest correct, including the `net.tls.CAFile` / `allowConnectionsWithoutCertificates` client-certificate semantics the brief flagged (client certs are required by default and `allowConnectionsWithoutCertificates: true` permits SCRAM-over-TLS without one while still validating any presented certificate), the `bindIp` localhost and `authorization` disabled defaults, the SCRAM-SHA-256 default (since 4.0), the localhost exception, `requireTLS`, and the links.
- Hardened `minio.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#164; both codex gpt-6-astra with full doc access and claude-opus-4-8 returned DO NOT SHIP on the same HIGH wrong-default). The guide said the S3 API is on "loopback or private networks by default", but MinIO's `--address` defaults to `:9000` on every interface, so a default install is internet-reachable while the guide's own next sentence warns that a host-less bind exposes all interfaces; the guide now states the all-interface default and gives the explicit `--address 127.0.0.1:9000` bind (verified at the AIStor server reference). The console Verify probe hard-coded `:9001`, but an unset `--console-address` picks a random port, so the probe passed while an exposed console sat on the real port; it now probes the actual console port from `ss`/the startup log, treats any response as reachable, and states a refusal or TLS error is not proof of isolation. The anonymous-access checks proved denial only for the tested GET, so the guide now inspects each bucket's full policy with `mc anonymous get-json` (which shows the upload and prefix grants that the GET probes cannot audit). The `mc alias set` note is corrected to say it performs a signed probe that does not by itself prove the keys are valid (a fronting proxy's 403 can satisfy it), so the app's required operations must be tested against MinIO's own endpoint. The `minioadmin`/`minioadmin` warning notes it is still the built-in default when the root environment variables are unset, and the console-source citation now points at `cmd/common-main.go`, where the host-less binding is implemented. Both families confirmed the rest correct: the lifecycle claim (the community repo was archived 2026-04-25 and is unmaintained, with AIStor Free and AIStor Enterprise succeeding it), the `MINIO_ROOT_USER`/`MINIO_ROOT_PASSWORD` names, the `public.crt`/`private.key` auto-HTTPS in `~/.minio/certs`, the console flag semantics, and the links.
- Swept the corpus for basic-auth password hashes generated at bcrypt cost 5 and raised them to cost 12 (#163). `apache.md` and `nginx.md` created their htpasswd files with bare `htpasswd -B` (bcrypt default cost 5, below the OWASP minimum of 10 that authentication.md sets), and `admin-uis.md`'s comment and `devops-uis.md`'s registry-auth prose showed the same bare `-B`; all now specify `-C 12`, matching the fixes already made to traefik.md, caddy.md, and ollama.md. `secrets.md`'s `htpasswd -cbs` example is deliberately left: it uses the `-b` argv flag as an illustration of a secret exposed on the command line, not a bcrypt-cost recommendation.
- Hardened `ollama.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#162; codex gpt-6-astra with full doc access returned DO NOT SHIP with six findings, claude-opus-4-8 was network-blocked and flagged the ship-blocking hash issue offline against the repo's own standard). The nginx `auth_basic` example generated its hash with bare `htpasswd -B` (bcrypt cost 5, below the OWASP minimum of 10 that authentication.md sets); it now specifies `htpasswd -B -C 12`, matching traefik.md and caddy.md. The Caddy example's `reverse_proxy` and the Cloudflare Tunnel route did not override the upstream `Host`, so Ollama's loopback host check would reject the authenticated request with 403; the Caddy block now sets `header_up Host localhost:11434` and the tunnel note sets `originRequest.httpHostHeader` (the nginx block already did the equivalent). The Docker loopback-publish advice (`-p 127.0.0.1:11434:11434`) now notes that a localhost-published port is only isolated on Docker Engine 28.0 and later; before 28.0 hosts on the same layer-2 segment can still reach it (verified at the Docker port-publishing docs). The proxy examples authenticated but did not rate-limit, so the guide now requires attempt throttling plus concurrency and request-time caps before publication and names the denial-of-wallet risk that authentication.md rule 9 calls out for exposed inference endpoints. The bearer-token `if` variant is now marked a convenience whose `!=` is not constant-time and whose token lives in the config, pointing readers to `auth_basic` or an Access service token. The Verify section now prints the HTTP status (rather than only the body), adds a wrong-credentials negative control (expect 401, never 200), and states that an absent host listener under Docker is inconclusive because a NAT-published port need not appear in `ss`. Both families confirmed the correct claims: the `OLLAMA_HOST` loopback default and the `0.0.0.0` exposure, the Docker image's `ENV OLLAMA_HOST=0.0.0.0:11434`, bare bridge `-p` publishing to all host addresses, `--network host` discarding `-p`, the guarded external probe's refusal to run on an unsubstituted placeholder, that a 401 on the unauthenticated `/api/tags` comes from the proxy (that endpoint does not authenticate callers in Ollama), the `basic_auth` Caddy directive name, and the curl write-out version.
- Hardened `mysql.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#161; codex gpt-6-astra with full MySQL and MariaDB doc access returned DO NOT SHIP, claude-opus-4-8 returned SHIP with one source-verified LOW). The Verify section confirmed the server configuration and per-account `REQUIRE` settings but never exercised the guide's core control end to end, so it now adds a client positive control (an `--ssl-mode=VERIFY_IDENTITY` connection to the deployment hostname must succeed) and a negative control (an `--ssl-mode=DISABLED` cleartext attempt must be rejected, not time out or wait at a password prompt: the `REQUIRE SSL` account rejects it with ERROR 1045, evaluated before the global check, and `require_secure_transport` independently refuses cleartext for any account with ERROR 3159), and the `\s` note now says it reports this session's cipher only, not server-wide enforcement or identity. Because MySQL's auto-generated server certificate does not carry the deployment hostname, the guide now states that the `VERIFY_IDENTITY` client needs a certificate covering the hostname clients use (verified at the MySQL client-options and automatic-certificate-generation docs). The X Protocol default-on exposure is no longer framed as 8.4-only: the X Plugin is enabled by default with `mysqlx_bind_address` = `*` on MySQL 8.0 as well (verified at the 8.0 X Plugin options doc). Added a conditional note that on MariaDB using systemd socket activation the TCP listener is defined by the `mariadb.socket` unit rather than `bind_address`. Both families confirmed the defaults and version claims correct: the `require_secure_transport` semantics (Unix-socket connections exempt), the `caching_sha2_password` default, the multi-factor FIDO/WebAuthn chronology and Enterprise-only server plugins, and the MariaDB divergences (no X Protocol, `require_secure_transport` since 10.5.2, the client's `--ssl-verify-server-cert`).
- Hardened `redis.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#160; both codex gpt-6-astra and claude-opus-4-8 flagged the protected-mode gap, and codex additionally found the ACL and version issues with deep source inspection). The least-privilege ACL example (`~app:* +@read +@write`) was not least-privilege: key patterns do not constrain commands that take no key argument, so `+@write` still permitted keyless commands like `FLUSHALL` (clears every database), `FLUSHDB` (the current database), `SWAPDB`, and the Redis 7 `FUNCTION` write subcommands (`LOAD`/`DELETE`/`FLUSH`/`RESTORE`), none constrained by `~app:*`; the guide now removes the destructive flushes explicitly and, because command categories still grant keyless and keyspace-discovery commands (`KEYS`/`SCAN`/`RANDOMKEY` in `@read`), points readers to a per-command allowlist (`~app:* +get +set`) for real least privilege (verified at the Redis ACL and command docs). The `protected-mode` description omitted that on Redis 6.x it only restricts to loopback when there is no explicit `bind` as well as no password, so an explicit `bind 0.0.0.0` with no password is fully exposed (7.0 drops the no-bind condition); the text now states both preconditions. The ACL alternative now makes securing the `nopass` `default` user (`user default off resetpass` or a password) a required step before remote exposure rather than a deferred one, and notes already-authenticated connections persist. The `bind 127.0.0.1 -::1` example is annotated that the `-` optional-address prefix requires Redis 6.2 (6.0 needs plain `bind 127.0.0.1`). The TLS block notes that `tls-auth-clients` defaults to `yes` and the guide's `no` is a deliberate downgrade, and the client/Verify commands now supply `--cert`/`--key` for the mTLS path and no longer imply `NOAUTH` without them. The plaintext Verify probe now treats any RESP reply as proof the port is open and a DNS/timeout failure as inconclusive rather than a pass. Both families confirmed the directives, the empty `requirepass` default, the PING-not-in-@read/@write claim, and the TLS/port-0 config as correct.
- Hardened `traefik.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#159; codex gpt-6-astra with full doc access found the issues, claude-opus-4-8 was network-blocked and SHIPped on recall). The basicAuth example used `htpasswd -nB` (bcrypt cost 5, below the OWASP minimum of 10 that authentication.md sets); it now uses `-nB -C 12`, and both illustrative hash prefixes move to cost 12. The middleware order put `app-auth` first, so basicAuth's 401 short-circuited before the rate and in-flight limiters, leaving failed-login attempts unbounded (contradicting authentication.md's rate-limit-auth rule); the order is now `app-rate,app-inflight,app-auth,app-body`, with the rationale and the Verify comment updated to match. The Verify section no longer treats a `401` as proof that Traefik's basicAuth is attached (the app's own login can also return 401): it confirms routing, and the guide now points to the router's `middlewares` for component attribution. The file-provider basicAuth alternative now says to enable it with `providers.file.directory` and to reference a file-defined middleware from a Docker router as `app-auth@file`. The unlabelled-canary checks carry an explicit "reasoned from documentation, not demonstrated against a live deployment here" qualifier. Both families confirmed the defaults (`exposedByDefault=true`, `api.insecure=false`, the ACME production CA, the v3 Swarm-provider split) and that the guide has no `insecureSkipVerify`, staging-CA, forgeable-forwardAuth-header, or untrusted-XFF defect; the legacy middleware doc URLs redirect to the current references (not broken).
- Hardened `firebase-supabase.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#158; codex gpt-6-astra found the issues, claude-opus-4-8 SHIPped). Two HIGH fixes: the PostgreSQL 14 view fallback revoked only `select`, but a simple view is automatically updatable and its DML runs with the owner's privileges, so `insert`/`update`/`delete` could still write through it and bypass the table's RLS while the SELECT-only check passed; it now revokes ALL privileges and confirms every privilege is gone (verified at the PostgreSQL 14 CREATE VIEW docs). And "New projects start in locked mode" was corrected: creating a Firestore database offers a test mode that lets anyone read and overwrite the data (about 30 days), and Storage/RTDB have their own starting rules, so the guide now tells readers to inspect the deployed rules per instance rather than assume a locked default (verified at the Firestore quickstart). The bundle leak scan switches to `grep -rlIE` (filenames only, so a key is never printed to a shared log; inspect flagged files privately), matching secrets.md and Supabase's "don't log a key." The Verify section seeds known A/B-owned fixtures and requires an authorized owner request to succeed, so an empty RLS-filtered result is not mistaken for enforcement, and it now tests cross-user writes too. Noted that a no-RLS table is reachable by `anon` through its default grants (grants and RLS are separate checks). Both families confirmed the key-name (anon/service_role and the 2025 publishable/secret rename), RLS-default, `security_invoker`, `aal2`-policy, and SECURITY DEFINER claims as correct.
- Tightened Verify discrimination and a broker-access contradiction in `host.md` against a two-family audit (#157; codex gpt-6-astra found the Verify gaps, claude-opus-4-8 SHIPped with a version-attribution nit, both source-verified). The firewall checks no longer accept an unrestricted SSH allowance: removing the firewalld `ssh` service leaves a separate `22/tcp` or broad source rule open, and a broad ufw "from <range> to any" allow admits SSH without a port-22 rule, so the guide now has readers check every effective allowance (ports, source rules, zone target). The brokered-access line is corrected to match cloud-firewalls.md: only agentless SSM and a tailnet need no inbound rule, while GCP IAP (`35.235.240.0/20`) and Azure Bastion (`AzureBastionSubnet`) need a scoped allow. The listener census becomes `ss -tulnp` (TCP and UDP); the external `nc` probe now says a refusal is not proof the host firewall denied it (a host with no sshd refuses too); the password check requires that no password prompt is offered (a keys-only server denies bad credentials too); and the MFA check requires rejecting an omitted/invalid factor and closing the `nullok`/`failmode=safe` bypasses, not merely seeing a prompt. The key-access prerequisite now confirms a non-root sudo-capable admin before root login is disabled, and the OpenSSH `Include` note is corrected (the directive dates to 7.3; the default drop-in became standard around 8.2). Both families confirmed the SSH directives, defaults, drop-in precedence, and firewall syntax as correct.
- Hardened `postgresql.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#156; both codex gpt-6-astra and claude-opus-4-8 verified the defaults and `pg_hba.conf`/`sslmode` semantics as correct, and codex found the Verify-discrimination gaps). The load-bearing fix closes a TLS-verification bypass: libpq prefers GSS encryption over TLS regardless of `sslmode` where a Kerberos/GSSAPI environment exists, so a `verify-full` connection could be satisfied by GSS and skip the certificate check; every `sslmode=verify-full` example and Verify probe now sets `gssencmode=disable` (verified at the libpq connection-parameters docs). The Verify section gains a runnable plaintext probe (`sslmode=disable gssencmode=disable`) that must be refused by `pg_hba.conf` rather than time out, a SCRAM-verifier check (`pg_authid.rolpassword LIKE 'SCRAM-SHA-256%'`) because the wrong-password negative control passes for a legacy md5 account too, and `pg_stat_ssl` is relabeled session diagnostics rather than an enforcement test. Added a reload step after editing `pg_hba.conf` (the numbered steps otherwise left the old policy active on Unix), and corrected the note that pre-existing passwords are necessarily md5 (each keeps whatever format it had when set; verified at the password-authentication docs). Added the libpq connection-parameters and password-authentication sources.
- Hardened `machine-auth.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#155; codex gpt-6-astra, which had full source access, found the issues claude-opus-4-8 could not verify because its sandbox blocked the GitHub/GCP/RFC hosts). The GCP Workload Identity Federation recipe restricted only the org and repository, not the branch or environment that section 4 tells readers to pin, so it now conditions on `assertion.ref` too (verified against Google's deployment-pipeline federation example). Access-token validation no longer points readers at the ID-token recipe in oidc-integration.md: it now gives the RFC 9068 access-token rules (signature, exact issuer, an `aud` naming your API, expiry, and the `at+jwt` type so an ID token cannot be substituted). Actions are pinned to a full-length commit SHA rather than a mutable release tag; the client-credentials refresh-token note is softened to RFC 6749's "SHOULD NOT"; the Verify probe reads the bearer key from a mode-0600 header file instead of the command line (its own section 1 rule, verified via curl `-H @file`), adds a positive control, closes a trailing-wildcard hole in the federation-subject check (`...:example-repo:*` had passed while admitting every branch), switches the image scan to `docker history --no-trunc` with a real placeholder and a layers-scan caveat, and adds an mTLS accept/reject check. Added RFC 9068 to Sources. The guide's strong core (federation over static keys, constant-time compare, at-rest hashing, no keys in URLs, mTLS revoke-and-test) was confirmed by both families.
- Closed Verify-completeness gaps in `cloud-firewalls.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#154; codex gpt-6-astra + claude-opus-4-8). Two of the three cloud enumerations could report "clear" while a port was world-open: `gcloud compute firewall-rules list` returns only classic VPC rules and misses hierarchical and network firewall policies, which GCP evaluates first (both families flagged this; verified at the GCP firewall eval-order docs), and `az network nsg rule list` misses Azure Virtual Network Manager security admin rules, whose "Always allow" bypasses the NSG entirely (verified at the Azure NSG overview). The guide now enumerates the GCP policy layers and reviews Azure security admin rules. Corrected the SSH-broker guidance: only AWS SSM Session Manager is agentless (no inbound rule); GCP IAP needs an ingress allow for TCP 22 from `35.235.240.0/20` and Azure Bastion needs the VM to allow the `AzureBastionSubnet` (verified at the IAP TCP-forwarding docs), so "remove the inbound rule entirely" was wrong for those, and the Verify's "named address, never an open range" now admits the documented broker ranges while still forbidding `0.0.0.0/0`. Clarified that the `nc` port list is a non-exhaustive spot-check whose refusal does not prove a cloud-firewall deny (an allowed port with no listener refuses too), that RDP and IPv6 need covering, and that the full-range external scan from a disallowed source is the authoritative test. Both families confirmed the guide's careful choices elsewhere (no stateful/NACL-default traps, AWS new-SG "no inbound rules", SG-focus sufficiency, the full-IPv4 anti-evasion note).
- Hardened `docker.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#153; codex gpt-6-astra found the HIGH, claude-opus-4-8 concurred on the `ss` gap; claude's WebFetch was blocked so its externals were NOT-LIVE-VERIFIED). The firewall section had a real IPv6 gap: publishing a port without a host IP on an IPv4-only bridge also exposes it on the host's IPv6 addresses through Docker's userland proxy (IPv6 mapped to the container's IPv4, verified at the Docker port-publishing docs), and that path terminates on a host process so it traverses `INPUT`, not the `FORWARD`-chain `DOCKER-USER` rule the guide told readers to add; the guide now closes it (publish to a specific address, disable the userland proxy, or restrict the host IPv6 `INPUT`) and probes IPv4/IPv6 separately. The Verify section is tightened: `docker compose ps` is labeled project-scoped (it misses containers outside the project and specific-address/IPv6 bindings), `ss -tlnp` becomes `ss -tulnp` with a note that a DNAT-published port with the userland proxy disabled shows no listening socket at all (both families flagged this), and the authentication probe now prints `http_code` instead of an empty body that made an exposed `200` and a protected `401` indistinguishable. Added a cross-link to `container-hardening.md` for capability-drop, `no-new-privileges`, read-only-rootfs, and socket-mount hardening. Both families agreed the guide makes no unsafe statement (no socket mount, no `--privileged`, no container-as-boundary claim); the Docker 28.0 and `DOCKER-USER`-iptables-only claims were left as-is (accepted by both families, DOCKER-USER placement verified).
- Hardened `oidc-integration.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#152; codex gpt-6-astra + claude-opus-4-8, reconciled). ID-token validation now rejects a token carrying an untrusted additional audience and checks `azp` when `aud` is multi-valued (OIDC Core 3.1.3.7 requires rejecting "additional audiences not trusted by the Client"; it had only required `aud` to contain the client ID). The authorization-code recipe now sends the `code_verifier` at the token exchange, the Python Authlib example enables PKCE (`code_challenge_method: 'S256'`) the guide had promised, and the Node openid-client example adds the missing `nonce`/`expectedNonce` and stores state/verifier/nonce in the server-side session. Microsoft Entra issuer validation is corrected: `common`/`organizations` return a templated issuer `https://login.microsoftonline.com/{tenantid}/v2.0`, so the guide now has you substitute the token `tid`, match `iss` exactly, restrict the signing key by its issuer scope, and enforce a tenant allowlist rather than expecting the issuer to equal the fetched prefix. The Verify section stops replaying an ID token "at the callback" (which only ever receives a code) and instead exercises the section-2 validator with expired/wrong-`aud`/bad-signature fixtures, and the `/admin` probe inspects the redirect target. Logout guidance is aligned to the authentication.md baseline (a copied pre-logout cookie must stop working). Added the log/`Referrer-Policy` leak-path caveat and a refresh-token note, and broadened the GitHub legacy-wildcard warning to subdomains as well as subpaths. Source-verification confirmed GitHub OAuth DOES support PKCE with `S256` (a stale-knowledge flag from one family was refuted at the GitHub docs) and that RFC 9700 says clients SHOULD NOT (not MUST NOT) use the implicit grant (the guide's existing wording was already correct).
- Tightened Verify discrimination and an identity-header caveat in `tailscale.md` (#151; codex gpt-6-astra audit, claude family memory-blocked). The direct-exposure probe used an unguarded literal LAN/VPC address with a `time_connect=0` pass signal (the #100/#106 false-pass shape: an unsubstituted address fails DNS to the same zero, indistinguishable from a filtered port) and honored proxy variables; it now uses the corpus's guarded `set -- PASTE_WHOLE_BLOCK` idiom with `--noproxy '*'` and `exitcode`/`errormsg`, run from another machine. The tailnet Verify checked only that an allowed device connects, which holds under both the default allow-all policy and a scoped one, so it could not detect a failure to remove allow-all; it now says so and directs asserting the restriction in the policy file's `tests` (accept the intended identity, deny an excluded one; Tailscale rejects a policy whose tests fail). The identity-header note no longer treats a loopback bind as proof of Serve provenance: loopback stops other machines but any local process can reach `127.0.0.1:3000` and forge the headers, so trusting them requires trusting every process on the host (Tailscale documents that localhost binding limits, not eliminates, tampering by other local services). Added the Serve identity-headers and policy-tests sources.
- Hardened `self-signed.md` against a source-verified audit (#150; codex gpt-6-astra, which generated the certs and tested with OpenSSL 3.5.5; the claude family stayed memory-blocked). Replaced the deprecated `-nodes` with `-noenc` in the RSA command and prose (deprecated since OpenSSL 3.0, which this guide targets; `-nodes` still parses). The Verify now inspects `basicConstraints` alongside `subjectAltName` and tells the reader to read the output, not the exit status (the inspection exits 0 even for a certificate with no SAN, and `-verify_hostname` falls back to the Common Name when no SAN is present, so a CN-only or `CA:TRUE` certificate would otherwise pass) and to check IP identities with `-verify_ip`. It also distinguishes the `s_client -CAfile` diagnostic (explicit trust) from confirming the section-4 trust-store install, which must be tested from each client's normal configuration (a fresh certificate should fail before `update-ca-certificates` and succeed after). Flagged the example `203.0.113.10` as an RFC 5737 documentation address to replace. All verified at the OpenSSL `req`/verification-options/`x509` man pages and RFC 5737/9525.
- Hardened `free-certificates.md`, the corpus's most-referenced guide, against a source-verified audit (#149; codex gpt-6-astra, with the claude family's audit OOM-killed by shared-host memory). Renewal reliability: a bare `certbot renew --deploy-hook ...` is a no-op right after issuance and never persists the hook (Certbot saves options only when a certificate is actually renewed), so the guide now installs the reload hook durably (`--deploy-hook` on the initial `certonly`/`run`, a `renewal-hooks/deploy/` script, or `certbot reconfigure` on 2.3.0+) and the Verify checks the renewal timer is active and exercises the hook with `--dry-run --run-deploy-hooks` (plain `--dry-run` does not run deploy hooks), plus independent expiry/renewal-failure monitoring rather than CA reminder emails. Standalone issuance now warns that a host whose web server will own port 80 must move renewal to `--webroot`/the server plugin or pre/post hooks. DNS-plugin credentials are scoped to least privilege (a Cloudflare API token with `Zone:DNS:Edit` on only the needed zones, not the Global API Key) and `chmod 600` in a `700` directory. The DNS-01 correction: it needs only control of the challenge `TXT` record and can certify a host with no public address record or inbound port, so the prerequisites and intro no longer present a public `A` record and inbound connectivity as universal (which had conflicted with traefik.md). The network Verify now probes the reader's own hostname through a guarded placeholder instead of `example.com`, which serves a live page and passed spuriously. All claims verified at the Certbot, certbot-dns-cloudflare, and Let's Encrypt docs; the 90-day/6-day lifetimes, the 2027/2028 reductions, the 47-day 2029 CA/Browser maximum, and the OCSP retirement were re-confirmed current and left unchanged.
- Corrected drifted tiers and a fail-open default in `identity-providers.md` (#148), from a cross-family audit (codex gpt-6-astra; the claude family's audit and QA workers were OOM-killed by shared-host memory pressure) with every changed figure source-verified at the vendor. The Duo Authentication Proxy bullet now names its fail-open default (`failmode=safe` admits a primary-authenticated user when Duo is unreachable; set `failmode=secure` for mandatory MFA). Firebase's free allowance is split correctly: the 50,000 MAU (50 SAML/OIDC MAU) figure is the pay-as-you-go Blaze free tier, while the no-billing Spark plan is capped at 3,000 daily active users (2 for SAML/OIDC). Auth0's MFA boundary is sharpened (factors start at the paid Essentials plan; Adaptive MFA needs an Enterprise plan plus its add-on) and the passkey note corrected (a passkey with verified user verification is itself multifactor; what Free lacks is Auth0's licensed MFA policy). Cognito's grandfathered Lite 50,000 MAU free tier (pools created on or before 22 November 2024) is noted. The Verify section gains positive controls (the admin function must succeed after MFA; an allowed account must reach the app) and its logging check is fixed to require the sign-in log plus the app's authorization decision rather than mere log presence, naming Entra Sign-in logs (not Audit logs). Entra External ID (50,000 MAU) and Clerk (50,000 MRU, Pro $25) were spot-verified current and left unchanged. Added four vendor sources.
- Hardened `secrets.md` against a cross-family-reconciled, source-verified audit (#147). The Verify grep printed matching lines (`grep -rn`), so a detected secret was echoed into the terminal or CI log, contradicting the guide's own no-secrets-in-logs rule; it now uses `grep -rlIE` (filenames only, never the secret, and `-I` skips binaries) with `--exclude-dir` instead of a `| grep -v node_modules` filter that suppressed any line merely containing `node_modules` (a false negative that could certify an exposed tree clean), and the pass condition is rewritten to be exit-aware and honest that a prefix grep is a supplementary search, not proof, with gitleaks as the primary gate. The `.gitignore` guidance gained `.env.*`: ignoring `.env` alone leaves `.env.local` and `.env.production` (the files Next.js and similar toolchains load, verified at nextjs.org) committable, and the grep's old `--include="*.env"` could not match them either. Added a `0600` / `umask 077` note for the `~/.pgpass` and `curl --netrc` credential files (PostgreSQL ignores a `~/.pgpass` that group or others can read, and curl warns the `.netrc` must not be readable by others; verified at postgresql.org and everything.curl.dev), a `set -x` caveat (shell tracing echoes an expanded `printf`-into-`--password-stdin` even though the argument list is protected), and a gitleaks version note (the `git`/`dir` subcommands need 8.19 or newer; older builds use `gitleaks detect`; verified at the gitleaks README). Both families were concordant on the `.env.*` gap; source-verification confirmed the PostgreSQL, curl, Next.js, and gitleaks-version claims and added the three new vendor sources.

## 2026-09-16
- Hardened `mfa.md` against a cross-family-reconciled, source-verified audit (#146). Both families (codex + claude-opus-4-8) flagged the same two core gaps: the Verify success signal was tied to enrolment rather than enforcement (rule 6 already taught the distinction, so the check contradicted the guide), and push was recommended with no MFA-fatigue caveat. The password-only Verify now fails only once the factor is enrolled AND enforcement is on (provider policy, Supabase `aal2`, or the app's own check), and states that a still-succeeding password-only session means enrolled but not enforced. Rule 3 ranks push below WebAuthn/passkeys and TOTP, notes neither TOTP nor push is phishing-resistant, and requires number matching or verified push (never a bare tap) against prompt-bombing. The SSH section makes the PAM factor mandatory (`AuthenticationMethods publickey,keyboard-interactive` so a key login still runs the challenge, drop `nullok` after rollout, Duo `failmode=secure` since its default `safe` fails open) rather than implying the module alone enforces MFA. Rule 4 requires re-verifying the existing factor before a user disables or replaces it or mints new recovery codes, so a stolen or password-only session cannot strip MFA. The hosted-identity-provider bullet now says enforcement lives in a layer that varies by provider (a tenant or Conditional-Access policy, a per-user requirement, or authorization in your own app and database; Supabase still admits an `aal1` session unless policies require `aal2`) rather than "only when a tenant policy requires the factor". Cloudflare Access now offers its own independent MFA (a TOTP authenticator or a WebAuthn key) as an alternative to IdP-enforced MFA. The passkey guidance requires verifying the user-verification (UV) flag server-side where a passkey stands alone, treats the sign count as advisory (synced passkeys often report 0) rather than a hard clone-block, and warns that printed recovery codes are the weakest, phishable link for an admin on a phishing-resistant key. The database-MFA section is scoped to unattended machine connections and notes some databases offer native interactive MFA for a human login (MySQL 8 supports up to three factors, including WebAuthn, verified at the vendor documentation). The section heading is corrected to "Identity layers and auth proxies (open source)" since oauth2-proxy delegates authentication rather than enrolling TOTP. Source-verification confirmed MySQL native MFA (codex) and refuted a staleness flag on `otplib` (claude): otplib remains actively maintained, so it stays.
- Closed baseline completeness gaps in `authentication.md` (#145), from a cross-family audit (codex + claude-opus-4-8) that both confirmed the normative core correct (RFC 9700, OIDC hygiene, the hashing set, federated-login-is-not-authorization, MFA-at-grant). Added the controls both families flagged as missing from THE auth baseline: session-ID regeneration on login and privilege change (session fixation), with idle/absolute expiry and server-side logout invalidation; a CSRF defense for cookie sessions (SameSite alone is not sufficient); a password policy plus known-breach screening (new rule 17); a secure account-recovery flow (new rule 18); account-enumeration resistance and a note that per-IP limits do not stop distributed credential stuffing; HTTPS across the whole authenticated session rather than only the credential POST (or an equivalent authenticated encrypted transport such as SSH); and PKCE `S256` (never `plain`) with fresh transaction-bound `state`/`nonce`. Corrected the htpasswd example to `-B -C 12` (the bare `-B` default cost is 5, below OWASP's minimum of 10, verified at the Apache docs), reordered the hash preference to OWASP's argon2id > scrypt > bcrypt > PBKDF2, and noted bcrypt's 72-byte truncation. Broadened the Quick checks to probe every non-public path and transport and to require a real secret scanner.
- Added a gate that keeps `ss` listener probes unfiltered (#144): `tools/check_unfiltered_ss.py` flags any guide Verify block that pipes `ss` to `grep` (the filtered-listener form fixed guide-by-guide in #113 and swept corpus-wide in #138 / row 1.74). A filter cannot reveal a listener on an unexpected port, and a port grep also matches an IPv6 address, so the corpus reads the whole table; this gate keeps the anti-pattern from regressing (zero instances at landing). It is scoped to top-level guide bash and console fences with comments stripped, so a prose warning that names the pattern does not trip it; it carries a 14-case self-test and is wired into `run_all_checks.sh` with the standard self-test-plus-gate-result discipline.
- Hardened `caddy.md` against a cross-family-reconciled, source-verified audit (#143). Adds an HSTS header (Caddy sends none on its own, so "the whole TLS setup" had left the first request downgradable) and states that Caddy ignores untrusted `X-Forwarded-*` by default (use `trusted_proxies` behind a CDN). Flags that fronting with Cloudflare Access needs origin lockdown, since Caddy still answers on 443 and a direct-IP client bypasses Access unless ingress is closed and the Access JWT validated (cross-links cloudflare.md). Verify now proves the `@admin` matcher scopes auth (a non-`/admin` path must be 200, not merely `/admin` returning 401).
